# 🗓️ CHANGELOG
[Changelog](/CHANGELOG.md) for the past releases

---

## Release: v0.11.0
### Release Date: 2026-11-02
#### ⚠️ Breaking Changes:

#### 🌟 New Features:
- **Workspace — Layer 3 - DT Logs:** the parsed drivetest logs of each campaign (the CSV files of the parser, or their ZIP) are a new dataset type, Layer 3 - DT Logs, which replaces the reserved Smart Orchestrator Logs type. A delivery is split into one dataset per campaign and NR Mode, whose rows are written to a Parquet source without going through the workspace database (the UK Q3 2026 delivery, 1.86 million rows and 370 columns, in about a minute), and every session (JoinID and UE) is summarised in the Layer 3 DT Log Sessions table; a card of its own in Datasets lists them with their campaign, NR Mode, operators, sessions and the NQ calls that have one, and Preview reads their rows from the Parquet source. In Non-Qualified Calls, the optional **Layer 3 - DT Logs** column (sorted and filtered like the others) and the header of a call show the UEs of the call with a session in them, found by its JOIN_ID, and the map of the call draws every layer of the KMZ files of the NQ team from them (Direction, RAN Configuration, Events, L3 Signaling and the LTE and NR PCell, SCell and neighbour cells with their channel, PCI, RSRP, RSRQ, SINR and UE Tx Power, in the markers and colours of their legend; the same points as the 2,382 KMZ files of UK Q3 2026) in a card **From Logs** by section, channel and PCI, with the real way of the UE as a road in place of its sketch and a signalling timeline of the session (the RSRP of its PCell, its events and RRC, NAS and SIP messages, the failures and SIP errors ringed) below the map or over the larger one, whose cursor, dragged along it, moves each UE on the map with its signalling of that moment. The DT Analyzer RCA reads what the log of each call shows at its failure (the radio of its last seconds, its SCG failures, re-establishments, handovers, SIP errors, rejects, BLER, UE Tx Power and a stronger neighbour of the serving channel) with 13 new default rules before those of the radio of the CDR and the rules of the provider's reports reading the log too, its evidence quoting the log; the workspaces with rules of their own take them, and a log imported or deleted calculates again the calls of its campaign. The Diagnosis hints of the map add what the log shows, each pointing at its moment on the map, and a call without a position in its CDR is placed by its log. A new section, **NQ Maps**, draws every filtered NQ call and their logs on one map with the information and controls of the map of a call (without its groups): from a grid of the average of a metric over the whole country down to the events (from zoom level 10), the sites (from 11) and the points of the layers and RSRP / SINR samples of a street (from 13), with the route of each session and of each CDR, the NQ calls coloured by call type, root cause domain, campaign, operator or site, the signalling timeline of the call chosen and, with a double-click, the map over the whole width of the window; it opens where it was left, and the map of each call can add the logs of the NQ calls around it (ticket #64).

#### 🚀 Enhancements:
- **Non-Qualified Calls — Maps of the calls:** what a map shows is switched in two cards over it by where it comes from, **From CDR** (the RSRP / SINR samples, a switch for each measure in the shapes of the KMZ layers and several at once, then NQ calls, Sites, Start and end cells (the lines from the call to the cells of its start and end), Cell path, Time-window paths and the route) and **From Logs**, folded when a map opens and by a click outside them, each with a switch for all of it and its layers in sections unfolded at first, ending above the panels over the bottom of the map when unfolded, and the legend below has a box for each; **Map**, beside **Operators** among the controls above the map (on one line), chooses the map under them (OpenStreetMap, OpenStreetMap in grey by default, Plain, Terrain, Topographic, Satellite or Dark), Alt/Opt + drag measures with the elevation profile of the ground between the two points, as Google Earth shows it, and the NQ calls, stars (larger on the map of a call), are coloured by the same choices on both maps (by site on the map of a call, those of its own site in burgundy, by call type on NQ Maps), with the switch **And Group** adding an outline in their shape for each group of the call they are of (cluster innermost, then time window and hotspot) and each group (hotspot zone, clusters, its own solid and the others dashed, and time windows, the way of each through its failures in a colour of its own) switched in From CDR and **Campaigns**, at the top of From CDR on the map of a call, choosing the campaigns of everything it draws from the CDRs (its NQ calls, samples, Radio around the call, Diagnosis hints and drive): *Selected campaigns* (those of the filters) or *Call campaign*. Neither map draws the Ping tests, both show the Focus operators by default, the map of a call shows only the NQ calls, samples and groups of its NR Mode, and on it the call itself has its star under its pin, taller than the star with all the outlines of its groups. The controls above a map show above the larger map too, the signalling timeline and the elevation profile fold with a click anywhere on their header and close with their **×** (the timeline comes back with its switch in From Logs), the panel of the element chosen floats on the right of the larger map and moves by dragging its title, and the direction of travel of the logs is drawn inside the road of their way. The RSRP and SINR of the samples of the CDRs and of the Layer 3 - DT Logs share one legend: the levels of the samples (Excellent to Very poor) with *Extremely poor* below −120 dBm.
- **Non-Qualified Calls — NQ Hotspots and NQ Calls:** the panel Hotspots, Time-Window & Clustered Issues is named **NQ Hotspots, Time-Window & Clustered Issues** (**NQ Hotspots** in the sections bar), in the page, its exports and the Help, as **NQ Maps** beside it, and the panel of the calls, Non-Qualified Calls, is named **NQ Calls** in the page, its sections bar and menu, and the Help.
- **Non-Qualified Calls — On phones:** the introduction at the top shows its first two lines with **More** for the rest, and over the maps of the calls and NQ Maps **Map** (with short names), the zoom buttons and the cards **From CDR** and **From Logs** fit in two rows; a double tap opens a map larger, filling the screen with a shorter note and signalling timeline, and zooms in there.
- **NQ Prioritization — Clusters within one circle:** a cluster holds the NQ calls of an operator and NR Mode that fit in a circle whose radius is the Cluster distance (half the ISD by default), the nearest joined first, so it no longer chains along a road: a long series of failures makes several clusters that meet at the widest gaps, and calls at the same site join only within the Cluster distance. A Repeated Issue (the Cluster condition of the strategies) is a call of a cluster, and a hotspot is what its clusters and time windows make together (OR: their envelope; AND: where they meet). Every workspace calculates its groups again once.

#### 🐛 Bug fixes:
- **Import / Export / Transfer — PPT Designs:** a PPT Design whose PPT Template was added apart on each server (with an identity of its own on each) no longer shows as updated (`Template Uid: … → …`) in the import preview and the completion message of every transfer back and forth: exports, imports and the preview refer to the template by the identity it has in each workspace, as the PPT Designer does.
- **Non-Qualified Calls — Cells of the call on its map:** a cell of the trace of a call farther than the distance of the Serving Networks (30 km by default) is placed at its site when its PCI and channel are those the CDR gives it (a cell on a high mast serving a call on high ground), with how far it is in its label and judged by the Diagnosis, instead of being taken for a cell the Network Inventory does not hold; the cells left unplaced say they are not in the Network Inventory within that distance.

## Release: v0.10.1
### Release Date: 2026-10-30
#### ⚠️ Breaking Changes:
- **PPT Reporting (old) — Removed:** the module leaves the tool with its page, report jobs and Chart Sets: PPT Designer, Chart Builder and Reporting make its charts and reports. Its former address opens PPT Designer; a workspace drops the records of its jobs when it opens (their files stay in `output/reports/reports-old`), and packages and backups of earlier versions still import.

#### 🌟 New Features:
- **PPT Designer and Chart Builder — The chart types of Tableau's Show Me:** Chart Type adds Horizontal Bars, Dot Plot, Bullet Graph, Highlight Table, Bar Table, Heat Map, Pie, Donut, Lines, Dual Lines, Areas, 100% Stacked Areas, Bars and Line, Box Plot, Symbol Map, Filled Map, Treemap, Packed Bubbles, Sankey and Gantt, grouped as Distribution, Comparison, Trends, Composition, Hierarchies and flows, Tables, Maps and Scorecards, and drawn the same in the viewer, Chart Builder and the PPT, with their tooltips, legend and Format. Chart Type shows them on a lilac panel, each group a card with its name in purple, as many on a row as fit and every type in sight without scrolling, the panel as wide as its rows; the wheel over it scrolls it instead of going through the slides. Their names carry no aggregation (the aggregation of the measure chooses it), the order of the measures gives their roles (a target, a second axis, a size or a colour), and their settings (Whiskers, P10–P90 by default; Target, such as the previous campaign or the best competitor; Direction; Sort; Date Bin) are kept among the filters of the row, as Threshold and Buckets. A time dropped on Rows or Columns is grouped by day, `DAY(Test_Start_Time)` (its pill chooses the hour, week, month or exact time), and Lines and Areas draw it as a time axis; Filled Map colours the Map Areas of the CDRs; a Pie or Donut of a dimension draws a grid of pies, a row per value of Rows and a column per value of Columns, named by their headers; the generated comments read the bars, dots, bullets, heat maps, tables, pies, lines, areas and box plots. A numeric field on the axis of Lines, Areas and Bars and Line keeps its numeric order (8 before 10), and in a Design of NSA & SA they draw the NR Mode as their series instead of a level of their axis. Every workspace receives once the example template and Design **DT Analyzer - All Supported Chart Types** (NSA & SA, the latest 2 campaigns of each NR Mode), which draw every supported chart type on the CDRs of the workspace, at most two columns a slide; the earlier All Chart Types and DT Analyzer - All Chart Types examples are replaced in place where they are as they were shipped.
- **Ticket Center — Statistics:** a **Statistics** tab shows how the tickets of a period are going (**All time** by default, This week, This month, This quarter or This year, chosen at its top and shared with Time Spent: the tickets opened in it, and those resolved or closed in it for the times to resolve and the versions that fixed them, against the period before): the resolution rate (a ring), the median time to resolve, the tickets opened and the comments and attachments per ticket; donuts of every ticket by type, status and priority; the tickets resolved by the time they took (or the open ones by their age) with their median, average and 90th percentile and the median of each type and priority; the tickets created, resolved and open by the end of each day, week or month of the period; the tickets fixed in and opened with each of the latest 10 versions of the tool (also those without tickets); every ticket of each module, reporter and assignee; and the activity of each day of the period (a calendar with the number and total of each day for a week or a month, a small calendar of each month for a quarter, a year or every ticket; every ticket since the first one, by weeks or, over 8 months, by months). A **Time Spent** tab adds up the time from opened to resolved or closed of the tickets resolved or closed in the period: in all, on average, its median and the longest; where it goes (the time New, In Progress and Waiting for Info, by the changes of status of their timelines); per day, week or month of the period (the weekends of every chart per day shaded, on a slate axis); and by module, type, priority, assignee, reporter and the version that fixed them, with a note on how it is measured (calendar time, 24 hours a day). Times are written in days of 24 hours, hours and minutes (`13d 4h`, `7h 12m`) everywhere in Ticket Center. Every part tells what it is, and the donuts, modules and people list their tickets.
- **Workspace Config — Auto-calculated Fields:** the fields have their own panel in Workspace Config, after Mappings & Reference Data (formerly a list in Workspace, whose links lead there), with a card per field: the CDR types it applies to with the state of their tables (up to date, updating with its percentage, or waiting for an update), the values it gives in the CDRs as a bar with the share of each one, the fields it is worked out from, what the rows no rule takes get, and the PPT Templates, PPT Designs, Query Builder queries and fields that use it; above them, the state of the CDR tables, a search, the Data, Voice and Speech buttons, **New Field** and a ⋯ menu with **Import JSON…**, **Export All Fields** and **Re-materialize All Fields**. A card, PPT Designer and the PPT Templates editor open one editor, which lists the fields at the left in the order they are worked out and edits the chosen one at the right: its name, the CDR types it applies to, and its value built from rules (conditions of a field, an operator and values, all of them or any of them, the fields of the CDRs and the fields above offered in the app's lists, the values of the CDRs offered with their rows as they are typed) or written as a Tableau-style formula (`[` offers the fields, buttons insert IF, ELSEIF, ELSE, AND, OR, fields and operators, and rules and formula turn into each other), with a reference of the operators, which add STARTS WITH, ENDS WITH, MATCHES (regular expressions), IS EMPTY and IS NOT EMPTY, and a list of values to CONTAINS; what the rows no rule takes get (nothing, a fixed value, or the value of another field and then a fixed value) is a choice of its own. The field is checked while it is edited and tried on the CDRs before saving (the rows of each value, of each rule and of no rule), and **Save** saves it alone and updates only its column, and those of the fields reading it, in the CDR tables in the background, with nothing else to save and no question on closing once saved.
- **PPT Designer and Chart Builder — Rows and Columns in your order:** as in Tableau, a value of the Rows or Columns of a chart (an axis label, a header, a row label or a row of a table) dragged on the chart before or after another value of its field changes their order wherever the field is (every level and pane), in the viewer and the PPT; a bar shows where it goes, the order is kept in the Format of the chart (values that appear later go after those ordered) and **Default order**, in the card of the values of the field, gives it its own order back.
- **PPT Designer — Cut, copy and paste charts between slides:** **Cut Chart** joins **Copy Chart** in the ⋯ menu of a chart (a KPI card, a text box or an image alike), **Paste Chart** in the ⋯ menu of a slide or of a chart of it puts it on that slide (on a place more when it has no free one), and Ctrl/Cmd+C, Ctrl/Cmd+X and Ctrl/Cmd+V do the same on the element chosen and the slide shown; the pasted chart keeps its fields, filters and format as an element of its own.

#### 🚀 Enhancements:
- **Ticket Center — Header, views and All chips:** the module has a header like the other modules (in its red, with a drawing of tickets, the **New Ticket** button, the tiles and the tabs of its views), followed by the floating bar of its sections and the view chosen: **New Ticket** (its form shown without signing in too, dimmed, with a note to sign in, and filled only once signed in), **All Tickets** (the list of tickets, formerly Tickets; its former links lead there), **Dashboard** (a panel of its own with its title), **Statistics** or **Time Spent**, in the same order as the tabs in the sections menu of the tab and the floating bar. The Dashboard follows the same **Period** as Statistics and Time Spent (All time by default, counting them the same way) and shows Created vs resolved per day, week or month of the period (the count over each bar; with more than 24 periods, as the days of This month, a single bar per period, the resolved tickets drawn narrow inside the created ones, both counts over it and the number of each day under it) beside **At a glance** (a ring of the open, resolved and rejected tickets, All clear when none is open, with the tickets opened and resolved in the period, the oldest open one and the unassigned ones), Tickets by type and status beside **Tickets per module** (every ticket, its open ones highlighted), a card per type with its median time to resolve (its open tickets by priority only while it has any), and, last, **Needs attention** (the most urgent and oldest open tickets with their assignee and age, or All caught up, and the tickets resolved last with their version and the time they took) beside **Latest activity** (the newest openings, comments and changes of status). The browser remembers the tab (Dashboard by default, New Ticket while there is no ticket), and the counts of the dashboard, the sections menu, the floating bar, the New Ticket button and the links to the tickets of a status open their tab. Each group of chips of the list starts with **All**, pressed while none of its chips is, which lists every value of the group (**All** of Status, every ticket).
- **Chart Builder — Charts at once:** a chart reads only its own columns from the columnar copies of the chosen CDRs, which stay in memory (a field dropped on a shelf reads only its column, and the common fields are read ahead once the CDRs are chosen), the rows in scope and the Operator Maps are worked out once per CDR, a chart drawn before is shown again at once, and the chart shown stays, dimmed under Updating, while the next one is worked out: on 1.2 million rows of the UK workspace the first chart takes about 1 to 3 seconds and each change 0.1 to 2 seconds instead of minutes, with about 2 GB of memory instead of 15. The filters of every chart, also in PPT Designer, compare each distinct value once (the same rows, three to five times faster).
- **PPT Designer, Chart Builder and exports — Charts in any layout:** every chart and table is laid out in the shape of its place, whatever the layout, in the PPT, the Word and the viewer alike: nothing is stretched (pies, donuts and bubbles stay round), the maps fill their place showing more around their places, the KPI cards take the grid that makes them largest (four cards: two rows of two in a 16:9 place, one row in a wide one, one column in a tall one), each as tall as its figures and its trend, and Multi KPI CDF Lines places its CDFs side by side or one above the other over the whole place. The renderer of the PPT starts again whenever its scripts change, so the PPT is always drawn as the viewer draws it.
- **PPT Designer and Chart Builder — (blank) in filters:** `=`, `!=`, `IN` and `NOT IN` read a missing value as the charts show it, so `Region != (blank)` leaves out the tests of no Region.
- **PPT Designer and Chart Builder — Where a field goes:** while a field or a pill is dragged to Rows, Columns or Legend, a bar between the pills shows where it will go (or the pill it replaces is outlined), and a pill moved along its shelf is shown dimmed.
- **Workspace, PPT Designer and Chart Builder — NR Mode field:** `NR_Mode`, the NR Mode of the CDR of each row (NSA or SA), is a pinned Derived field right after Campaign in every table of rows (the preview of a CDR and of the combined CDRs, the rows of a chart), filtered and exported like the others, and a field of PPT Designer (its Fields and the filters of its charts) and Chart Builder beside Campaign to split, colour or filter their charts.
- **Workspace — Default Auto-calculated Fields:** `RAT_Group` keeps each technology of the RAT on its own (EN-DC, NR SA, LTE, VoNR, EPSFB, VoLTE, CSFB, 2G/3G, No Service) and groups its combinations (ENDC Mixed, NR SA Mixed, VoNR Mixed, VoLTE Mixed, LTE Mixed) in Data, Voice and Speech CDRs alike, `Test_Result_Group` counts dropped calls as failures, and the new `NR_Mode_from_RAT` gives each sample NSA, SA or Others from its RAT (the NR Mode of its CDR stays in NR Mode).
- **PPT Designer — PPT Templates and PPT Designs in your order:** the tab on the left of each name in PPT Templates and in the PPT Designs library drags the template or Design to another place of the list (or moves it with the up and down arrow keys), saving the order of the workspace at once for every user; the Template of a new PPT Design follows the order of the templates, new templates and Designs go to the end, App Events record each new order, and exports, transfers and backups carry it (the templates and Designs of a package keep their order, ahead of those only the destination has).
- **Workspace Management — Starter PPT Templates and PPT Designs:** a new workspace starts with the PPT Templates and PPT Designs of the UK workspace as they are now: *NetCheck CDR Report*, *NetCheck CDR Report (Main Cities)* and *RF Quality (RSRP & SINR)*, with a Design per NR Mode, and the examples of the application named *DT Analyzer - …* (*Tableau Dashboards*, *Tableau Charts*, *Tableau Charts - Only Maps*, *FDFS DL (NSA & SA)* and *All Supported Chart Types*), each with one NSA & SA Design of its name but Only Maps, which the favourite Bundle Report Templates and Reporting Jobs name. An existing workspace keeps the examples it has; its earlier *FDFS DL (NSA & SA)* is replaced in place by *DT Analyzer - FDFS DL (NSA & SA)* where it is as it was shipped.
- **PPT Designer — Designer actions:** while designing, each chart has **Refresh chart** beside View dataset, which works that chart out again and draws it again, and **Refresh Design** (which lays the slides being edited out again), **Auto-Calculated Fields** and **Edit PPT Template** stay available; only **Generate PPT** and the presentation wait for the Designer to close, and Generate PPT is the last action of the row.
- **PPT Designer and Workspace — Filter values from the columnar copies:** the facets, the values of the filters and the vendor families of a Design read each distinct value of their columns once per CDR from the columnar copies instead of every row (0.3 to 0.5 seconds instead of 8 to 10 on the Data CDRs of the UK workspace), and the filtered Preview of a combined table reads its filtered columns once for its count and its page.
- **Ticket Center — Files in tickets:** a ticket, its Description and its comments take any file besides the screenshots (a log, a CDR sample, an Excel, PowerPoint, Word or PDF file, a ZIP…), pasted, dropped or chosen in the same zone, up to 10 at once of 100 MB each. Screenshots keep their thumbnails and full-size view; the other files are listed with their type, name and size and download when clicked, never opened in the page (nor an image whose content is not one). Programs and scripts (`.exe`, `.bat`, `.ps1`, `.sh`, `.jar`…) are refused; zipped, they can be attached. Downloads, exports, transfers, backups and restores of Ticket Center read the attachments one at a time (a download in chunks), so large files are never all in memory, and packages of earlier versions import as before. Super-admins free space with **Clean up attachments**, beside New Ticket: it lists the attachments larger than the size chosen (10 MB by default) of the tickets of the statuses chosen (Resolved and Closed by default, or Rejected), the largest first with their ticket, deletes those left checked after a confirmation and compacts the application database, telling its size before and after. The All Tickets list and Statistics count the attachments, and App Events record each file added and deleted and each cleanup.

#### 🐛 Bug fixes:
- **PPT Designer and Chart Builder — Table headings:** the rows of a Table are headed by their fields (Region) instead of an internal name (`__catalog_primary`).
- **PPT Designer and Chart Builder — NR Mode:** the NR Mode field, and the NR Mode split of the NSA & SA Designs, is always the NR Mode of the CDR of each test, even beside an Auto-calculated Field of before named like it.
- **Workspace — Auto-calculated Fields materialization:** an update compares the fields with those the CDR tables hold, so a save that changes no CDR (a new order) no longer works every field out again, a field saved without materializing or an update stopped half-way is caught up, a field reading a changed field is worked out again, and a CDR table already holding the fields is left alone. The fields of a CDR whose columnar copy is its only store are worked out on the copy (only the columns they read are read, and only their own columns written), never putting the CDR back in SQLite nor copying it again: a change in a workspace of 30 CDRs takes a minute or two instead of more than half an hour. An update left pending starts on its own within half a minute, without opening Workspace. A rule keeps its AND and OR as written instead of being split into several rules, a value with spaces is saved quoted so it can be read back, and a field can no longer take the name of a field of the CDRs or of the application (`NR_Mode`, the NR Mode of each CDR, is never overwritten in the combined tables). Renaming a field no longer saves again the PPT Templates that do not use it.
- **PPT Designer — PPT Templates updated in place:** setting the default, duplicating, deleting, creating and importing a template update the PPT Templates dialog at once, without reloading PPT Designer (which closed and opened the dialog again and opened the last Design under it), and a renamed template reaches the Template of a new PPT Design and the PPT Designs without reloading the page.
- **Workspace — CDRs changed in their columnar copy:** renaming a CDR whose rows are in its columnar copy no longer fails. Placing its tests in the Map Areas, changing its NR Mode, renaming it and working out its missing Vendor read only the columns they need from the copy and rewrite only their own columns in it (10 to 15 seconds for a Data CDR of 450,000 rows), instead of putting the whole CDR back in SQLite and copying it again (about two minutes per CDR with the workspace locked, an hour for the Map Areas of a whole workspace). A Vendor, Region or Cluster mapping rewrites only the columns it changes in the copy: about a minute for that CDR instead of more than four, two of them with the workspace locked. Database Management shows the rows of a CDR kept in its copy, and those of an emptied combined table, read-only: CDRs stay as they were imported.

#### 📚 Documentation:
- **Help — Ticket Center presentation:** new slides show the module as it looks now, in its colours: the header with its tiles and the tabs of its five views, the New Ticket form (dimmed while signed out), the Dashboard, the periods (how each one draws its charts and its calendar of activity, and which tickets each part counts), Statistics and Time Spent; the Ticket Center slide of the Overview names the Dashboard by period, Statistics and Time Spent.
- **Help — Roadmap:** the Roadmap slide, the roadmap of the one-slider and the Roadmap chapter show what the tool brings today (8 modules live, PPT Designer with its charts built as in Tableau, slide comments written by GenAI, the columnar store with its gain in speed and space, the DuckDB engine, durable analytical caches and Tableau connected to the workspace database), next (the Germany pilot, Umlaut and P3 CDRs, the Active Tests community and Non-Qualified Calls out of Beta), soon (Network Insights improvements and automatic emails) and later (Smart Orchestrator Logs and TEMS logs and health monitoring).

## Release: v0.10.0
### Release Date: 2026-10-29
#### ⚠️ Breaking Changes:
- **Import / Export / Transfer — Workspace databases:** the CDR rows of a workspace travel in its columnar copies (`cdr-store/` beside `database.sqlite` in backups, packages and transfers), with its CDR and combined tables empty: earlier versions cannot restore or import the workspace databases this version exports.

#### 🌟 New Features:
- **Workspace — Columnar store of the CDRs:** every ready Data, Voice and Speech CDR is copied in the background to Parquet files (`.cdr-store` beside the workspace database, read with DuckDB), which become the only store of its rows: once copied, its table is emptied (its columns and indexes kept), and so are the combined tables once the copies hold their CDRs, and every reader of the application, Query Builder and Database Management included, gets the same rows in the same order from the copies; a change of the rows of a CDR puts them back in its table first, and the CDR is copied again (triggers on its table, counted in the CDR Store Versions table). Network Insights, Scoring & GAP Analysis (with its Points Lost Map), Non-Qualified Calls (indexing, NQ Prioritization and the call Map) and PPT Designer read the copies directly. The start of the server empties the copied tables and compacts the workspace database (VACUUM) to give the disk back their room (a workspace database of 51 GB keeps about 5 GB); the copies travel with the workspace database in backups, packages, transfers and duplicates; the copy waits for free disk space and shows among the background tasks and in App Events; and `.cdr-store/catalog.duckdb` gives tools such as Tableau one view per CDR type over the active CDRs (`.cdr-store/postgres-views.json` the same views for PostgreSQL with pg_duckdb).
- **PPT Designer — Designer:** **Open Designer**, in the viewer, edits the slides and charts on screen (the button reads Close Designer, in amber, and closes it, asking about the changes only when something differs from what is saved; the viewer actions that would leave it wait for it to close and the tools over the charts are hidden but View dataset; on wide screens the controls of the viewer share one row, Generate PPT, Refresh Design, Auto-Calculated Fields and Edit PPT Template as icons named by their tooltips) and draws each change at once on the CDRs the Design already prepared: the slides at the left, each drawn in small as it is laid out (new, duplicate, drag to move, hide, optional, delete), layouts from a grid of up to 6 × 6 places whose rows and columns can be resized and places merged or split, or from layouts ready to use that are not symmetric grids (two above and one as wide as both below, a tall one beside two, three above and two below…) on a grid of up to 6 rows and 12 columns, places deleted (their cells left empty) or centred in the empty cells beside them, with a chart that fills the free places beside it from its menu, New Chart (empty, any chart type, reading no CDR until it can be drawn: a `Chart in progress` row) and Duplicate Chart that give a full slide a place more, titles and text boxes with `{NR Mode}`, parameters and the selection of each Design (`{Design}`, `{Scope}`, `{Campaigns}`, `{Operators}`, `{Vendors}`, `{Regions}`, `{Clusters}`, `{Cities}`), KPI cards (figure of the latest campaign compared with the previous one, the best competitor or a target, colour bands and trend; **KPI Card**, under Scorecards in Chart Type, turns a chart into one and another type turns it back, and Chart Builder previews it and adds it to a PPT Design), text boxes and pictures (PPT Images, which travel with the PPT Designs and PPT Templates that use them) written as native text boxes and pictures in the PPT, charts chosen, dragged between the places of the layout and built as in Tableau in the panel at the right, whose head says where it is and what a change reaches, with the actions in a ⋯ menu, and which widens by dragging its edge (Data: the Source and NR Mode of the CDRs the chart reads, fields of the source grouped by the sections of the CDR Field Dictionary, dimensions and measures told apart, dragged to the Measure, Rows, Columns, Legend, Filters and One chart per shelves, One chart per drawing a chart for each value of a field in the place of the chart; filters with the values of the Design's selection and their samples, or a parameter; Chart Type, a tab in the colour of the theme on the right edge of the chart chosen opening, over the slide beside it, a picture of each type grouped by what they show, greying those the shelves cannot draw, which cannot be chosen (any one for a chart still without a measure), and saying what they need, a row for each group, closing once one is chosen, the Chart subpanel and the form of a KPI card saying what the chosen one needs; the measures of a Map or a Scatter keep their meaning when they are moved on the shelf) and formatted on the chart itself (series colours from their legend with a Default button back to the Operator / Vendor colours, line width and dash (dashes and round dots in proportion to the width), opacity, legend place and size, labels, axis titles, axis ranges with a slider and the values of a text axis ticked (Only, Show all, search) on a card opened from the axis itself; copy and paste format), the same in the viewer and the PPT, undo and redo, and a draft kept on the server (PPT Drafts) until it is saved or discarded. A Design follows its template live and keeps only what it changes (This Design scope), or the change goes to its template and every Design that follows it (Template scope), with marks on what differs, a Differences panel (a card per slide or chart, a chip per change that takes the template back), Reset to template, Apply to template, Template changed with Keep mine, Detach, Save as template and Save Design as; template parameters (`{City}`, `{Threshold}`) are filled by each Design in PPT Scope & Filters, and by each PPT Design of a Reporting Job, which can also generate One PPT per value of a parameter (one PPT for each city chosen, named after it), and a Bundle Report Template gives a parameter on a field of its common filters (City, Region, Operator…) the values chosen there. The slides of a template are kept in an optional last `Format` column of its CSV, so imports, exports, transfers and backups carry them; deleting a template detaches its Designs, and slide comments stay with their slide when its title changes. Neither the charts of a slide nor the expanded viewer have a Chart Definition tab any more (the Designer changes them); the image buttons of every chart, also the only one of its slide, stand at the left of its controls, those of the whole slide (with its comments) beside the slide chooser, and those of the expanded viewer above its chart, with its actions as in the viewer. Chart Builder uses the same shelves, Chart Type (the same tab, on the edge of its chart), **One chart per** and **Format** (series, legend, labels and axes, in its panel or on the chart itself), and **Add to PPT Design or Template…** puts its chart, with its format, on a slide of a PPT Design (one of its differences) or of a PPT Template (every Design that follows it) whose slides the user can edit; a field dropped on a pill of a shelf takes its place and one dropped before or after a pill goes there. As with Designs, a user-editor edits only the Designs and changes only the PPT Templates they created.
- **PPT Designer — PPT Templates:** the templates of the workspace are managed from **PPT Templates**, at the top of the PPT Designs library (import, New Template, rename, duplicate, default, export and delete), no longer from Workspace Config: their actions use addresses of PPT Designer (`/ppt-designer/templates/…`, the former ones lead there), need PPT Designer in the Modules Profile, are recorded in App Events under PPT Designer and follow its rights (a user-editor changes and deletes only the templates they created, admins and super-admins any; a user-viewer sees and exports them). **Edit** opens a template in the viewer and its Designer as a PPT of its own, never listed with the Designs, whose changes all go to the template (saved only as a template), and **Edit PPT Template**, the pencil of the viewer, shows it as a table; a new template starts with its cover and opens in its Designer. A PPT Design can also start blank (PPT Template **None — blank PPT** in **Create PPT Design**): with its cover only, it opens in its Designer, follows no template and can be saved as one. The button beside **PPT Template** opens a gallery of the templates, each with its first slides in small as it lays them out (the picture of each chart type in its place), its slides and charts and the default one marked, and the blank PPT; a click chooses one.
- **PPT Designer — Warm-up while the server is idle:** after 5 minutes without anyone using the application, every PPT Design and every PPT Template (as PPT Templates → Edit opens it) is prepared and the models of its charts worked out and kept in the cache folder of the workspace, so after a restart they open at once; a Design or template that changed works out again only its changed charts, and nothing runs when nothing changed. **Warming up PPT Designs** shows in the background tasks card and stops from it; it pauses when someone uses the application or other background work starts, and goes on from where it was the next time the server is idle (App Events `design_warmup_started`, `design_warmup_finished`, `stop_design_warmup`).

#### 🚀 Enhancements:
- **Non-Qualified Calls — Indexing, NQ Prioritization and DT Analyzer RCA:** they take about half the time with the same results: the calls of each CDR are worked out column by column, the nearest site of the calls is looked for many calls at once, and the PCI collisions, hotspots, clusters, time-window issues and repetitions are measured with arrays.
- **PPT Designer — Charts from the columnar copies, restored after a restart:** the charts that aggregate their rows (CDFs, histograms, averages, stacked and grouped bars) are grouped by DuckDB on the columnar copies of the CDRs, each distinct combination of the chart's fields once with its number of rows, and the others read the columns of the CDRs from a cache kept in memory only; a chart reads the row order of each CDR once and compares its Operator, Vendor and Campaign filters once per distinct value, with the same results. The preparation of each Design and the model of each chart already drawn are kept in memory and as compressed files in the `.cache/ppt-designer` folder of the workspace (each written whole under a temporary name and then renamed, a file that cannot be read deleted and worked out again, up to 1 GB with the least recently used removed first and those unused for 30 days removed, left out of backups, exports and transfers and emptied by Clear workspace cache), so after a restart a Design already prepared opens at once with its charts; what earlier versions kept in the workspace database moves there when the workspace opens, and the `.dashboard-data-cache` and `.dashboard-chart-cache` folders of earlier versions are removed. A Design shows Ready when it has CDRs to show; the values of a filter the profile of a CDR does not list (such as Call Status) or lists only in part (such as City) are read from its columnar copy once per CDR version (about 0.05 s per CDR instead of 0.6 to 1.9 s), a filter every CDR answers (an empty Cluster catalogue included) is not looked for in the combined tables, and the first and last day of each CDR are kept, so the first opening of a Design after a start takes seconds (up to about 15) instead of two to five minutes, and a new selection of CDRs already read (a campaign more or less, another filter) is prepared in about 6 to 13 seconds instead of 25 to 40, with the same filter values and row counts. After a restart the cache lookup of a Design answers at once, the requests that open a Design take the browser's next free connection to the server before those the pages poll on their own, the charts loaded ahead (thumbnails, nearby slides) go one at a time behind the charts in sight, and the fields the charts name that no CDR of the workspace has are not looked for again in every CDR at each preparation; App Events record how long each opening took (`design_opened`, with how long its cache lookup waited in the browser and on the server) and each phase of its preparation. The fields worked out from several columns are evaluated once per distinct combination of their values, and maps and scatter plots group their points by series once and turn into numbers only the points they show, their statistics computed on arrays: a scatter of millions of points draws in seconds instead of 15 to 20, with the same values. The RF charts read their coordinates and RSRP/SINR measures as the numbers the CDRs hold instead of turning them into text and back (a value that is no number stays text, and blank text is no value), the day of each time, to the millisecond or beyond, is worked out once per day, and blank or zero measures are left out once per distinct value: a cold RF coverage map of Data, Voice and Speech draws in about 3.5 to 6 seconds instead of 8 to 13, the same map.
- **Scoring & GAP Analysis — Scoring History:** checking that the CDRs of a calculation are in the Scoring History reads the CDR tables of the workspace once instead of once per CDR, several seconds less per calculation in a workspace with many CDRs.
- **Workspace — Active?:** the In Combined? choice of the CDR tables of Workspace, which chooses the CDRs the modules use, is called **Active?** everywhere (Help included).
- **Non-Qualified Calls — Start and end of a call on its Failure Map:** the site where the call started is ringed in green and the one where it ended (where it failed) in red, with the cells of its start and end named on their sectors, which show at every zoom level; its serving site, and the Serving site health card, are now those of the cell that served its end (the CDR gives that of its start). A cell the Network Inventory does not hold (2G, 3G) is named as the CDR gives it, never taken for an LTE cell, and the nearest site to where the call started or failed is ringed dashed, an estimate the legend names as such. The details of the call and of each Non-Qualified Call around show the sites where it started and ended and, below them, its start and end cells and, on a line of their own, its handover cells, and hovering a sector shows its cell. The clusters of the call are purple on the map and in its header, never the green of the site where it started. Each handover is a cyan arrow from the cell before it to the cell after it (sector to sector when the sectors show, else site to site), numbered with the order of that cell and with its details on hover, and on a Voice call a dashed ring marks on its way where each handover took place, an estimate by the time on each band of its RAT timeline; every line of its cells has a white edge, and lines with the same ends go side by side.
- **Non-Qualified Calls — Group tables and JOIN_ID searches:** a click on a Hotspot ID, Time-Window ID or Cluster ID filters the calls by the group and takes the page to the Non-Qualified Calls panel; the columns of the Hotspots, Time-Window Issues and Clustered Issues tables filter like Excel, kept in the browser for each table; and both JOIN_ID searches (Join ID of the Non-Qualified Calls panel and Go to a JOIN_ID… of an open call) offer the 10 JOIN_IDs searched last, shared and kept in the browser for each workspace.
- **Web interface — Laptop screens:** on a short screen (a 13" to 15" laptop, about 860 px of height or less, or a large zoom of the browser) the header of the application and its tabs take less height and the hero of every module is compact (its title on one line, its description on two), so the content of each module starts in the first screen; the notice of the unassigned values of the CDRs starts minimized there. Larger screens and phones are unchanged.
- **Scoring & GAP Analysis — Points Lost Map in seconds:** the rows of each CDR type are read at once from the columnar copies of the CDRs (their text as categories, without a value per row), every group of tests of a CDR type is mapped at once (each KPI weighs every test once, and the weights are added in the same order, so the map is the same), the places and serving sites are counted once per distinct value, and each methodology draws its Data tests and its other tests in processes of their own (up to four at a time, the ones with the most tests first), put together in the order of the CDR types, so the application keeps answering while it runs and gets its memory back when it ends: a Saved Filter of 9 campaigns with a methodology per campaign went from more than an hour to about 10 to 12 seconds, the same map. A calculation from the CDR rows maps its points the same way.
- **Non-Qualified Calls — Map and details of a call:** the row of the call is read from the columnar copy of its CDR instead of copying the whole CDR first, and the campaign and NR Mode of each CDR are counted once per indexing: the map of a call opens in a few seconds (about one once its CDRs were read) instead of about a minute, and its details at once, with the same content.
- **Non-Qualified Calls — Failure panels:** the calls of the panels of how and where they fail are counted when the calls are indexed (the NQ Call Population Counts table, in Database Management, with a Parquet copy in the columnar store that DuckDB adds up), so the panels open in under a second the first time instead of 15 to 20, with the same numbers.
- **Non-Qualified Calls — Way of a call, way of its car and new Diagnosis hints on its Failure Map:** the way of the call is a winding road with an arrow towards where it went, from where it started to where it ended on a Voice call; a Data test, whose CDR gives only where it started and how far it went, has its pin where it started and its way simulated, its travelled distance along the way of its car (else a dashed circle of that distance), the estimated places of its handovers on that road by the time on each band of its PCell RAT timeline; **START** (green) and **END** (red) mark where it started and ended. The way of its car is drawn as grey roads with arrows through the tests of its drive (15 minutes before and after) that a car could have driven to one after the other, joined to the road of the call where it started and where it ended. The Diagnosis hints add **Overshooting cells** (a cell of the call serving it from at least twice the inter-site distance of its site and pointing at it) and tell ping-pong handovers with how many went back to a cell it had left in how much distance and time.
- **PPT Designer — Former PPT Dashboards:** the module is called **PPT Designer** and its Dashboards are **Designs** everywhere (PPT Designs, PPT Designs Library, View PPT and Edit Design, which opens it in the Designer, PPT Filters and PPT Scope & Filters, the Designer button, and PPT Designs in Reporting Jobs, Bundle Report Templates, Export, Transfer and Backup), its page and API at `/ppt-designer` and `/api/ppt-designer`. Each workspace keeps its Designs, PPT jobs (`output/reports/ppt-designs`), drafts, pictures, App Events and default filters under the new names, migrated in place when it opens; the Modules Profiles, Interface Settings, tickets and scheduled backups name the new module. Links to `/ppt-dashboards`, the Help chapter and presentation of PPT Dashboards, the `{Dashboard}` field of titles, and the packages, backups, transfers, Reporting Jobs and Bundle Report Templates of before keep working.
- **PPT Designer — Charts of one NR Mode:** in a PPT Design of NSA & SA, a chart that keeps one NR Mode (`NR Mode IN (SA)`) reads only the CDRs of that NR Mode, and a chart of both NR Modes, or split by NR Mode, reads them all; the charts are the same.
- **Non-Qualified Calls — Recommended levels of the NQ Prioritization:** the levels shared by every strategy start with the configuration the NQ team recommends: a Cluster period of 0 campaigns, a Time window of 6 minutes and a Time-window distance of 0.8 ISD (500 m in cities and 2,000 m on roads without an ISD), the others as before (High 50 %, Medium 80 %, Cluster size 2, Cluster distance 0.5 ISD, 250 m and 1,000 m, Hotspot join OR, 20 calls Next to analyse). Every workspace takes them once when it opens, whatever it had saved, its calculated calls take their groups, levels and rules again at once, and App Events records each change; new workspaces, new strategies and Default Rules start with them.
- **Campaign filters — Flex campaigns only when checked:** the Flex campaigns (local campaigns, complete but localised in one region, cluster or city: a campaign or Campaign Maps label with the word Flex, such as `UK_Flex_2026` or `26-Q2-Flex`) are used only where they are checked by hand: Latest campaign, Latest 2, 3 and 4 campaigns, Previous campaign and All available campaigns never count them, and a Campaign filter without campaigns (such as All complete campaigns of Scoring) leaves their CDRs out, in Scoring & GAP Analysis, PPT Designer, CDR Analysis, Network Insights, Non-Qualified Calls, Reporting Jobs and Bundle Report Templates; the NQ Prioritization never takes one as the campaign whose Final CDR gives the Expected Gain of the calls of other campaigns (every workspace calculates its calls again once); and the Final, Weekly and Daily CDR cards of Workspace list the CDRs of Flex campaigns in a **Flex CDRs** card of their own inside them, with a notice that says how they are used.
- **Workspace — Cache folder:** the caches of the modules are gathered in the `.cache` folder of each workspace, one folder per module (`scoring`, `network-insights`, `ppt-designer` and `bundle-reports`), instead of `.scoring-cache`, `.network-insights-cache` and `.ppt-designer-cache` beside the workspace database and `output/reports/bundle-reports/cache` among the generated reports, which move there the first time each workspace opens (App Event `workspace_cache_folders_moved`). Like them, it is left out of backups, packages and transfers; `.cdr-store` and `.upload-sessions`, which are not caches, stay where they are.

#### 🐛 Bug fixes:
- **Developer — Tests:** the tests no longer write into the local installation of the application (its tickets, users and databases): every test process points the application at storage of its own.
- **Import / Export / Transfer — Scoring & GAP Favourites Filters:** the import preview and the completion message no longer show as updated (`CDRs: 3 added, 3 removed`) the Favourites Filters with a Campaign choice, such as Latest 2 campaigns, whose campaigns hold other CDRs on the server that sent them: they are compared by their choice, as the import takes the CDRs it stands for in the receiving workspace.
- **Non-Qualified Calls — Panel of a call on laptop screens:** on a short screen (a 14" or 15" laptop, or a large zoom of the browser) the panel of a call scrolls as a whole, its tabs staying at the top, so its tabs and fields are no longer hidden below the bottom of the screen; on a large screen only the tab scrolls, as before.
- **Scoring & GAP Analysis — Stop the Points Lost Map:** the card of a Points Lost Map among the background tasks has a **Stop** button, which ends it at once (it could not be stopped and kept the application busy for hours); its scores, tables and charts stay, the results say the map was stopped or why it failed, a stopped map is drawn again once nobody has used the application for three minutes, and the stop and the resumption are logged in App Events.
- **Import / Export / Transfer — NQ Call Tracking:** the import preview merges the follow-up as the import does, so it no longer shows as updated the calls whose comments the package lacks (the import removes none) or whose follow-up is older than the one of this server; a comment with the text and author of one its call already has (the same analysis imported on both servers) is taken for that one, so transfers back and forth no longer add it again, and the comments a transfer had already repeated on a call are kept once (the first one, removed once per workspace when it opens and logged in App Events). In the details of an updated call, a new or removed comment shows alone, and the What changed column no longer overflows its package.
- **Help — Presentations stopped by a restart:** a generation of the presentations interrupted by a restart of the server no longer leaves its working folder (about 80 MB) in the presentations folder: the next generation removes it.
- **PPT Designer and Chart Builder — Average and Median Vertical Bars:** a chart with Rows and no Columns, or Columns and no Rows, draws its bars again (they were left empty).
- **Non-Qualified Calls — Cells repeated in a row:** a cell the cell trace of the CDR repeats in a row (a new TAC, or a moment on a cell without identity it came back from) is one cell of the call, as NetCheck shows it: the Failure Map, its cells and those of the NQ calls around no longer show a handover between the same cell, its Diagnosis counts the handovers right, each cell takes the PCI the CDR gives it, and the DT Analyzer RCA, calculated again once, finds the ping-pong handovers these repeats hid.
- **Chart Builder — Refresh chart:** the button at the right of the head of the chart shows its icon (it was an empty purple button).
- **Import / Export / Transfer — PPT Templates used by PPT Designs:** a PPT Template a PPT Design of the destination uses is replaced when the transfer also carries the PPT Designs, checked or left unchecked because the destination has them already, so transferring again no longer shows it as updated (`Content`) while its import kept it; without the PPT Designs it keeps its content, as the import preview now tells.
- **Non-Qualified Calls — Page position when the filters change:** applying or removing a filter (a JOIN_ID, a chip, a card or a value of a chart), or changing the page, order or rows of the list, keeps the panel in use, or else the one being read, where it was while the calls and every panel on screen are drawn again, instead of leaving the page on another section; scrolling meanwhile leaves the page where it is taken.
- **PPT Designer — Generated comments of failure counts:** a Count Stacked Horizontal Bars chart whose filter keeps only the failed and dropped tests (`Call_Status IN (Failed, Dropped)`, such as **Voice failures** and **Voice failures per City**) is commented by the counts it draws (the operators from the one with the most, the change since the previous campaign, the CDR type, city and bar with the most, and the operators without any), also by Kiro CLI and Claude, which get those counts as facts; it wrote no comment, or shares of 100%.
- **PPT Designer — Generated comments of a transferred Design:** a PPT Design transferred to another server, where the same CDRs were uploaded on their own, no longer says its generated comments were written for another selection (campaigns, filters or CDRs): the selection is now named by the CDRs it holds (their type, stage, campaigns and rows) instead of their ids and processing times on each server, so processing a CDR again or adding CDRs the Design does not read no longer marks them out of date either; the comments generated before this version are no longer checked.

#### 📚 Documentation:
- **Help — Workspace presentation:** two new slides show the columnar store of the CDRs (the Parquet copies read with DuckDB by every module, the light workspace database and Tableau connected through PostgreSQL with pg_duckdb) and its impact, before and now.

---

## Release: v0.9.1
### Release Date: 2026-10-27
#### ⚠️ Breaking Changes:
- **Workspace Config — PPT Templates packages:** the PPT Templates of exports, transfers and backups are now one library (`report-templates/library/<template>.csv`), which versions before 0.9.1 do not import; this version still imports the NSA and SA libraries of the packages and backups of before.

#### 🌟 New Features:
- **Workspace Config — Operator Maps · NR Modes:** each Operator label has the NR Modes it has a network in (**NSA and SA**, **NSA only** or **SA only**), and its tests of the CDRs of the other NR Mode, such as the SA CDRs of Three, which has no SA, are out of scope in every module: Scoring & GAP Analysis (with its Scoring Trends and Geographical Areas), Network Insights, PPT Dashboards, CDR Analysis, the Chart Builder, Reporting, the filters and Non-Qualified Calls, whose Out of Scope shows them read only beside the regions and the operators that are not Focus operators. Each module names them beside its selection (*Out of scope: SA · Three*), and the Scoring charts of the operators at their foot, on the page and in the PowerPoint and Word. The Operator Maps state it under the NR Modes of the label, show its NR Modes beside the Role of its names of the CDRs and sum up what is out of scope in the workspace (in every module and in Non-Qualified Calls only); a label whose NQ calls of the SA campaigns barely ran on NR SA gets a suggestion, never applied on its own. The NR Modes travel with the Operator Maps in exports, transfers and backups, and the NR Mode · operator pairs the NQ Prioritization left out of scope (SA · Three) became the NR Modes of their label once.

#### 🚀 Enhancements:
- **Super Admin — AI Comment Writers:** the key of the server of Kiro CLI or Claude is removed with **Remove key**, beside its field, once confirmed, without saving the rest of the panel, and **Open Artifactory**, beside Install Kiro CLI, opens the screened Kiro CLI packages of the Ericsson Artifactory, with a note on which file to download; Install Kiro CLI also takes **Kiro CLI.dmg** when the app runs on a Mac.
- **App Logs — Execution Log:** it opens on its latest lines and follows the new ones; scrolling up stops it, and **Go to the end** (or scrolling back down to the end) follows them again.
- **App Logs — App Events:** the Date filter offers **Last Day**, the newest date of the events, to include or to exclude; the days are disabled while it is chosen.
- **Super Admin — Users:** a **Last Log-in** column, after User, shows the date and time of the last sign-in of each account and how long ago it was (*Never* before its first one); the sign-ins already recorded in App Events fill it in once. The title of every column but Password sorts the table by it, and a second click reverses the order; the browser remembers the sort.
- **Non-Qualified Calls — Hotspots, Time-Window & Clustered Issues:** the Consecutive Issues are named **Time-Window Issues** everywhere (the panel and its table, the filter, the condition and the **Time window** and **Time-window distance** settings of the Prioritization Rules, the Groups and map of a call, the PPT and Word exports and the Help presentation), and the IDs of the groups take one letter: **H-** a hotspot, **T-** a time-window issue and **C-** a cluster (such as *T-E94389*). Every call keeps them as **Hotspot ID**, **Time-Window ID** and **Cluster ID**, fields *From the NQ Prioritization* after the Repetition Place, to show as columns of the table with **Columns**, filter by and export with **Export Excel** (one ID each; the Cluster ID is that of the cluster of its NR Mode, as the rules take it); existing workspaces get them once.
- **Non-Qualified Calls — Map of the call:** its groups are drawn as the Groups slide of the presentation shows them: a green circle around each cluster and a blue ellipse along each time-window issue of its hotspot, its own bolder, every NQ call of them inside one, and the failures of each time-window issue joined and numbered in their order (shown or hidden with **Time-window paths**); the hotspot is an orange zone, their envelope with the OR join or where they meet with AND. The NQ calls are filled in the colour of their first group (hotspot orange, cluster green, time window blue) and outlined in those of their other groups, those at its site outlined in red and the others grey; filtering the list by its cluster or its time-window issue leaves that group alone on the map, and the legend lists only what the map shows, its groups apart. On phones and tablets two fingers zoom it (a pinch) towards their middle.
- **Scoring & GAP Analysis — GAP bars:** the value of a GAP no longer sits over its bar: it is written beside the zero line on the side the bar leaves free, left of it when the operator is ahead and right of it when it is behind, in the GAP cells of the Scoring and GAP tables and in the KPI GAP Profile of the PowerPoint and Word exports in every theme, and on the page in the NetCheck and Umlaut tables and in the KPI GAP Profile.
- **Workspace Config — Campaign Maps:** an exception label also gives its year and quarter when it is written as the label format does, so with `{yy}-Q{quarter}{-mode}` the label *26-Q2-Flex* is ordered in 2026 Q2, after the campaigns of that quarter, instead of first, in every chart, table, filter and report.
- **Ticket Center — Tickets:** the list filters by the days the tickets were **Created** and last **Updated** (several at once, after **Last Day**, the newest day of the tickets, which unticks and disables the days while it is chosen), and every column filters it as in Excel from the **▾** of its header (Updated by year, month and day); the filters add up with the chips, **Reset filters** and the counts of the dashboard clear them, and the browser remembers them.
- **Ticket Center — Ticket references:** a ticket number written after `#` in a description or a comment (`#7`) links to that ticket and shows its title and status when pointed at, on its page and in the editor; a number of no ticket, one inside a word, a web address or code, or one with a backslash before its `#` stays text.
- **Ticket Center — Opened and Resolved dates:** **Manage** (super-admins) corrects the dates a ticket was opened and resolved (also closed or rejected), for example for a ticket recorded after the work it describes, and its timeline follows them in its order: the resolution (the change that set its status and the comment just before it) takes the Resolved date, the earlier entries outside both dates take the Opened date and the others keep theirs; App Events records the change.
- **Workspace Management — Campaign of the CDRs:** the Campaign of a CDR takes a name after its quarter (`2025-Q2 Flex` is saved as `2025-Q2-Flex`), a campaign of its own with its own group in the CDR tables, and a CDR whose filename has no quarter or year takes the campaign of its rows as Campaign Maps reads it: rows of `UK_Flex_2026` with the exception `25-Q2-Flex` propose `2025-Q2-Flex`.
- **PPT Dashboards & Workspace Config — NR Mode as a filter, NSA & SA in one Dashboard:** a Report Template has no NR Mode: one library whose templates serve the CDRs of any NR Mode, their titles naming it with `{NR Mode}` (NSA, SA or NSA & SA) and `{NR Mode Name}` (Non-Standalone, Standalone or both), and a **Preview NR Mode** (NSA, SA or NSA & SA) in the editor. Each Dashboard keeps the NR Mode of the CDRs it reads as part of its selection, whatever its template: **NSA**, **SA** or **NSA & SA**, which reads both with a **Campaign (NSA)** and a **Campaign (SA)** filter, each with its Latest choices, and has a name unique in the workspace. Every row of the combined CDR tables keeps the NR Mode of its CDR in the **NR Mode** field: a chart keeps one NR Mode with the filter `NR Mode IN (SA)` (and says so in a Dashboard that does not read it), NR Mode splits and labels series in Rows Split, Columns Split, Legend and the Dynamic Fields, and a chart reading both NR Modes without NR Mode or Campaign in its splits gets NR Mode as the last level of its legend. The SA series take a lighter tint of the colour of their operator (and a dashed line), the newest campaign of each NR Mode the thickest CDF line; a chart names the NR Mode without samples of a field only the CDRs of the other one have, which the template editor warns about, and the auto comments compare each operator in SA with itself in NSA. When a workspace opens (and when a package or backup of before is imported), the NSA and SA copies of a template that only told the mode apart in their titles become one template, which their Dashboards, PPT jobs and Reporting (old) jobs use; the App Events record it. Reporting Jobs (also with NSA & SA) and Bundle Report Templates generate any saved Dashboard with their own NR Mode (a Dashboard of NSA & SA keeps both in a Bundle; favourites of before keep checked only the Dashboards of their NR Mode), without its saved comments when that is not its own. Every Voice and Speech test takes the NR Mode of its CDR in Reporting (old) and the template previews too, instead of being classified by its call mode. Every workspace receives once the example template and Dashboard **FDFS DL (NSA & SA)**.
- **Workspace Management — Starter content of new workspaces:** a new workspace starts with the current content of the UK workspace without what only fits the UK (its regions, London, its cities one by one and its operators): six Report Templates (*Tableau Charts - Only Maps* too), ten Dashboards (the NSA and SA ones of RF Quality and the Tableau templates too), five Scoring report configurations, seven Query Builder queries, 18 Favourites Filters, 12 favourite Bundle Report Templates and nine Reporting Jobs in its order (each job of a favourite generates the one it names), the *Unknown* Vendor label and, for the United Kingdom, its Operator labels (*Three*, NSA only, and *VF SM*); the default Campaign Map writes the year with two digits (`{yy}-Q{quarter}{-mode}`: *26-Q2*, *26-Q2-SA*), also in the workspaces without their own Campaign Maps.
- **Ticket Center — Ticket navigation:** the page of a ticket opens the **First**, **Previous**, **Next** and **Last** ticket from the buttons at its top, beside its position (*3 of 12*): in the order of the Tickets list as the browser showed it last, with its filters, or by number among every ticket when the ticket is not in that list (*By number*); pointing at a button shows the number and title of the ticket it opens.
- **Scoring & GAP Analysis — Excluded Calls panel:** the panel sits below the Scoring & GAP Results and above the Favourites Filters, and the floating section bar and the sections menus of the module (its tab and the side menu) list it in that place.
- **Scoring & GAP Analysis, Network Insights, PPT Dashboards and Reporting Jobs — CDRs used:** the CDRs of each type are listed newest campaign first (the order of the Campaign Maps reversed: in a quarter the SA campaign before the NSA one) instead of by upload date; the CDRs of the same campaign keep the newest uploaded first.
- **Scoring & GAP Analysis — PowerPoint exports:** the Scoring PowerPoint documents are generated about a third faster, with the same slides.
- **Developer — Tests:** the test suite runs in parallel, one process per CPU (`pytest-xdist`; `-n 0` runs it in one process), the slowest tests first as the last run measured them; each test opens a copy of a workspace prepared once per process instead of creating and seeding one, the synthetic Scoring results are calculated once per run, and the Scoring export tests export only the panels they check.

#### 🐛 Bug fixes:
- **Super Admin — AI Comment Writers:** **Install Kiro CLI** no longer fails with an HTTP 500 error before reading the package, takes the kiro-cli program of a .deb among its shell completions and icons of the same name, and says when a package is for another system (a Linux package on a Mac, a .dmg on the Linux server).
- **PPT Dashboards — Generate comments:** the scatter charts no longer fail with *No module named 'scipy'*, on the Dashboard, in its PPTs and in the cache prewarm recorded in App Events, when they measure how their two KPIs move together for the comments.
- **Non-Qualified Calls — small screens:** the tables of the Hotspots, Time-Window & Clustered Issues panel no longer squeeze their columns until the words split and From and To overlap: on screens too narrow for them they scroll sideways in the panel (as the other tables do) and From and To put the time below the day; the periods of the Progress View table no longer split at their hyphen, and the RCA source tabs of Root Cause Analysis fit the panel on phones.
- **Workspace Config — Operator Maps:** choosing the Role **Include** for a label that was Focus or Exclude is saved again, from its row and from **Save Labels**, instead of keeping the Role it had.

#### 📚 Documentation:
- **Help — PPT Dashboards, Workspace Config, Reporting and Technical Considerations:** Report Templates without NR Mode, the NR Mode of each Dashboard (NSA & SA in one Dashboard), its tokens, the NR Mode field of the charts and the merge of the NSA and SA templates of before.
- **Help — Workspace Management, Workspace Config, Configuration and Technical Considerations:** the starter content of a new workspace, its Operator and Vendor labels and the campaign labels of the default Campaign Map.
