## Release: v0.9.0
### Release Date: 2026-10-26
#### ⚠️ Breaking Changes:

#### 🌟 New Features:
- **Non-Qualified Calls — Failure Map:** a new step of the call between RCA and Failure Details (also the **Map** button of its header, and **Map of the call** in Failure Details and Call Details, which opens it larger), with Failure Map and Failure Details as phases of the call and a wider call panel that keeps every step and phase on one line and five fields per row, shows on OpenStreetMap the area where the call failed: where it started and failed, the cells of its Cell ID trace at their inventory sites (start, handovers, end and the NR leg of NSA calls by PCI), the inventory sites of the networks that serve its operator with their sectors, bands, PCI and states (cells with the PCI and channel of a cell of the call flagged), its repetitions at the same site, its hotspot and the other Non-Qualified Calls around (of its CDR, its campaign or every campaign, chosen in **NQ calls from**, where *This CDR* names its type, such as *This CDR (Voice)*, and coloured by their relation to the call, call type or campaign in **Colour NQ calls by**), and RSRP / SINR samples of the tests around (from its CDR or every CDR of its campaign, chosen in **RSRP / SINR samples from**, shown or hidden with their own button), of its operator, of the Focus operators of the Operator Maps or of every operator (**Operators**). It navigates like the Network Insights maps (Ctrl/Cmd+wheel zoom in the call, the wheel alone in the larger view, rectangle zoom, distance tool, double-click to enlarge), shows the details of each element on hover and click (the call and each Non-Qualified Call with its call type and campaign, each sample with its campaign and CDR), lists below it the radio of the call and its cells, the calls around, Diagnosis hints (coverage and coverage holes, interference, serving distance, PCI collisions, cells out of service, the other operators, repetition and handovers; the serving distance and the coverage holes judged by the inter-site distance of the sites, the mean distance to their 3 nearest sites of the same network), the root causes decided at its place (with Use for the call), its drive in the 15 minutes around it, the NQ rate of its serving site per campaign and the radio per operator, downloads or copies as an image with the panel of the element chosen, and App Events records each map opened.
- **Non-Qualified Calls — DT Analyzer RCA:** a new RCA source, in every workspace whatever its CDR type and also beside the RCA of the provider (the NetCheck RCA), finds the Root Category, Root Cause and root domain of every Non-Qualified Call, test and Speech sample from the data of its CDR, read through the field profile of the workspace: its failure description, phase, technology and side, the LTE and NR radio of both sides (RSRP, RSRQ, SINR, BLER and transmit power), its mobility (technologies and handovers) and its KPIs (throughput, access, DNS and TCP times, packet loss, latency, setup time, MOS and RTP). Ordered rules decide, the first one of the service of the call whose conditions hold giving its root cause on the Root Cause Catalog with the evidence of the values that met it; the default rules (what the provider reports outside the radio, then the radio, then the service, and Not identified) are the same in every workspace and give every call a Root Category (the catalog gains 8 for the causes found outside the radio coverage and interference: Protocol / Server, RAN Performance, Core Network, Authentication & Registration, IMS / VoLTE, EPS Fallback, BLER LTE and BLER NR, each default cause mapped to its own), and admins change them in **DT Analyzer RCA Rules** of the NQ Analysis Configuration (order, services, conditions, thresholds, category and cause), each rule with the calls it explains. With a Network Inventory it also reads the network around the call, as its Failure Map: a coverage hole (the nearest site of the networks of the operator beyond 1.5 times its inter-site distance), overshooting (a serving site beyond twice its inter-site distance), PCI collisions within 2 km and cells out of service on its sites, before the plain coverage and interference; it finds a lack of resources from the provider (insufficient resources or congestion of the core), from the signalling of the CDR itself (an RRC connection or immediate assignment rejected) and from the few resource blocks a Data test cut off with good radio was given (a loaded cell); and ping-pong handovers in the cell trace of the call. The catalog gains the Root Category RAN Capacity and the Root Causes Access rejected (no resources), Insufficient resources and Ping-pong handover, and the workspaces with rules of their own take the new default rules once. The Root Cause Catalog keeps the values the rules give: they show DT in place of their remove button and cannot be removed, nor a Root Category or Root Cause renamed, until no rule gives them. Its **Learn from the decisions of the analysts** option (off by default) gives a call the Root Category and Cause the analysts gave most of the other calls of its pattern (the same rule, service and failure of the provider), when at least 2 of them and more than half agree, also for the calls the rules leave Not identified. It comes first, before the RCA of the CDR (the NetCheck RCA) and the Ericsson RCA, in the Root Cause Sources (also in the comparisons, Selected Cause ↔ DT Analyzer RCA and DT Analyzer RCA ↔ NetCheck RCA) and Root Cause Analysis panels (with their filters; the Root Cause Analysis opens on it), the recommendation (weight 2, with the rule and evidence in its card, Not identified proposing nothing), five read-only fields of the RCA section (Root Domain, Root Category, Root Cause, Rule and Evidence) and the Excel, PowerPoint, Word and Reporting exports. In the hierarchies of the Root Cause Sources, titled as the cards above them (DT Analyzer RCA → Root Cause, NetCheck RCA → Root Cause, Ericsson RCA → Root Cause and Selected Root Cause, also in the PowerPoint and Word exports), every category, cause and root domain tag of each RCA filters its calls. Calls are calculated when they are indexed (the CDRs are indexed again once to read its values) and again whenever the rules or the catalog change; the rules travel with the NQ Calls configuration (export, import, transfers and backups), App Events records their changes, and Database Management lists its NQ DT Analyzer RCA Results.
- **CDR Field Dictionary:** a new subpanel of Mappings & Reference Data in Workspace Config shows, for every field the modules read (processing, CDR-Main fields, Non-Qualified Calls, DT Analyzer RCA, Network Insights and Vendor Mapping), the columns of the workspace's CDR files that can hold it in order of preference and the one its processed CDRs have. Each workspace can change the columns of any field (for example the Option D call setup of Umlaut CDRs); only the changed fields are saved, recorded in App Events and carried with Mappings & Reference Data in import, export, transfer, backup and restore.
- **PPT Dashboards — Generate comments:** icons after the slide navigation give editors analysis comments for the open slide, or for every slide at once (only the slides without comments, replacing the generated ones or every comment), with a menu for the writer and the Kiro Brief, written from the exact figures its charts draw with the Dashboard filters and the legend items hidden in the viewer: every chart type of the PPT Templates (medians, tails and worst classes of CDFs and histograms, bars, shares of the stacked bars and failure counts, correlations of scatters, the weakest class of maps and where it gathers, the highest and lowest rows of tables), the change since the previous campaign and the gap to the leader from the point of view of the Focus operators, the vendors of each operator in Multivendor and the charts of a slide read together (NR against LTE, one operator per chart); groups with fewer than 10 samples are never named and changes too small or not significant read *stable* or *on par*. Suggestions are reviewed, edited and added; generated comments keep a tag and say when they were written for another selection, and **Auto comments** write them, with the writer chosen in the viewer or the job, into a Dashboard PPT or the PPT of a Reporting Job without changing the Dashboard. The rules run on the server; **AI Comment Writers** in Super Admin enables LLM writers that rewrite the same facts, every figure they write checked against them: **Kiro CLI** (the default, installed from the screened Artifactory package into the configuration folder) and **Claude**, each with one key of the server or each user's own key (user menu → **API keys**) as Super Admin chooses, and the **Kiro Brief** to export the facts to Kiro IDE and import its comments. Keys stay in a file of the server, never in its database, exports or backups; App Events records every generation, writer and change, and the Help and the PPT Dashboards presentation explain them.

#### 🚀 Enhancements:
- **Non-Qualified Calls:** while a call is open the mouse wheel scrolls only its details, never the page below them.
- **Non-Qualified Calls:** in the RCA tab of a call, the values of each RCA source (DT Analyzer RCA, NetCheck, Ericsson RCA, the analysts) start a row of their own, four per row so each pair of the Ericsson RCA keeps to one row.
- **Non-Qualified Calls:** in an open call, Analyst and Reviewer sit beside the Assignee (saved as soon as they change), the Unique ID of the previous tool beside the JOIN_ID, the values of its repeated issue on a row of their own in Prioritization Info, and the Activity tab is titled Call Activity Log with a comment box that reaches the bottom of the panel.
- **Ready for more benchmarking providers (Umlaut and P3 coming soon):** the tool is prepared to analyse the CDRs of benchmarking providers other than NetCheck, such as Umlaut and P3, each with its own field names, values and methodologies. Every workspace handles the CDRs of one provider, its CDR type, which it keeps once it has Data, Voice or Speech CDRs, and P3 CDR joins Umlaut CDR as coming soon. Ingestion, Non-Qualified Calls (index, analyses import and Failure Map), Network Insights, the serving-cell Vendor Mapping and Map Areas read the CDR columns through the field profile of the workspace's CDR type (`cdr_field_profiles`); NetCheck CDRs are read as before. The Umlaut reader and profile are ready too, while Umlaut CDR stays coming soon until a multi-operator export validates it: each Umlaut service workbook is a CDR (its Statistics sheet left out, the headers of the second side of a call named `Side2 …`), every field keeps its Umlaut name, the results read SUCCESS as Completed and the throughputs in Mbit/s, the tool adds `Campaign` (from the file name), `Test_Key` and `ECI`, an Umlaut call is Non-Qualified when its qualifier is NOT QUALIFIED, a Voice call takes the mean radio of its speech samples and Non-Qualified Calls names the RCA of the CDR and its definition of an NQ call after the provider (Umlaut RCA, also in its documents). In an Umlaut workspace the upload names the types *Umlaut CDR — Data / Voice / Speech*, proposes each file its type from its service and the Final stage, and offers **Leave out** for the All_Data and All_Voice summaries.
- **Workspaces table:** the workspaces table of Workspace Management shows the CDR Type and the Country of each workspace right after its name, before Access and Size.
- **Root Cause Catalog of Non-Qualified Calls:** its Domains come first, before the Root Categories, Root Causes and Mapping, and the catalog opens on them.
- **Direct links to the Presentations:** **Copy link**, on each presentation card of the Help and beside **Close** in the slide viewer, copies a short link (`/presentations/<id>?slide=<n>`) that opens the presentation, at the slide shown, directly in the slide viewer of Product Presentations, also for whoever has not signed in, at the new **Public address of the server** of Application Config when it has one (WhatsApp on Android opens no link with an IP) and otherwise at the address the application was opened with; Help pages with `?presentation=<id>&slide=<n>` keep opening it; where it is shared (WhatsApp, Teams, Slack…), its preview shows the title and description of the presentation and the picture of that slide (Open Graph), and that of the Product Presentations chapter the cover of the Full Presentation; while a presentation is open, the address of the page is that link too.
- **Presentations on phones:** the slide viewer of the Help fits the part of the screen the browser leaves visible, so its buttons never hide behind the address bar, with the arrows of the slides first and large, then **▶ Slideshow**, **Copy link** and **Download** (× at the top closes it); a swipe changes slide, also in the slideshow, two fingers zoom and a double tap shows the whole slide. Held sideways, also on the narrowest phones, the slides it lists stand on the left and every button on one row, **Close** among them (the narrowest show only the play icon and **Link**). The settings of the slideshow rise from the bottom of the screen on phones, in two columns when held sideways, and the slideshow draws its play, pause, arrows and stop as icons, the same on every device (some phones showed ▶ as an emoji).
- **Hotspots, Consecutive failures and Clusters of the NQ Prioritization:** a failure is consecutive when another failure of its operator, NR Mode, campaign and drive (the car, from the measurement system of the CDR) started within the new **Consecutive time** (15 min by default) and the new **Consecutive distance**, whatever its call type; a call belongs to a cluster when the calls of its operator and NR Mode within the Cluster distance of it or at its site reach the Cluster size, in its campaign and the campaigns before it (the **Cluster period**, now in campaigns, whatever the date of the calls); a hotspot joins the failures of an operator, NR Mode and campaign whatever their call type (4 Data failures and 1 Voice failure make a hotspot of 5), each linked to another one within the Consecutive time in the same drive OR the Cluster distance (or at the site where it ended), or within both with the new **Hotspot join** set to AND (OR by default; Hotspot distance and Hotspot time are gone); the Ping tests and the calls out of scope (its regions, such as Vendor B, the operators that are not Focus operators and the NR Mode · operator pairs chosen) are left out of all three, so that with a Cluster period of 0 a hotspot of OR holds exactly the consecutive and the clustered failures. Both distances follow the network: the **Cluster distance** is 0.5 and the Consecutive distance 2 inter-site distances (ISD) of the sites nearest to the two calls (the larger one, from the Network Inventories, kept for each call), so that a car that drove from one city to the next breaks a run, and without an ISD they take meters by area (250 m and 1 km in cities, 1 km and 5 km on connecting roads), all set in the Prioritization Rules. The Data steps of list 1 of both Dual Queue strategies take **Hotspots** (formerly Consecutive failures AND Cluster), and the steps saved with Consecutive failures and Cluster alone took Hotspots once, in every strategy. A new **Hotspots, Consecutive & Clustered Issues** panel, below the NQ Prioritization & Queues and in the sections bar and the menus, shows the criteria on top as chips (with the NR Mode of the clusters: when the NR Mode filter, or the campaigns filtered, choose one NR Mode, its clusters count that NR Mode alone) and three tables with the same columns and look, each with its calls in a badge: **Hotspots**, its title telling the join (*Hotspots (Consecutives OR Clustered Issues)*), **Consecutive Issues** (each run of consecutive failures) and **Clustered Issues** (each cluster, the calls linked by distance or site, formerly the Repeated issues by site), with a **Hotspot / Consecutive / Cluster ID** that selects the calls of the group and tells its sites and its period, the operator, NR Mode, campaign, **Site(s)**, **From** and **To** (*20 Nov 16:18*), calls and their Voice / WhatsApp / Data calls in a stacked bar, points lost, open calls and main root category, the more general tables first (the Hotspots first with OR, last with AND, and between the other two the one with more calls); PowerPoint, Word and the Bundle Report Templates show it as a panel of its own, with the 25 groups with the most calls of each table.
- **Map of the call and its groups:** the NQ calls of the hotspot, cluster and run of consecutive failures of the call show wherever they are in red, those of its site in violet (red goes first, a call of both ringed in violet) and the other NQ calls of its operator in amber, and coloured by call type or campaign they keep a red or violet ring; the calls on one point make one mark with how many they are (×11), **Fit** frames every call of its groups and its site, and **Non-Qualified Calls around** counts each group and those within the radius; a call opens with the radius that holds its hotspot and cluster, or 1 km when it is in neither; every NQ call of its campaign within the radius comes, and the 600 nearest of the other campaigns (the header tells how many more were left out), and its counts take the call itself too; hovering an NQ call shows its operator, and the header of every call shows its groups (its hotspot, cluster and consecutive run with their NR Mode, ID, calls, sites and period), a click on one filtering the list of calls by it after confirming, in place of any other group filtered, as a click on a group ID of the panel now does too; on the map each group of the call has a colour of its own (hotspot blue, cluster green, consecutive run cyan) and the group that filters the list is red, and the list keeps the page of the open call as the filters change.
- **Next to analyse of the NQ Prioritization:** the Voice, Data and Tracked queues fit their card without scrolling sideways, the JOIN_ID always on one line and the other columns and headers wrapping when the card is narrow.

#### 🐛 Bug fixes:
- **Non-Qualified Calls:** a section opened from the menu of the tab or from Modules & Sections stays at the top of the page until every panel above it is drawn, also when they take more than a minute in a large workspace (in Safari it moved down as they filled).
- **Sections bar of Non-Qualified Calls on phones:** the sections bar of Non-Qualified Calls stays at the top of the screen below the Menu bar while the page scrolls, as in the other modules, instead of hiding behind it.
- **NetCheck RCA on the Root Cause Catalog:** the NetCheck values find their Root Category in the Root Cause Sources (NetCheck RCA → Root Category), the Root Cause Analysis, the recommendations and the exports: a value that names a root cause but no category takes the category of that cause (DNS → Protocol / Server, also for the Ericsson RCA), the categories of the DT Analyzer RCA have the keywords of the NetCheck values they stand for (Registration reject, Tracking Area Update, RRC, BLER Problems…; added to the workspaces whose categories had none) and the Failure Comment counts only when the Failure Category and Subcategory give nothing. In the UK workspace, the NQ calls with NetCheck values and no catalog category went from 3,513 to 2.

#### 📚 Documentation:
- **Non-Qualified Calls:** the Help explains the Map of the call, its layers and controls, and its troubleshooting, and the DT Analyzer RCA with its values, the network around the call, the lack of resources, every default rule and the root domains, categories and causes it gives, with the new **DT Analyzer RCA** presentation; Benchmarking Providers and the Roadmap say it is available.
- **CDR type:** Workspace Config, Workspace Management and the Roadmap explain that a workspace keeps the CDR type of its CDRs, the plan for Umlaut and P3 CDRs, the upload of Umlaut CDRs and the CDR Field Dictionary.
- **Benchmarking Providers:** a new Help chapter describes the adaptation to Umlaut and P3 CDRs: the status of each phase and the multi-provider model. Below it in Help Navigation, **Umlaut CDR Compatibility** and **P3 CDR Compatibility** open the compatibility report of each provider in its own design (the Umlaut one compares every NetCheck field the tool uses with its Umlaut equivalent and tells what each module will have; the P3 one waits for a sample of P3 CDRs). Its presentation, **Benchmarking Providers Roadmap**, joins the presentations of the Help Home and Product Overview in the design of its own deck.
- **Presentations:** every main module has a presentation of its own, very visual and with the Ericsson logo at the top right, at the start of its Help chapter: Workspace (with **Workspace Config** after it, everything to set up in a new workspace: operator, vendor and campaign maps with their colours and order, Map Areas, Serving Networks and the methodology), CDR Analysis, PPT Dashboards (with its PPT Templates and their editor), Scoring & GAP Analysis, Network Insights, Non-Qualified Calls (with the groups of NQ calls, consecutive runs, clusters and hotspots, the map of the call, and the DT Analyzer RCA beside it), Reporting, Builders and Ticket Center, and **Administration** after them (Admin and Super Admin: users and access with the Users table, the databases and the Database Viewer, backups, packages and transfers, Application, Admin, Super Admin and Workspace Config, and App Events). Help Home and **Product Presentations**, a new Help chapter of General above Product Overview that gathers every presentation (Product Overview points to it), show them in **Module presentations**, after those of the tool, and the **Full Presentation** joins every presentation in one deck: the Overview, the Benchmarking Providers Roadmap, every module with Workspace Config after Workspace, Network Insights after CDR Analysis and Administration after them, the DT Analyzer RCA and the Roadmap. They open in the slide viewer and download as PowerPoint. The server generates them all, with the pictures of their slides, into the data directory, where they stay across updates, and none is kept with the application: always in the background (about half a minute), so the application never waits for them, at its start when one is missing (the Help says which ones are coming), when their figures or the generator change, and on demand with **Regenerate Presentations** in Super Admin → Presentations; the Docker image copies their sources.

## Release: v0.8.6
### Release Date: 2026-10-23
#### ⚠️ Breaking Changes:

#### 🌟 New Features:
- **NQ Calls in Scope Only:** a switch above the filters of Non-Qualified Calls leaves the calls out of the scope of the team out of the whole module, as if they were not there: the table, every panel, chart, count and share (also the calls of the NQ Rate and their percentages), the exports and the values the filters offer. Beside it, a line tells what is out of scope and **Out of Scope** opens the Regions and the Operators out of scope, each operator with SA, NSA or All, with a link to the Operator Maps to choose the Focus operators, the only ones that can be in scope; the same settings as the Out of Scope card of the Prioritization Rules (admins and super-admins). The switch is kept with the filters, its chip leads the chips of the filters, darker than the others, **Reset Filters** leaves it as it is, and App Events records each change. The calls of every CDR are indexed again once, to know the Region of every call of the NQ Rate.

#### 🚀 Enhancements:
- **Dual Queue strategies of the NQ Prioritization:** **Prioritization Rules** chooses the strategy of the workspace (admins and super-admins): **Dual Queue (Cities first)**, the default, **Dual Queue (Roads first)**, **Dual Queue (Scoring Based)** (Points Lost Based before, the Custom rules of the first version, now steps: by the impact levels, list 2 of every strategy as its only list; rules of its own become its steps), **Vodafone Proposal** (list 1 only, in one **Unique queue** that the panel and its queue chart show alone: every SA and then NSA Voice and WhatsApp call, the Data calls of a cluster and then every other call with P5; the Ping tests and Excluded Calls tracked with P8) or strategies of your own (**New Strategy**, a copy of the one shown, deleted with **Delete Strategy**); **Rename Strategy** renames any of them. The Dual Queue strategies give every call a step of two lists: during a campaign by what the call says about itself (SA Voice in Cities, SA Data, SA Voice on Connecting Roads, SA WhatsApp, NSA Voice, NSA Data, NSA WhatsApp, as in list 2; SA Voice on Connecting Roads before Cities with Roads first, and in both the data failures that are consecutive and of a cluster), and once the Final CDR of the campaign is scored by the real impact, the same list 2 in every strategy (SA Voice High P1, SA Data High P2, SA WhatsApp High P3, SA Voice and WhatsApp Medium then every other SA Voice and WhatsApp call P4, NSA Voice P5, NSA Data High P6, NSA WhatsApp P7, Tracked P8). Admins edit every field of each step in the dialog (one list at a time, with a **Weekly CDRs** / **Final CDRs** switch; list 2 can be turned off for each strategy, list 1 then orders every call), add (**+ Add step**) or delete steps, and move them with drag and drop, also whole Priority blocks by the handle before their Priority, which swap with the Priority they are dropped onto (the **Queue order** card explains how each queue takes its calls): Priority (P1 to P8; P6 to P8 are added to the Priority list, and every Priority without a colour takes that of the queues and steps, from red P1 to grey P8), NR Mode, Call Type, Area (Cities or Connecting Roads), Condition (each in a colour of its own, the impact levels as the Scoring Impact; the same for every call type and both lists: Consecutive failures, Hotspots, Cluster, the impact levels by Expected Gain (from the latest Final CDR during the campaign, then the call's own points; the calls in scope compared only with each other, as in **#Calls** and the Scoring Impact of the calls), Drops and Setup failures; several conditions, each joined to the one before by **AND** or **OR**, AND first, and grouped in one level of parentheses with **AND (** / **OR (**; **Every call** alone), Operators (by default the Focus operators by name, those that use SA in the SA steps, such as `VF`, and `VF & Three` in the others; or **Focused Operators**, or operators of the workspace joined with ` & `) and Queue (the SA calls of an operator that no SA step takes, such as Three, take the NSA steps). Voice and WhatsApp calls go to the **Voice queue** and Data calls to the **Data queue**, shown side by side in the panel, PowerPoint and Word; inside a step the calls with the highest **Expected Gain** (what their failure lost in the Final CDR of the latest campaign) go first, then the oldest. **Prioritization Queue** and **Expected Gain** are new fields of the calls, the call panel shows every value of the NQ Prioritization of the call, read only, in a **Prioritization Info** subpanel above its phases instead of the Activity tab (the Prioritization Rule, there and in the table, as what its step takes with its list and number in tags below it, such as *SA Voice · Medium impact* over **FINAL** **STEP 3**), the Priority of a call never changes once its analysis has started, a **#Calls** column tells how many calls each step takes in the latest campaign with its Final CDR (named as the Campaign Maps say) and the **Calls per Queue** chart beside the steps sums them by Priority for the Voice and Data queues in vertical bars (with the calls outside the queues in a folded note: tracked by kind, taken by no step by operator, out of scope by region or operator without SA), the calls of the **Out of Scope** card above the steps (its **Regions**, Vendor B by default, and its **Operators** by the labels of the Operator Maps, each with SA, NSA or All: always every operator that is not a Focus operator, grey and fixed, and by default SA with each Focus operator without SA such as Three) stay out of the queues and are not tracked, the **Hotspot distance** and **Hotspot time** are settings (250 m, the same as the Cluster distance, and 15 minutes by default), the Repeated condition reads **Cluster** (with its **Cluster size**, **Cluster period** and **Cluster distance**, ex Repeated issue, Repetition period and Spot without a cell, under **Scoring Impact, Clusters and Hotspots**) and the High and Medium shares and the cluster and hotspot settings apply at once (in **#Calls** and on Save), the panel lists the **Tracked** calls folded below the Voice and Data queues, whose tables show the Priority after the JOIN_ID, the campaign as the Campaign Maps name it and the step as a pill (such as *Step 8 · Final*), changing the strategy or a step is recorded in App Events, Save keeps the dialog open with a line that tells whether the configuration is saved (closing with unsaved changes asks to save or discard them), the levels say they are shared by every strategy, and the **NQ Prioritization & Queues** panel (**NQ Queues** in the sections bar) comes right after the Filters, with a **Strategy** selector above Recalculate and Prioritization Rules (the same users as the rules) that applies another strategy at once, below its cards four subpanels in this order (also in PowerPoint and Word): **Next to analyse** (the queues), the steps of the **Strategy** (a tab per list, **List 1 · Weekly CDRs**, **List 2 · Final CDRs** and **Outside the lists**, each step with its fields as the Prioritization Rules show them and in their colours, Priority, number, NR Mode, Operators, Call Type, Area and Condition, then its calls, their **Follow-up** (waiting, in progress and closed) in a stacked bar over its calls, the points they lose in a blue bar and its Queue; a table per tab in PowerPoint and Word), **Scoring Impact** and **Repeated issues**.
- **Modules & Sections at the left edge on large screens:** the **Modules & Sections** tab and its drawer stand at the left edge of the window on large screens (1200 px wide and more), and stay at the right edge on smaller ones.
- **Default columns of the NQ Calls table:** after the root category and cause, the workspace layout of a new workspace shows the **Prioritization** with the **Priority** below it in the same column (each name of the header sorts and filters by its own field), **Repeated Issue**, **Scoring Points Lost**, **Analysis Status** and **Implementation Status**; **Scoring Impact** is no longer a default column. The workspaces created before take the Priority as a column once.
- **NQ Calls table within the panel:** the default columns of the Non-Qualified Calls table fit the panel without scrolling sideways on screens of 1600 px and wider: the time of a call goes below its date, the Comments column shows the number of comments (the latest one on hover, without the *Add a comment* text), the selects, paddings and header names are narrower, and the filter funnel of a header stays on the line of the end of its name.
- **Tables on phones:** on phones the Datasets of Workspace Management, the Super Admin tables of users and of the Database Viewer, Datasets Management, the Reporting Jobs and their Run History, the App Events of Application Logs and the Non-Qualified Calls are tables scrolled sideways instead of one card per page, and the App Events take at most 70% of the screen, with their pages right below them.
- **Reloading Non-Qualified Calls:** as Scoring & GAP Analysis, a reload (or Back and Forward) opens the page where it was being read, showing what was in view when it was left, dimmed under a gradient with a loading notice in the colour of the module, and holds it there until the calls and every panel on screen are drawn, so the panels arriving in any order no longer move it.
- **What an import changes in the Non-Qualified Calls:** the import and transfer summaries (and their preview) of the NQ Calls Configuration list each change instead of the sections that changed: each status, team, section, field and its values, Status Rule, root cause entry and suggestion rule, layout of the workspace and of each user, equivalence of the Previous NQ Analyses and setting of the NQ Prioritization, with its values before and after. Each updated call of the follow-up of the Non-Qualified Calls (the first 300 of an import) unfolds **Details** with every value before and after, whole, and each comment added, edited or removed.

#### 🐛 Bug fixes:
- **Repeated issues of one site only:** the place of a Non-Qualified Call (its **Repetition Place**, the **Cluster** condition and the **Repeated issues**) and the site of each eNB / gNB of the Root Cause Analysis took the first row of the cell mappings with the number of the eNB or gNB of its last cell, though that number repeats across networks, so calls of distant places shared a site of another network (such as *Site ENB0 (ENB0)*, the placeholder of the H3G rows of the VFUK mapping). The site now comes from the Network Inventories and cell mappings of the workspace, in a network that serves the operator of the call (its own first, as the **Serving Networks** say) and within their maximum distance of it, from the newest snapshot that lists the node; it reads as its site ID and host network, as the site that served the start of a call (*Site 47258 (Vodafone)*), so both are the same place, and the NQ Prioritization is calculated again, also when the inventories or the Serving Networks change.

#### 📚 Documentation:

## Release: v0.8.5
### Release Date: 2026-10-22
#### ⚠️ Breaking Changes:

#### 🌟 New Features:
- **Preview before an import or transfer:** an Import ZIP and a received transfer first show what importing each package would do, without importing anything, in the words of the completion message (new, updated and unchanged items and, unfolded, the new items and what would change in each updated item, compared as the import would save them: the creator it gives, the CDRs, favourites and Dashboards of this server for the Reporting Jobs, the default of this server for the PPT Templates and what only this server has for the NQ Calls Configuration; for the App Events, how many the workspace does not have; for an Application Config, which replaces the whole application database, the users, Modules Profiles, Workspace Groups, tickets, Interface Settings and Scoring Aggregation Hierarchy it would add, update and remove), with a box for each package in two or three columns, checked only when it would change something (**Select all**, **Select none**, **Invert selection**): **Import selected** imports the checked ones, and **Cancel** (Import ZIP) or **Reject transfer** imports nothing; a rejected transfer deletes its package and tells the source, which waits for the decision. The preview, the choice and the rejection are recorded in App Events.
- **Filter Navigator for the Favourites Filters:** in Scoring & GAP Analysis, **Filter Navigator** (beside the title of the **Favourites Filters** panel, and the tree button of the Saved Filter dropdowns on top of the Calculation panel and in **Scoring Results**) shows the Favourites Filters as a tree built from their filters: Area (the Region, Cluster or City, or **Nationwide**), NR Mode (**NSA**, **SA**, **NSA vs SA**) and Campaign, with what tells apart two that would read alike; choosing one uses it. Its search keeps, as you type, those with every word typed in any order (`man sa lat 2`), keeps together words a Favourites Filter has together (`latest camp` finds *Latest campaign*, not *Latest 2 campaigns*) and marks them, and the tree is used with the keyboard too; on a phone it takes the whole screen.

#### 🚀 Enhancements:
- **Points lost by kind, with what each kind counts:** **Where the points go** of Scoring & GAP Analysis (on the page, in PowerPoint and Word and in the Bundle Report Templates) splits the points lost into **Not completed (NQ)** (the calls and tests whose result is not Completed, as the Non-Qualified Calls, and the Multi RAB data transfers that did not succeed), **Below the minimum** (completed under the minimum of the other Reliable KPIs, such as POLQA of 1.6 or less and the lowest throughputs), **Below the threshold** (completed above it but under the threshold of a KPI) and **Quality shortfall**, instead of Failed tests, Below the threshold and Quality shortfall, with a table of what each kind counts and the KPIs of the methodology it holds.
- **Points lost per 1,000 tests per City, Region and Cluster:** **Rank by** in the Points Lost Map ranks the places, and colours the map, by their National points lost or by the points they lose per 1,000 of their Voice calls and Data tests (the places with 500 tests or more), so a place does not rank high only because it was measured more; the ranking shows both figures, and in PowerPoint and Word each Points Lost per City, Region or Cluster slide is followed by its per 1,000 tests slide. Saved Filters calculated before have to be calculated again to count the tests of each place.
- **Primary bandwidth in Network Insights:** the bandwidth ladders, the spread of the bandwidth and the location strips of the Key Findings and the Spectrum (on the page and in PowerPoint and Word) are titled and labelled **Primary bandwidth** instead of total bandwidth, and say that the CDRs report only the NR primary cell, so NR carrier aggregation is not included.
- **Exclusions on the covers of Bundle Report Templates:** a common filter that only leaves values out, such as Operators `-Three`, shows as *All except Three* on the covers.
- **Area and operator labels:** the labels of the Reporting Jobs and of the cards of the Favourites Filters of Scoring & GAP Analysis show first the Region, Cluster or City they filter, or **Nationwide** when none, then the Operator or Vendor (for example **London** and **-Three**, every operator but Three), also when they filter several of these fields; in the Reporting Jobs and the Run History the name of the job stands out in bold below its labels.
- **Modules & Sections at the right edge:** the **Modules & Sections** tab and its drawer move to the right edge of the window, on every screen, and open above every floating card. The red card of the unassigned values moves to the bottom centre and, with task cards on screen, takes the middle of their row, between those of other workspaces and of the open workspace and as wide as them (above the system task cards when there are any; on a phone the row takes the whole width and the button back to the top rises above it), instead of covering them; the cards of the open and of other workspaces no longer overlap each other on a phone.
- **Menu on small screens:** on phones (up to 640 px wide, and in landscape) a bar with the module open and a **Menu** button replaces the two rows of tabs and stays at the top of the window while scrolling, with the sections bar below it; **Menu** opens on the whole screen the tree of Modules & Sections (Main Modules, Administrative Modules and Documentation with their sections, the module open marked) and **About**.
- **Column filters, 10 rows and fixed headers in Reporting Jobs and Run History:** every column of both tables but Actions filters its rows as in Excel (Artifacts by module, and the dates by year, month and day whatever their time), the filters kept in the browser for each workspace and user, a count of the rows they leave as you search and of the rows shown, with **Select all**, **Move Up** and **Move Down** acting on the rows shown; each table shows up to 10 rows and scrolls for the rest, with a floating arrow while rows are hidden below, and while the page scrolls the bar of its buttons and its header stay at the top.
- **Rich texts written as they read:** the descriptions and comments of Ticket Center and the long texts and comments of Non-Qualified Calls are written in Markdown and read the same in their editor as on the page: headings (`#` to `######`), bullets and numbered lists at any level (`1.`, `a)`, `1.1.`), tasks, bold, italic, strikethrough, highlight, code, links, cards of Notes, Tips, Warnings, Errors and the like (`> [!TIP]`), quotes, code blocks and dividers, and a short line followed by a list is a title, as one ending with a colon. The editor has a toolbar with its keys, gives the Markdown its format as it is typed, goes on with a list at its level on Enter (Tab and Shift+Tab change the level), pastes and copies Markdown, undoes and redoes, and can edit the Markdown itself; the Comments column of Non-Qualified Calls shows the latest comment without its Markdown.
- **Going through the slides of the Presentations:** in the slide viewer of the Help, every control stands in one row below the slide: the zoom buttons and **▶ Slideshow** (its settings open above it), the previous (slate) and next (blue) slide as arrows as large as **Close** and **Download PowerPoint**, and these; the wheel and the up and down arrows also change slide (the arrows in the slideshow too), while Ctrl/Cmd+wheel zooms.
- **Slides at the left of View Dashboard:** the viewer of PPT Dashboards lists every slide in small at its left, as the presentations of the Help (the covers and transitions as in the viewer and the charts of the other slides drawn once calculated, with their number and title; on wide screens), marks the slide shown and shows the one chosen; **Presentation** and **First / Previous / Next / Last** stand below the slide and its comments, and, as in the presentations of the Help, the keys (← ↑ Page Up, → ↓ Page Down or Space, Home and End) and the wheel over the slide move through the slides, while Ctrl/Cmd+wheel zooms the chart under the pointer. The red card of the unassigned values stays beneath an open dialog or viewer.
- **Cell Assistance applies what is chosen:** in the Report Template editor, a value chosen in Cell Assistance (Layout, Chart type, Source Dataset, KPI and its operation, positions, formats, Rows Split, Columns Split, Legend…) is written to the cell at once, without **Apply selected values**, and **Close** at its bottom closes it; only the Filter Builder keeps a button, **Apply filter**, which writes its conditions to the cell once the filter is complete. Opening the KPI operation list no longer closes Cell Assistance without applying the operation.
- **What an import or transfer changed:** every package of the completion message of an import or transfer follows the same pattern, `Imported 32 Reporting Jobs into 1 workspace: 2 new, 1 updated and 29 unchanged.` (also PPT Templates, which said only *imported successfully*, Dashboards, Users & Access and Ticket Center), and an item counts as updated only when one of its values really changed (not when and by whom it was saved, also inside its rows, as the Map Areas of Mappings & Reference Data and the personal layouts of the NQ Calls Configuration, which an import of the same package saved again with that time); the summary window lists, for each element and workspace, each updated item with what changed in it (for example `Role: user-editor → user-viewer`). What was added stands out in green (`2 new`) and what was updated in amber (`1 updated`), also in the titles of the sections of the updated items.
- **Tabs of a Non-Qualified Call as the steps of its analysis:** the tabs of the call panel are raised buttons in the colour of their section, numbered in the order a call is analysed (1 Activity, 2 RCA, 3 Failure Details, 4 Analysis, 5 Implementation & Planning) and joined by a line, the open one tinted in its colour; **Call Details**, a reference, closes the row after a separator. The panel is a little wider so every tab fits on one line.
- **Changes of a Non-Qualified Call never lost without asking:** leaving a tab with fields changed (or, in Activity, a comment not posted or being edited), closing the call panel with **×**, Escape or a click outside it, and going to another call ask first what to do with the changes: **Save**, **Discard** or **Continue Editing**.
- **Whole databases marked in Export / Transfer:** in the content selector, Application Config, each Full Workspace and Full Environment carry an amber warning tag (**Whole application database**, **Whole workspace database**, **Whole application and workspace databases**), since importing them replaces those databases instead of merging their items.

#### 🐛 Bug fixes:
- **Bundle Reports generated again after an update:** the sections of the Bundle Report Templates, in the exports and in the Reporting Jobs, are no longer reused from an export made with an earlier version of the application, which kept the slides it generated (such as those of Scoring & GAP Analysis) for up to a day after an update; the run of a Reporting Job lists in its details the sections it reused from an earlier export.
- **Super Admin and Ticket Center in their Help groups:** the Help Navigation lists Super Admin under Administrative Modules and Ticket Center under Main Modules, as the Help menu of the tabs, instead of under General.
- **Structural slides by their layout and End Slides in the Report Templates:** a row without Source Dataset or Chart type takes the structural type of its layout (**Title Page** a Title Slide, **Transition** and **Title Only** a Transition Slide, **Black logo end slide** an **End Slide**, a new structural Chart type, also when the row still says Title Slide or Transition Slide) and saves with it, instead of asking for a CDR chart; an End Slide needs no Slide Tittle or Slide Subtittle, the CDR Reports and the Dashboard PPTs add it as the closing slide of the template, without counter, and the PPT Dashboards viewer shows it as that slide. Every validation error of a row names it as the editor shows it, `Slide: n - Chart: n` (or `Slide: n` for a structural slide), instead of its row in the CSV.
- **Exclusions in the Operator filters:** a pattern such as `-Three` added to an Operator filter with every operator ticked chooses on its own (every operator but Three), the list shows it as **All except Three** instead of **All values**, and in PPT Dashboards it changes the filter and enables **Save Filters**, instead of being taken as every value and dropped. The PPT Dashboards with a pattern in their Operator filter (`-Three`, `VF*`, `NOT Leb*`) show the rows of the operators it takes, matched by their spelling in the CDRs or their label in the Operator Maps (`-Three` also leaves out the `3` of the CDRs), instead of no rows; their prepared data is calculated again.
- **Save Filters leaves nothing pending in PPT Dashboards:** after **Save Filters**, closing the filters, opening the viewer or leaving the page no longer warns about unsaved or unapplied changes, and the **Unapplied filters** badge and the yellow filters no longer show while the saved filters are being applied. **Apply Filters** and **Save Filters** are enabled as soon as a filter changes, also while the Dashboard is being prepared (when it opens, after its NR Mode changes or while other filters are applied), instead of waiting for the preparation to end.
- **The Saved Filter chosen stays in use:** choosing a Saved Filter in Scoring & GAP Analysis (**Use Filter**, the Filter Navigator, editing one or cancelling the editing) no longer leaves the selectors saying *No Saved Filter matches the Calculation panel's filters* when the lists of the Calculation panel were not ready yet, as right after opening the page: the panel keeps its Campaign choice (such as Latest 2 campaigns) and its filters until the lists of its NR Mode arrive, instead of dropping them and saving the selection without them.

#### 📚 Documentation:
- **Presentations first in Help Home and Product Overview:** the Presentations of the tool open Help Home and Product Overview, before their index (In this guide), with a shorter description and a bullet with what each presentation holds.

---

## Release: v0.8.4
### Release Date: 2026-10-21
#### ⚠️ Breaking Changes:
- **Imports and transfers add Dashboards, PPT Templates, Auto-calculated Fields and Methodologies:** an Import or Transfer of Dashboards, PPT Templates, Auto-calculated Fields or Scoring & GAP Analysis Methodologies replaces the items the destination workspace has and adds the others, instead of replacing the whole collection, so those only the destination has are kept (a PPT Template used by one of its Dashboards keeps its definition unless the Dashboards come too, and the default template and default methodology stay), and so does a backup restore of them. Imports and transfers of Scoring & GAP Analysis Methodologies packages made before no longer restore the Saved Filters and report configurations they carried (backups made before still do).

#### 🌟 New Features:
- **Light backups, backups every 4 hours and backups kept by days, weeks and months:** Scheduled backups can run **Every 4 hours** (from the hour of the execution time), and hourly, every 4 hours and daily schedules can make light backups (without the workspace databases, Input and Output) between full ones made once a week on a chosen day (a full one missed is made at the next backup). Full backups go to the `full` folder of the backup folder and light ones to its `light` folder, and the backups made before, all full, are moved to `full` when the application starts. Instead of a number of backups, the schedule keeps every scheduled backup of the last **Days to keep** days and the newest full backup of each of the last **Weeks to keep** weeks and **Months to keep** months (7, 4 and 6 by default; schedules saved before keep the days their number of backups covered), deleting the other scheduled backups (recorded in App Events) and no longer those made with **Backup Now**. The tiles of Backup Protection say how many backups, and how much space, are full and light, and whether the last and the next backup are full or light. The panel explains what the days, weeks and months kept keep as the schedule is set, with the dates kept today and the most light and full backups kept at the same time with the space they take. **Backup Now** asks whether the backup is full or light, and **Restore** chooses between **Full backups** and **Light backups** and lists the backups of that type, saying which ones Backup Now made.
- **Charts and tables as images in every module:** the panels of Network Insights and Non-Qualified Calls with their charts, maps and tables, the chart of Chart Builder, the slides of the PPT Dashboards viewer with their charts, the Charts Panel with its thumbnails, and the larger view of a chart, map or table opened with a double-click (also in Scoring & GAP Analysis) have the floating download and screenshot buttons of the Scoring & GAP Analysis results, which work as there: blue for the whole card and light blue for its chart or table, clear of the buttons of the page, PNG images as shown (maps with their tiles) and every download or copy recorded in App Events.
- **NQ Prioritization of the Non-Qualified Calls:** a new **NQ Prioritization** panel (and **Priorities** in the sections bar) tells which calls to analyse first. Each call gets its **Scoring Impact**: the points of the scoring of its campaign it loses (each KPI that counts tests shares the points the operator loses in the environment of the call among the tests that miss it, from the Scoring History, with the methodology of the campaign), High, Medium or Low by the share of the points of its campaign, operator and service it holds, or Not scored / Excluded from Scoring. It also gets its **Repeated Issue**: the Non-Qualified Calls of its operator at the same place (the site of the cell where it ended, else the site of its start, its eNB / gNB or a spot) in its quarter and the one before, in any service and NR Mode. Ordered **Prioritization Rules** (NQ Analysis Configuration; admins and super-admins) by Service, NR Mode, Operator, Region and City prioritize every call, the repeated or high impact ones (and other criteria) or only track them, and fill the **Priority** of the open calls while nobody chose it by hand; by default SA Voice P1, SA Data repeated or high impact P2, NSA Voice P3 and NSA Data repeated or high impact P4, the other Data calls tracked with P5. The panel shows the calls of each rule, the scoring points lost by KPI, the repeated issues and the calls **Next to analyse**, all drilling down into the calls, and is in the PowerPoint, Word, Reporting Jobs and Bundle Report Templates. Its values are fields of the calls (columns, filters, Summary breakdowns and Excel), are calculated in the background when the server is idle, and its rules travel with NQ Calls Configuration and NQ Call Tracking.

#### 🚀 Enhancements:
- **Renamed items and their creators in Import / Export and Transfer:** Dashboards, PPT Templates, Auto-calculated Fields, Query Builder Queries, Network Insights Favourites, Bundle Report Template Favourites, Scoring & GAP Report Templates, Scoring Excluded Calls, Reporting Jobs, the workspaces of Full Workspace and Full Environment packages and the users, Modules Profiles and Workspace Groups of Users & Access travel with an identity (also in backups, and the workspaces also when a transfer checks whether the destination holds the Input and Output files it does not send), so an import, transfer or backup restore replaces the item of the destination with that identity (or else with its name) and one renamed on the source server is renamed there instead of being added twice, its new name reaching the Dashboards, PPT Templates, CDR tables, saved Query Builder queries and Reporting Jobs that use it (`Name (2)` when another item has that name); the completion message and its App Event list them. Each Dashboard refers to its PPT Template by the template's identity, so a renamed template stays its template, and renaming an Auto-calculated Field in the Workspace also renames it in the SQL of the saved Query Builder queries. Every imported item belongs to its creator on the source server when that user is active in the destination, otherwise to the user who accepts the transfer or imports the package, and a Favourites Filter with the same selection as one of the destination replaces its name and creator (and adds its star).
- **PPT Templates with the PPT Dashboards in Export and backups:** the **PPT Templates** element of Admin → Import / Export / Transfer and the PPT Templates content of Super Admin backups are listed in the **PPT Dashboards** group, beside the Dashboards that use them, instead of Workspace Config.
- **Faster Favourites Filters imports with their progress:** an import, transfer or restore of Scoring & GAP Favourites Filters prepares only the filters of its package, reading the methodologies, CDRs, catalogues and campaigns they share once, and finds their CDRs by the hash it already knows of each file, also when the file only moved (a renamed or duplicated workspace) or arrived with an import or transfer of its workspace, instead of reading it again. In an import or transfer of several elements the bar moves within each element, saying what it does (for example the CDR it identifies or the Favourites Filter it prepares).
- **What an export package is being made of:** while the source of a transfer, or **Export ZIP**, creates the package, its dialog says which part it is archiving (the application database, the database with its size, settings, Input and Output files of each workspace, or each element of a selection by its position), and the copy of each database counts in the progress bar, which no longer stays still while a large database is copied.
- **Room checked before each backup:** a backup is not started when its folder has no room for the snapshot of its largest database and its ZIP, counting on macOS what the system frees on demand (a scheduled one with light backups on is made light instead); the reason shows in Backup Protection until a scheduled backup is made as configured, and in App Events.
- **Leftovers of interrupted work removed every night:** once a night between 00:00 and 07:00, with nobody using the application and no long work running (or at the next idle moment once two days pass without a cleanup), the application removes the snapshots and partial ZIPs of backups killed mid-write, the temporary folders of imports, exports, transfers and restores, the CDR uploads never finished after a week and the temporary files of the workspace caches, all untouched for six hours, and each backup first removes what an interrupted one left in its folder; App Events records what was removed and the space freed.
- **Readable import and transfer summaries:** the message at the end of an import or transfer (and on the source server of a transfer) opens in a wide window with what was imported and, for each element and workspace, a table of the items renamed, before and after, instead of one paragraph.
- **Run, Stop and Download for the checked Reporting Jobs and runs:** the bar of the checked Reporting Jobs also runs them, stops their queued or running runs (a job with one also has **Stop** in its row) and downloads the artifacts of their last runs in one ZIP, and that of the Run History downloads the checked runs; each button acts only when every checked job or run has that button on in its row. These bars and that of the Favourites Filters of Scoring & GAP Analysis are grouped like the buttons of each row, with an icon each.
- **DT Analyzer Bundle Report files:** the files a Reporting Job generates from a favourite Bundle Report Template are named `<time> - DT Analyzer Bundle Report - <name>`.
- **What the application does while it starts:** the startup page says the step being done while the active workspace opens (checking its database, its Output folder, the Scoring calculations and jobs left unfinished, the PPT Templates, the starter content and the creators, and the CDRs), and says that the workspace database is upgraded, which can take a few minutes, only at the first start of another version.
- **One campaign in Network Insights:** the **Campaign** of Network Insights is a simple selector of one campaign, **Latest campaign** by default, with **Previous campaign** and each campaign (no more Latest 2, 3 or 4 campaigns or All available campaigns); its sections of Bundle Report Templates and entries of Reporting Jobs choose one campaign the same way, and selections, favourites, sections and entries saved with several keep the newest one (Latest campaign for the other choices).
- **Access requests approved before the activation:** the email of **Request Activation** on the login page goes to the administrator with the approvers in copy, and asks one of them, in an **APPROVAL NEEDED** block framed by lines, to approve the request by replying to all and then, apart, the administrator to activate the user in Super Admin › Users once it is approved.
- **Created vs resolved by period in Ticket Center:** the chart of the tickets created and resolved of the Ticket Center dashboard is shown **Daily** (the last 30 days), **Weekly** (the last 12 weeks, by default), **Monthly** (the last 12 months), **Quarterly** (the last 8 quarters) or **Yearly** (the last 5 years), chosen at its top right, and the browser remembers the choice.
- **Exclude values in the App Events filters:** each filter of App Events (Date, Triggered by, Executed by, Type and Action) has **Include** and **Exclude** at the top of its list: Exclude shows every event except those of the values ticked, crossed out in red, also the events of values that appear later (the button reads, in red, for example `All except login` or `2/9 excluded`). The values left out stay in their list while the other filters change, and the browser remembers each filter's mode with its values until **Clear Filters**.

#### 🐛 Bug fixes:
- **Incoming transfer contents in lower case:** the window that asks to accept an incoming transfer names Ticket Center, Users & Access and the Application database with their titles, as it does the other elements, instead of in lower case.
- **Progress of the Reporting Jobs of a Bundle Report Template:** while a Reporting Job generates a favourite Bundle Report Template, its run moves its progress and says what the report is doing (for example `Generating Bundle Report Template (1/1): Composing the PowerPoint`), instead of staying at 1% until it finishes.

#### 📚 Documentation:
- **Presentations in the Help:** the Help Home lists the presentations of DriveTest Analyzer in Ericsson style, the **Overview** deck (the tool, the teams it serves and its platform, its eight modules with a slide for each one, two for Scoring & GAP Analysis, in the order of their tabs, and its roadmap, closing with the one-slider), the **one-slider** and the **roadmap** (also in the Roadmap chapter), each opening in a slide viewer like the preview of the Bundle Report Templates (sections and slides on the left, zoom with the wheel, a rectangle or the buttons, arrows to go through them) with **Download PowerPoint** and a full-screen **Slideshow** with the transitions of the presentation mode of PPT Dashboards. Their figures are live: those of the workspaces chosen in the new **Presentations** panel of Super Admin, added up, and when they change the server generates the presentations again in the background (`tools/presentations`, included in the Docker image) while the Help shows the last ones.

---

## Release: v0.8.3
### Release Date: 2026-10-21
#### ⚠️ Breaking Changes:
- **Dataset uploads, In Combined and the NQ Analysis Configuration only for admins:** only admins and super-admins upload datasets to a workspace (the other roles see a card in **Data Ingestion** that says so, and the server rejects their uploads), change the **In Combined?** choice of a CDR, which recalculates the combined tables and indexes the calls again (locked for the other roles, with the reason as its tooltip), and change the NQ Analysis Configuration of Non-Qualified Calls (Fields & Sections, Statuses & Teams, Status Rules, the Root Cause Catalog, the Ericsson RCA results and Analysts / Reviewers → Asignees); the other roles, also `user-viewer`, open every option to see it from the button beside the sections bar or the sections menu, also Previous NQ Analyses (whose template and equivalences they download), under a card that says that only admins and super-admins can change it, with its controls locked, and `user-editor` accounts keep following up the calls.
- **Super Admin module:** a new **Super Admin** tab, for super-admins only, holds **Users**, **Modules Profiles**, **Workspace Groups**, **Interface Settings** and **Database Management** (with Backup Protection and the Database Viewer) and **Reset Application**, which leave **Admin**; Admin keeps **Import / Export / Transfer** and **Datasets Management** for admins and super-admins, and only super-admins import packages (for admins the Import card says so). The access requests, their email and the Help (a new **Super Admin** chapter) point to Super Admin, and App Events records its actions under it.
- **Shared items deleted only by admins or their creator:** PPT Dashboards, PPT Templates, Scoring Saved Filters, Scoring Methodologies, the saved configurations of the Scoring & GAP Analysis report editor, Reporting Jobs and favourite Bundle Report Templates are deleted by admins and super-admins and, for the other roles, only by the user who created them (a Saved Filter by the user who starred it, and only until then by the user who calculated it last, and only they, admins and super-admins unstar it); their **Delete** buttons are disabled for the other users with a tooltip that says why, the server rejects their deletions (also a bulk delete of Saved Filters or Reporting Jobs with one of another user, which deletes none of them, or an import or save that would remove a methodology or report configuration they cannot delete), and each time a workspace opens the items without a known creator (such as those created before this version) or created by the application (`system`) are assigned to `super`, the default super-admin, so only admins and super-admins delete them (recorded in App Events).

#### 🌟 New Features:
- **Table layouts in Non-Qualified Calls:** the columns of the Calls table, those of the Excel export (each field of the Analysis Center now with **Table** and **Excel** in **Columns**, which Fields & Sections no longer shows) and the column order make a layout, and **Layout**, a switch beside Columns, shows **Mine** (to the left), only for the user, or **Workspace** (to the right), what every user sees. Every role arranges their own (Columns, dragging a header or Default column order, kept with the workspace instead of in each browser, whose order becomes the user's own once), **Copy Workspace Layout** starts it again from the workspace one, and admins and super-admins make theirs that of the workspace with **Save for the workspace**; Export Excel follows the layout shown. The layouts are listed as **NQ User Table Layouts** in Database Management, every change is recorded in App Events, and they travel with NQ Call Tracking and NQ Calls Configuration, whose imports now take the workspace layout of the package (with its CDR columns for Excel only and its column order), the layout of each user by username, the section, order, Table column, Excel and Summary breakdown of its fields, its suggestion rule and its Require setting, also where the destination had its own.
- **CDR Analysis in the Bundle Report Templates:** the Bundle Report Template editor has a fifth section, **CDR Analysis** (last by default), whose sub-modules are the CDR types (**CDR Data**, **CDR Voice** and **CDR Speech**, checked and ordered by dragging) with the parts of the analysis of each CDR as their charts & tables (**CDR Summary**, **Selected Metric Cards**, **Visual Analytics of each metric** and **Processed Metrics**), and whose options are the **Global Comparison**, the **Global CDF Comparison** and the metrics of each CDR type (all by default), as the CDR Analysis artifact of a Reporting Job. Its Campaigns choose the CDRs of the NR Mode of the report in the combined CDR tables, the common filters filter them, each CDR starts with a cover named after it, and it uses the theme of the report; it is reused as the other sections while its CDRs and options do not change. Favourites saved before keep it unchecked, so they generate what they did, and it travels with them in exports, transfers and backups.

#### 🚀 Enhancements:
- **Move Up and Move Down in the Favourites Filters:** the **Favourites Filters** panel of Scoring & GAP Analysis has **Move Up** and **Move Down** buttons before **Use newer CDRs** that move every checked Saved Filter one place up or down in the list (those side by side together), keeping the order for every user of the workspace as dragging does and recording it in App Events; readers do not see them. The moved cards light up in amber for a moment where they land, the page follows the one leading the way, and the bar of Select all to Delete stays at the top of the window while the panel is scrolled.
- **Rows per page in Non-Qualified Calls:** the page size of the Calls table is called **Rows per page** and is as tall as the buttons beside it.
- **Application starts in App Events:** every start of the application writes an `application_started` event by `system` in every workspace (the active one first, the others once the application is ready), shown as **Server** tagged *Startup*, with the version, the active workspace, how long it took to be ready, the address it listens on, the Python version, the platform and the process ID, and as an Error with the reason when the active workspace could not be opened.
- **In Use on the Favourites Filters:** the card of the Saved Filter in use (selected at the top of Scoring & GAP Analysis, with its results shown) has an outlined **In Use** label in place of **Use Filter**, in the Favourites Filters panel and in the Scoring Results dropdowns.
- **Total rows in Points Lost and the KPI GAP Profile:** the tables of Points lost per KPI, the KPI GAP Profile, the Points Lost map, Points lost per Vendor and per kind of serving site, Sites to review and Route stretches close with a total row (the map, the sites and the stretches with the total of the rows listed), as GAP by part, on the page and in PowerPoint and Word; in the Vodafone theme the total rows of the Scoring, GAP and insight tables keep their label on a dark grey pill and put each figure on a white pill ringed in that grey, so the red and teal GAPs read clearly.
- **Labels of the Reporting Jobs:** the name of each job in the **Reporting Jobs** table starts with the labels of the Favourites Filters of Scoring & GAP Analysis: the only Region, Cluster, City, Operator or Vendor its artifacts filter, in the colour of the field and on one line (for example **London** or **Vendor A, VMO2 North, VMO2 South**), or **Nationwide** when they filter none of them (as the cards of the Favourites Filters now read in place of **All**), once each when they filter different ones; below them, always on one line, the modules it generates artifacts of, abbreviated (**SCO**, **NI**, **NQ**, **PPT** and **CDR**, those of the favourite Bundle Report Templates it generates included) in the colours of their artifact panels, and after a separator its NR Modes; and then its campaigns, each choice once after the modules that make it when they choose differently (for example **SCO · PPT: Latest 2 campaigns**). A job of a favourite Bundle Report Template takes the common filters and the campaigns of the sections of the favourite. The Name column is wider, and Artifacts takes the room left by the actions, now icon buttons in one light toolbar, and by Next Run and Last Run, whose times go below their dates.
- **Several Reporting Jobs at once:** editors have a tab on the left of the name of each job, as the cards of the Favourites Filters of Scoring & GAP Analysis, to drag it by and with a box on top to check it; with the checked jobs, or **Select all**, in a bar above the **Reporting Jobs** table that stays at the top while the table is scrolled, **Move Up** and **Move Down** move every checked job one place (checked jobs side by side move together and light up where they land) and **Delete (N)** deletes them together after one confirmation that names them, each with its runs and their artifacts and recorded in App Events as its own delete button would.
- **Run History of the Reporting Jobs:** each run has the labels of its job above its name, as in the **Reporting Jobs** table; **■ Stop** stops a queued run before it starts or a running one after the step it is on (keeping what it generated, without its email), **↻ Rerun** runs a failed, interrupted or stopped run again from the beginning in its own row, and the box before each run (or **Select all**) checks several to rerun, stop or delete them together from the bar above the table, each action recorded in App Events; the actions of a run sit in the same light toolbar of icon buttons as those of the jobs. A run interrupted by an application restart goes on from the step it was on when the application starts again, instead of failing (after more than three restarts it fails). **Queued** and **Stopping** runs have labels of their own, apart from the blue one of **Running**, and the table shows the latest 10 runs, with the older ones a vertical scroll away under its header.

#### 🐛 Bug fixes:
- **Buttons hidden in Non-Qualified Calls:** **Default column order** shows only when the column order differs from the default one, and the other buttons the page hides stay hidden.
- **Points lost of All Environments with scaled environments:** when environments without results in any series are scaled out of All Environments (for example City: London, measured only in Drive - City), the points lost per KPI, the KPI GAP Profile, the Points Lost maps, Points lost per Vendor and per kind of serving site, Sites to review, Route stretches and Where to act count in the scaled points of All Environments, so every panel gives each operator the points lost of Where the points go and of its score (49.0 instead of 31.8), on the page and in PowerPoint and Word.
- **Scaled results told once:** a calculation with a methodology per campaign shows the **Scaled results** notice once when every methodology scales the same way, instead of once per methodology, also for results saved before and in PowerPoint and Word; when they scale differently, each notice starts with its methodology and campaigns.
