## Release: v0.7.1
### Release Date: 2026-10-15
#### ⚠️ Breaking Changes:

#### 🌟 New Features:
- **Cell Configuration KMZ inventories:** Network Inventories also accept Google Earth `KMZ` and `KML` files, alone or in a `ZIP` package (with folders or nested ZIP files), whose Placemarks describe one cell each: every cell becomes a row with its Technology, source file and folder, keys spelt differently in LTE and NR files share one column, and the site points and sector shapes are left out. A Network Inventory without an Operator is **Multi-operator (Network column)** and takes the owner of each cell from its Network column.
- **Inventory snapshots:** every Multivendor Mapping and Network Inventory is a snapshot of the network on its **Snapshot** date, chosen when it is uploaded (filled in with the date of its file name) and shown and changed in its row of the Network Inventory & Vendor Mappings card (the date in its file name or its upload date until one is chosen), and its cells are normalised once into the new **Inventory Cells** table (with **Inventory Snapshots**, both in Database Management): technology, owner Network and Host Network kept apart, vendor, site, cell identity, band, channel, PCI, position (the British National Grid position when the coordinates are rounded), azimuth, configuration and status, so Network Insights and the Vendor mapping change snapshot without reading the inventories again.
- **Serving-cell Vendor mapping:** each sample takes the Vendor of the cells that served it among the networks allowed to serve its Operator (the new **Serving Networks** of Workspace Config, which lists every Operator with its serving networks to add, remove or return to the defaults by clicking them: by default Vodafone and 3 serve each other through eMOCN, Lebara is served by both and every other Operator by its own network), so 3 samples on Vodafone cells, Vodafone samples on cells hosted by O2 and O2 samples on cells hosted by Vodafone get their vendor. Every selected Multivendor Mapping and Network Inventory is pooled as a snapshot; a cell in several snapshots takes the one closest to the date the CDR was measured, and a cell farther than the maximum serving distance (30 km by default) from the sample is ignored. A sample none of whose cells is in the snapshots is `<Operator>_Unknown`, as with Vendor polygons, instead of Mixed. The mapped CDRs also receive `Serving_Network`, `Serving_Host_Network`, `Serving_Site_ID`, `Serving_eMOCN_Scenario`, `Serving_Distance_km` and `Serving_Inventory_Snapshot`, and the Map dialog marks the CDRs mapped under another rule, other Serving Networks or other snapshots as **Vendor mapping outdated** and selects them. The Serving Networks travel with Mappings & Reference Data.
- **Network Insights on inventory snapshots:** an **Inventory snapshot** selector (the newest snapshot of each network, or one snapshot for the networks it covers) and four new or rebuilt panels. **Serving Cells** finds each sample's serving cell among the networks the Serving Networks of Workspace Config allow to serve its Operator and shows, per group, the cells found, the median and P90 distance to the serving site, the overshooting share beyond a chosen distance, the share hosted by another network, the distance CDF, the breakdown by band, vendor and host network, the overshooting cells, the NR cell of the NSA samples (the cell with their NR PCI whose band and carrier hold their SSB frequency: on the LTE anchor's site, else the nearest one, marked when ambiguous) with its vendor and distance, and the MIMO of the serving cells (TXRX mode or TX antennas) against the rank and maximum MIMO layers the samples reported. **5G Availability** measures the samples anchored on LTE cells of sites with NR that measured no NR and lists those sites, and for the samples that reached NR whether their NR cell was found, on the anchor's site and how far, with the NR sites that served the most samples. **Network Snapshot** shows the snapshot of each network, the owner × host network matrix, the inventory quality and the changes from another snapshot (cells added and removed and vendor swaps by region). **Network Deployment** counts the sites and cells of each owner network of the snapshot (Vodafone, 3 and O2 from one Cell Configuration package) by owner or host network, vendor, technology, band, eMOCN scenario, site type, region, subregion, cluster, city, urban/rural, performance polygon, status or MIMO, for LTE, NR or both and in service only, with CSV exports; **Cluster Sites Density** calculates the sites per km² and the inter-site distance per cluster or region, and **Spectrum** adds the carriers and MHz deployed per band. SA serving cells (`5G NR` traces) are recognised in the observed sites and cells. The coverage and interference maps draw the sites around the weakest and most interfered areas as one wedge per cell in the colour of its vendor, with a ring around the sites whose cells the snapshot shows locked, halted, disabled, planned or in progress, listed in a table below the maps with their nearest weak area, and the PowerPoint and Word summaries include every new panel.
- **Notices for super-admins:** after signing in and while signed in (checked with the poll of incoming server transfers, every few seconds), a super-admin is told in a dialog, like that of an incoming server transfer, of the access requests waiting for activation and of the bug reports still New that other users reported, on whatever page is open, with **Open Access Requests** (Admin → Users with the new **Pending access requests** status), **Open Bugs Report** (the New reports) and **Close**. Each super-admin is told of each one once (a later request of the same user is told again, and an opened bug report is not told), and the open dialog takes in what arrives meanwhile; what each one was told of is kept in the new **Admin Notices Seen** table of the application database and recorded in the App Events (executed by the module of the page), and Users & Access carries the pending request of each inactive user.

#### 🚀 Enhancements:
- **Map CDR fields dialog:** the mappings to apply come right after the CDRs, in Vendor, Region and Cluster cards, and the dialog fits the window: only the CDR lists scroll. The Vendor card offers the **Cell inventories** (the source formerly called Network Inventory) or the Vendor polygons, and lists the Vodafone UK and Three UK Multivendor Mappings and the Cell Configuration and other Network Inventories, each with its networks and snapshot date. Shorter rules follow under **How each mapping works**, each below its card, and the Cancel and Map buttons, with the number of selected CDRs, stay visible at the bottom of the Map, Clear Mappings and Reprocess Datasets dialogs, which no longer scroll the page behind them.
- **Network Insights design:** every panel opens with the question it answers and explains itself in information notes; the Analysis Selection groups its controls in Scope, Thresholds and Maps & inventory, and the grouping selectors are called **Split by** (View / Split by in Network Deployment, Split sites by in the density table); the KPI cards, section headings, toolbars and export buttons share one style; numeric columns align with their headers and long tables scroll under a fixed header; the maps no longer overlap their title with an axis name and draw the inventory sites as triangles (sectors once an area is zoomed) in vendor colours outside the quality scale, with an Inventory sites check to hide them; and double-clicking any chart, map or table opens it larger, as in Scoring: every map moves by dragging, zooms with the mouse wheel or a Ctrl/Cmd+drag rectangle down to street level and measures distances and bearings with Alt/Opt+drag, with square samples, translucent sectors under them in the Vendor Maps colours (hidden per vendor or state from the legend) and the inventory sites of the whole view once close enough. Network Insights reopens with the last analysis, also after a restart (running it again when its stored result is out of date), and keeps its scroll position when reloaded. Switching the Inventory snapshot is immediate (the snapshots are prepared in the background and kept on disk with their cells) and an analysis takes about a third of the time; the Network Snapshot panel shows the snapshot in use, its cards and the density columns carry each network's colour, LTE (red) and NR (green) have their own colours in every table, and every list of operators and networks follows the order and colours of the Operator Maps. Every CDF has the P10, P50 and P90 of each group under it and ends its axis where the curves flatten, as in CDR Analysis, the RF Quality table gives its RSRP and SINR in dBm and dB, and Serving Cells adds the distance CDF per RAN vendor beside the one per group, a breakdown by serving network and marks with **No inventory** the groups whose serving networks have no cells in the snapshot. The Coverage & Interference Maps follow the Overview, on the page, in the sections menu (which now lists every panel) and in the PowerPoint and Word summaries.
- **NQ Implementation Proposals:** the Implementation Proposal field is called Implementation Proposals (workspaces that kept the former name take it once). In Lifecycle & Phases, a phase field with more than 12 values takes a row of its own below the other cards, across the panel, its values in columns from the most frequent (a slide of its own in PowerPoint and Word), and the values of a field without colours take a palette of different colours.
- **Sections bar in every module:** every page with two or more sections shows them in a floating bar in the colour of its module, as in Non-Qualified Calls: it stays at the top of the window, starts with an arrow back to the top of the page, holds every entry of the sections list of its tab (the parts of an entry, such as the Scoring Results tabs or the Mappings & Reference Data maps of Workspace Config, open below it), goes to the chosen section and marks the one being read. In Non-Qualified Calls the bar now goes to each panel, with Filters first, instead of showing one panel at a time, so All panels is gone, and the NQ Analysis Configuration beside it opens on hover, its options with the icons of the sections menu.
- **Saved Filters panel:** the Saved Filters panel of Scoring & GAP Analysis shows ten Saved Filters before it scrolls, and is only as tall as fewer ones.
- **NQ panels while loading:** every panel of Non-Qualified Calls shows an animated **Loading…** bar while it waits for its data, as Scoring & GAP Analysis while it calculates, and the chips of the active filters appear on every panel as soon as the filters change, without waiting for the data.
- **Dashboard charts in the background:** the charts of the Dashboards start rendering once nobody has used the application for two minutes, instead of five.
- **App Events details and filters:** App Events shows the newest 10,000 events instead of 1,000, 100 a page with a pager, filtered on the server and refreshed only when an event changes; the columns before Details read a little smaller, the time of each event is a green badge below its date, every badge of the table the same size, action names wrap only at their underscores, the User column and filter are called **Triggered by** and show each person by first and last name with the username as a badge below, as the filter lists them; **Executed by** always tells where the event ran: the module whose page was used, the Server (Scheduler, Background job, Idle task or Startup), a Remote server, or Public pages used without signing in (events of earlier versions take it from their action), each with its tag, and is exported with the events; the Date, Triggered by, Executed by, Type and Action filters choose several values each (a tall list, as wide as its values need, with its values close together, its search and Select All / None fixed at the top, that stays open while the table refreshes; none ticked shows every event, and filters saved with one value keep it); the Details of each event are one readable sentence, with the recorded data folded under **Raw details** instead of shown twice; each save of a user records only the fields it changed, from the previous value to the new one (or that nothing changed), creating or deleting a user records the account, and so do the changes of Modules Profiles, Workspace Groups, Interface Settings, the application configuration, email delivery, backups and Operator and Vendor Maps; events of earlier versions, which kept only what was saved, say that they did not record what changed.
- **NQ Not Attended and Stalled indicators:** the indicators of Lifecycle & Phases, on the page and in PowerPoint and Word, count the calls Not Attended (the first status, in its colour) before Open, and a click on Not Attended or on Stalled shows only those calls (Stalled with its own chip, the open calls without changes for 14 days or more from today). The Calls card of the NQ panels reads Selected Non-Qualified Calls.
- **NQ Filters panel:** the General Filters and the NQ Analysis Fields Filters each in a card of their own, the filters with a value applied in the colour of the module (their name and their list), and a **Follow-up** filter after Status Set (Attended, Not attended, Open, Stalled, Closed, With team, Assigned, Commented, With a root cause, RCA identified and RCA not identified, the calls in any of those chosen), the one the indicator cards of the panels set (Open, the attended calls not closed, for the Open cards of Lifecycle & Phases). The badge of every panel with the calls that match the filters (such as 774 Filtered Calls) is larger, filled in the colour of the module.
- **NQ Analysts and Reviewers linked to their accounts:** Analysts → Asignees, now Analysts / Reviewers → Asignees, lists every Analyst / Reviewer of the calls, one row per person with their calls as Analyst and as Reviewer, and the Analyst and the Reviewer of the calls take the first and last name of the account chosen for them, shown in a New Analyst / Reviewer column (every spelling of a person becomes one value; an account without a name leaves them as they are), besides the Asignee of the calls they analysed, with the changes counted before applying and kept in the history of each call; the calls whose CDR is not loaded yet change too, in their imported analysis.
- **Saved Filter ID:** every Saved Filter card of Scoring & GAP Analysis, and the selected one of the Scoring Results dropdown, shows its **Filter ID: N** in a badge in the bottom right corner, before its buttons.
- **Starred and unsaved filters in App Events:** the Scoring calculations and exports of App Events name their Saved Filter as `saved filter ID N (starred)` when it is starred, and as `unsaved filter ID N` otherwise.

#### 🐛 Bug fixes:
- **Vendor mapping cell identities:** the VFUK Multivendor Mapping and the Network Inventories identify each LTE cell by its ECI, `eNodeB ID × 256 + Cell ID`, and each NR cell by its NCI, `gNodeB ID × 16384 + Cell ID` (22-bit gNodeB IDs), the identities the CDRs report. The internal Local Cell ID of Huawei and Nokia cells (about 54,000 Vodafone and O2 cells) and the former `× 4096` NR rule found no cell, or a wrong one, for their samples, and the 5G sheet of the VFUK mapping now also maps the NR cells of SA CDRs; one Network Inventory can hold LTE and NR cells. Map the CDRs again to apply it. Non-Qualified Calls takes the gNB of NR cells with the same rule.
- **Operator of a Network Inventory:** changing it in the Network Inventory & Vendor Mappings card is saved again.
- **NQ calls per page:** **Rows** offers 10 (the default), 25, 50 and 100 calls a page, and the table shows that many; it showed ten calls whatever Rows said when every panel was shown.
- **NQ filters after Operator Maps changes:** an Operator or Vendor of the saved filters that the Operator Maps now show with another name (Vodafone SM, mapped to VF SM) is chosen with that name when the page opens; it was dropped from the list, leaving out its calls, and the NetCheck RCA values saved with another spelling are chosen the same way.
- **Scoring calculation progress:** the progress of a scoring calculation no longer seems stuck at 2%: it tells when it checks the Scoring History of the selected CDRs, which now takes about a fifth of the time, and that check no longer waits for other work writing to the workspace.
- **System in App Events:** every event of the application itself belongs to `system`, shown in red as **System** tagged *application* in the table and first in the Triggered by filter, with a line of its own before the default users; the table columns taken from an imported Non-Qualified Calls configuration were recorded under an `import` user, and those events move to `system` when the workspace opens, with the import in their details.
- **NQ Stalled calls:** the Stalled indicator and the Stalled Open Calls of Lifecycle & Phases count only the open calls (attended and not closed) without changes for 14 days or more, also in PowerPoint and Word; they also counted the calls Not Attended.

#### 📚 Documentation:

## Release: v0.7.0
### Release Date: 2026-10-14 (First Public Release)
#### ⚠️ Breaking Changes:
- **Modules Profiles and Workspace Groups:** what an account sees and opens comes from two independent settings that only add access: its Modules Profile (the main modules it sees; one per user) and its Workspace Groups plus the workspaces granted to it individually; super-admins open every workspace and see every module (Full Access) unless they are given a Modules Profile. They replace User Groups, Features Activation (with its Allowed and Forbidden lists) and Workspace Access, and workspaces are no longer granted to roles. Admin shows each profile and group as a card, saved at once, each profile with a colour that marks it in the Users table and Request Access, with a chip per module (with the title, icon, colour and order of its tab) or workspace and its users, several at a time, and the username badge of the header shows the Modules Profile of the account in its colour (the colour of the role for admins and super-admins); a module in no profile, as PPT Reporting (old), is seen only by the super-admins without a profile. On upgrade the module groups become profiles with the modules they gave, the other groups become Workspace Groups with their workspaces and members, every account gets the profile with the modules it saw (or with every module of its groups when it had several) and the workspaces granted to a role become grants of its accounts. A new deployment starts with the Non-Qualified Calls (Workspace, Non-Qualified Calls, Reporting and Builders), Benchmarking and All Modules (the default one) profiles, and Request Access offers the Workspace Groups and the Modules Profiles.

#### 🌟 New Features:
- **NQ Analysts → Asignees:** a dialog of the NQ Analysis Configuration menu lists every Analyst of the Non-Qualified Calls (the names written with the same letters as one analyst) with their calls, proposes the user account named as each one (by username or by first and last name) and gives it as the Asignee of their calls: only those without one unless asked to replace it, every call keeping its team (the user joins it when it has members), with the calls that change counted before applying and the change in the history of each call.
- **Users & Access in Import / Export / Transfer and Backup:** the users, with their names, passwords, roles, Modules Profiles and workspaces, and the Modules Profiles and Workspace Groups export, transfer, back up and restore on their own, apart from Application Config; importing them adds the missing users, profiles and groups and updates those with the same name, matching workspaces by name, and keeps the others and the role, status and password of the account that imports.
- **Bugs Report:** a new main module, in every Modules Profile and open without a workspace, where users report the bugs of the tool with their screenshots (pasted, dropped or chosen), the module, a priority from P1 to P5 and the version and date they were opened with, and follow them in a timeline of comments and changes, whose descriptions and comments read with nested bullets (the lines starting with -, * or ·) and the lines ending with a colon as titles, and whose header counts list the reports of each status and whose list filters them by status, priority, module and reporter; anyone with the link reads the reports and their screenshots without signing in (signing in from them returns to them), the reporter edits a report and its priority, and only super-admins manage it (status, assignment, reporter, module, the version it was opened with and the fixed version) or delete it. The reports travel with Application Config and on their own as Bugs Report in Import / Export / Transfer and Backup / Restore, where they merge without deleting anything.
- **App Events on their own:** the App Events of the active workspace export, transfer, back up and restore on their own (App Events in Import / Export / Transfer and in the Workspace Content of Backup Protection); importing or restoring them adds the events a workspace does not have, and App Events lists the events by their date, so the imported ones take their place among the others.

#### 🚀 Enhancements:
- **App Events of every user action:** App Events also record deleting a workspace (in the workspace open at that moment), cancelling an upload, Network Insights analyses, Scoring & GAP Analysis calculations, cancelling a Query Builder run, the NQ Analysis Center follow-up (changes to one call or in bulk, comments, catalog imports and exports, equivalences and Excel, PowerPoint and Word exports), stopping and deleting single Dashboard PPT and PPT Reporting (old) jobs and deleting Reporting Job runs, the downloads of generated reports, Dashboard PPTs, chart sets, Reporting Job runs and export packages, the CSV exports of dataset previews, chart data and Network Insights tables, the exports of PPT Templates, Dashboards, Scoring results and reports, methodologies and report configurations (and saving, deleting and importing these), export packages and outgoing server transfers with their outcome, importing a recovered transfer package, creating backup folders and the requests to stop background tasks.
- **NQ Analyst and Reviewer filters:** the NQ Analysis Fields Filters of Non-Qualified Calls, and the funnels of their columns, filter the calls by every Analyst and Reviewer name they hold, with Empty for the calls without one.
- **NQ Analysis Configuration:** the Analysis Center button of Non-Qualified Calls, and its entry in the sections menu of the module, is now NQ Analysis Configuration.
- **NQ filter groups:** the Filters panel of Non-Qualified Calls shows the General Filters (formerly Default Filters: the CDRs and the call) and the NQ Analysis Fields Filters (formerly Analysis Center Filters), which also gather Failure Classification, Failure Category, Root Domain, Root Category, Root Cause, Global Status, Status Set, Team and Assignee, all in the order of Fields & Sections.
- **NQ filters with the values left by the others:** each filter of Non-Qualified Calls, and the funnel of its column, lists only the values of the calls the other filters leave and those chosen in it (the CDRs, those with some of these calls), so a Campaign leaves only its operators in the Operator list.
- **NQ NetCheck RCA values written several ways:** the Failure Classification, Failure Category and Failure Subcategory that the CDRs of different campaigns write differently (RF_Problems, RF Problems, RF problems; E2E traces required, E2E Trace Required) are one value in Non-Qualified Calls, with spaces for underscores and the words and capitals most calls use, in the filters, the table, the panels and the exports.
- **Users table:** a new design that shows the name of each user above its username, ID and creation date, edited with its pencil; the Modules Profile column follows Role, Workspaces chooses the Workspace Groups and the individual workspaces of the user, a search and filters show the users of a name, role, profile, group or status, a selection bar changes the profile, groups, role or status of several users at once, and its last row creates a user.
- **Request Access:** a new design in three steps (the account, its Workspaces and its Modules Profile) with the Workspace Groups and Modules Profiles as tiles with their descriptions, workspaces and modules; it also asks for the first and last name of the user, sent in the activation email.
- **NQ assignees by name:** Non-Qualified Calls shows each assignee by the first and last name of the account (its username when it has none) in the table, the call, the bulk bar, the Assignee filter, the Progress View and Analysts → Asignees, whose lists start with the default users (super, admin and demo), tagged default user and apart from the others.
- **NQ Call Tracking by JOIN_ID:** the NQ Call Tracking packages of Import / Export / Transfer and the backups carry the `JOIN_ID` and service of each call, and their import finds the calls that have one by it, so a package still finds its calls when the identity of the calls is computed otherwise.
- **Backup Protection:** a new design with the stored backups, their size and the last and next backup above the Back up now, Restore and Scheduled backups cards; the content, workspaces and folder chosen are saved as they change for the next backups, scheduled ones included, and Restore chooses the workspaces of the backup to restore besides its parts.
- **Database Viewer:** a new design whose Table picker lists the tables of each group by kind (Users & Access, Application, Datasets, Dashboards & Templates, Scoring & GAP Analysis, Non-Qualified Calls, Reporting, Mappings & Maps, Query Builder, Workspace and the CDRs of each type), each in its colour, with a search.
- **Import / Export / Transfer, Datasets Management and Interface Settings:** a new design; Import takes the package dropped on its card or chosen in it, each dataset shows its kind and processing status below its name, and the module labels of Interface Settings turn on and off with a switch.
- **NQ long texts as bullets:** the Description, Findings, Proposed Measure and the other long texts of a Non-Qualified call read as bullets, nested as deep as each line is indented, with the lines ending with a colon as headings and the lines of dashes as dividers, until a click opens the text to edit it in the font of the page, and an empty one shows a guide in grey with how to write them until you type; the text is saved as written.
- **NQ Excel header shades:** in the Excel export of Non-Qualified Calls every field of a section has the same shade, also those from the CDR, the Ericsson RCA or derived (such as Status Set, Session Type or Last Cell ID); and the status of each block and the fields read most stand out darker than their section: the Selected Root Category and Root Cause, the Failure Comment, the Comments, the Description, Findings and Proposed Measure (these three in wider columns), and the JOIN_ID and Call Key.
- **Scoring tables with KPI values:** with **Show KPI values**, the **KPI Value** and **Score** column groups of the Scoring tables have their own header colours (slate blue and teal), the KPI values a light blue background, and thick lines delimit both blocks.
- **National & area summary unchecked by default:** the Calculation panel of Scoring & GAP Analysis opens with **Include National & area summary** unchecked, and only a Saved Filter that includes it checks it; while it is checked, the Region, Cluster and City filters show a **Locked** badge and a notice above the Context filters explains that the summary needs the scoring of the whole country, with a button that unchecks it to filter by area.
- **Users by name in every list:** the User and Executed by filters of App Events, the Users of Modules Profiles and Workspace Groups (with the names of their members), the Access of Workspace and the reporter and assignee of Bugs Report show the users by their first and last name (their username when they have none), the default users (super, admin and demo) first and tagged, with a line before the others, as Non-Qualified Calls does.

#### 🐛 Bug fixes:
- **PPT Dashboards background charts after changing workspace:** the charts of the Dashboards of a closed workspace wait until it is opened again, instead of being retried continuously while the application is idle and holding back the charts of the Dashboards of the open workspace.
- **NQ Analysis Configuration menu under the Filters:** the menu opens above the Filters panel instead of under it when a filter list is open, and opening it closes that list.
- **Backup file of Restore:** the backup file chosen in Restore stays chosen when the list of backups refreshes, every few seconds.
- **Files refused by Import:** Import says why it refuses a file (a ZIP package cut short or damaged, a JSON document other than a Scoring & GAP Analysis Configuration of at most 4 MiB, or another kind of file) instead of always naming the size limit of that JSON, and the App Events record each uploaded package and each refused file with the reason.
- **Failed sign-ins in App Events:** a failed sign-in is recorded with the `system` user and the login typed in its details (the ones recorded before show so too), instead of under the login typed, which listed it as a user in the filters.

#### 📚 Documentation:
- **Non-Qualified Calls help:** the chapter follows the scheme of the other main modules: a note to start, **Choose your task**, numbered steps for the imports and the follow-up of the calls, coloured notes, tips and warnings at the key points, tables for the filters, tabs, sources and defaults, and new **NQ Analysis Configuration**, **Exports** and **Troubleshooting** sections.

---

## Release: v0.6.1
### Release Date: 2026-10-10
#### ⚠️ Breaking Changes:
- **Points Lost Map of earlier Scoring results:** the map now colours the map areas of every country, so results calculated before this version show their cities as bubbles until they are calculated again.
- **Non-Qualified Calls identity:** each call is identified by its NetCheck `JOIN_ID` (the same in the Daily and Final CDRs; without one, as before) and Speech CDRs are listed once per call instead of once per sample. The follow-up of the indexed calls moves to their new identity once, the first time a workspace is opened, and each Speech sample keeps its own; NQ Call Tracking packages exported by earlier versions only match the calls without a `JOIN_ID`.
- **PowerPoint template:** the PowerPoint master of every report and export is `assets/powerpoint-templates/Template_01.pptx` (formerly `assets/ppt-templates/Template_CDR_analysis.pptx`); deployments with their own `APP_ASSETS_DIR` must move it there.
- **PPT naming:** E2E Dashboards is now **PPT Dashboards** (`/ppt-dashboards`, `/api/ppt-dashboards`) with its **PPT Manage Dashboards** panel, Reporting (old) is now **PPT Reporting (old)** (`/ppt-reporting-old`, `/api/ppt-reporting-old`), and Report Templates Management in Workspace Config is now **PPT Templates Management**, whose editor (**PPT Templates Editor**) the **Edit PPT Template** buttons of Reporting, PPT Dashboards and PPT Reporting (old) open; bookmarks to the former pages no longer work. Import / Export / Transfer and the backups call them **PPT Templates** too, and their export is named `<workspace>_ppt-templates_<date>.zip` like the other workspace exports; PPT Templates packages exported by earlier versions must be exported again, and a transfer that includes them needs this version on both servers. The saved Dashboards of every workspace, the Features Activation rules and the Interface Settings of both modules are renamed when the application starts or a restored or transferred workspace is opened.
- **Query Builder Queries without CDRs:** exported, transferred and backed-up Query Builder Queries carry their name, description and SQL without CDRs, which are chosen when an imported query is used (loading it keeps the CDRs chosen in the panels); the CDRs named by packages of earlier versions are left out, so importing them no longer fails when a CDR is missing in the destination.
- **NQ Analysis Center:** the Non-Qualified Calls Excel export starts with `JOIN_ID`, the Call Key and the CDR, has two header rows (section and field) and the fields grouped by section, and ends with Updated At instead of the Call Key; the root causes are chosen as a Root Category and a Root Cause of the Root Cause Catalog (the domain follows them); the first default statuses and teams are replaced by those of the meeting workbook while unchanged (Under Investigation → Under Analysis, Pending Information → Open, Resolved and Not Applicable → Closed), and the statuses chosen before stay set by hand. NQ Call Tracking packages of earlier versions are still imported.
- **Scoring Jobs are Saved Filters:** the Scoring Jobs of Scoring & GAP Analysis are now **Saved Filters** (`/api/scoring/saved-filters`, `/scoring/saved-filters/<id>/export/<kind>`, table **Scoring Saved Filters**, formerly `/api/scoring/jobs`, `/scoring/jobs/<id>/export/<kind>` and Scoring And GAP Analysis Jobs): the jobs of each workspace become Saved Filters once, the first time it is opened, those of the same selection joined with their latest result. Processing CDRs no longer calculates a default Operator scoring on its own.

#### 🌟 New Features:
- **Map Areas:** Workspace Config finds the countries of the tests of the CDRs and downloads the administrative areas of each one from geoBoundaries (with the suggested level and the licence of each level, for example municipalities in Spain or counties in the USA) or imports them from a GeoJSON or Shapefile, and the Points Lost Map colours them. The application also ships, for servers without Internet access, the ITL3 areas of the United Kingdom and the areas of Germany, France, Italy, Spain, Poland, Romania, the Netherlands, Portugal, Switzerland, the United States, Canada, Mexico, Japan, South Korea and India at the level closest in size to them (`assets/map-areas`, with the world country polygons), which a workspace can replace with its own; any other country is coloured as one area, the whole country. Repeated names take the name of their region and broken accents are repaired. They are stored in the **Map Area Layers** table and travel with the Mappings & Reference Data in Import / Export, transfers and backups. Each CDR keeps the map area of each test and its country (`Map_Area`, `Map_Area_Country`), placed in the background while the application is idle (and again after the Map Areas change), so the Points Lost Maps do not place the tests each time.
- **Final, Weekly and Daily CDRs:** every CDR is a Final CDR (delivered after its campaign) or a Weekly or Daily CDR (incremental or cumulative, received every week or day while the campaign runs), proposed from the file name on upload (a name with `Final`, or with a date range, is Final; a weekly word is Weekly; a daily word or a single date after the start of the name is Daily) and changeable in the Workspace without reprocessing. The CDR selectors of Scoring & GAP Analysis, Network Insights and PPT Dashboards highlight the Weekly and Daily CDRs as the upload does. The combined CDR tables, and with them PPT Dashboards, Network Insights, Scoring & GAP Analysis and Reporting, include the Final CDRs and the Weekly and Daily CDRs until an included Final CDR of their type and NR Mode covers their campaign; a cumulative Weekly or Daily CDR replaces the previous ones it contains in the combined tables (they stay in the Workspace), each CDR can be included or excluded by hand (**In Combined?**: Auto, Yes or No, with whether it is included and the reason) and every change, including deleting a CDR, updates the combined tables in a background job shown in CDR Tables Updates. The CDR Type and Combined choice travel with the datasets in packages, transfers and backups; earlier CDRs are Final CDRs.
- **NQ Analysis Center:** Non-Qualified Calls follows each call through six sections — General, RCA, Failure Details, Analysis, Implementation and Planning — shown as the Activity, RCA, Failure Details, Analysis, Implementation & Planning (two subpanels) and Call Details tabs of the call, each in its colour and counting the fields filled in, with a customizable field catalog (by default the 37 fields of the meeting workbook, in its order but with the NetCheck RCA before the Ericsson RCA, with the Ericsson RCA Initial Category, Initial Subcategory and Evidence and the Last Cell ID and Failure Comment of the CDR (the fields of the NetCheck RCA and of the Ericsson RCA named after their source), and those of the analyses of the previous tool: Priority (P1 to P5), shown after the NQ Call Status in the header of the call, Analyst and Reviewer beside the Asignee, Unique ID, Initial Root Category and Cause, Polygon Area, Vendor 1 and Vendor 2 when Failure, Description, Implementation Team and Planned FY) whose fields are entered by the analysts (lists chosen from coloured pickers, which also add a new value typed in their search, text, long text, number, date and Yes / No, optionally proposed from a CDR value; the Planned FY takes fiscal years only, a year being written FY27), show the follow-up, come from the CDR or from the Ericsson RCA, or are derived from the call by keywords (Session Type); every field can be a table column (every column of the table headed by the colour of its group, as in the Excel export; the Analysis Status and Implementation Status by default, the NetCheck RCA and Ericsson RCA Suggested Cause not), a Summary breakdown, required to close and exported to Excel in the catalog order. **Fields & Sections** edits the sections and fields, and **Export Catalog** / **Import Catalog** exchange the whole catalog (sections, fields, lists, root cause catalog, statuses, teams and Status Rules) as an editable workbook, also reading the field list of the meeting workbook. The **Root Cause Catalog** groups the Root Categories and Root Causes of the analysts under technical domains with keywords: each cause can be mapped to its root category (which a call without root category takes when the cause is chosen, and whose causes its lists show first), each category and cause takes its domain by hand or automatically (a cause from its category, else from the keywords of the domains), and its **Mapping** tab maps them all in one table and gives the calls with a root cause and no root category the category of their cause (**Apply to Calls**); **Ericsson RCA Results** imports the results of the Ericsson RCA by `JOIN_ID` (CSV or Excel, with preview and template), also the `RCA_Summary.csv` of its Auto RCA output (its initial category and subcategory in the new **Ericsson RCA Initial Category** and **Ericsson RCA Initial Subcategory** fields and the final ones as the suggestion, the A and B sides, the evidence in the new **Ericsson RCA Evidence** field and every measurement, the rows of a `JOIN_ID` combined), and can add the Ericsson RCA values missing from the Root Cause Catalog, and every call gets a recommended Root Category and Cause merged from the NetCheck RCA (first), the Ericsson RCA, its comments and previous decisions, with its confidence and the RCA it comes from, to accept per call or in bulk. The NQ Call Status follows customizable **Status Rules** on the phase fields until it is set by hand — an attended call is open, Under Analysis, Under Implementation or Under Planning (Open, in the pipeline and its indicator, being their sum), until it is closed, and the default rules go from the end of the pipeline to its start — (**↺ Back to automatic** returns it; **Default Rules** marks the default rules whose status no longer exists to choose it, and **Default Statuses** / **Default Teams** put back only the default statuses and teams; a status only set by the rules no longer blocks its removal), the header of each call shows its `JOIN_ID` with a copy button, the previous and next call of the list and a field to go to the call of a `JOIN_ID`, and its phases in a subpanel of their own, one card per phase from RCA Identification to Planning, and choosing an assignee brings the call to their team. A dark floating bar above the Filters, with the **Analysis Center** menu beside it, shows the **NQ Calls** table, one panel at a time (Summary, NQ Rate, RCA Sources, Root Causes, Lifecycle and Progress, each opened at the top of the page, with the number of calls that match the filters below the Collapse button of every panel) or **All panels** (every panel, the Filters too, with the active filters as removable chips at its top, followed by the calls table with ten calls a page), and the **Lifecycle & Phases** (status pipeline with the clickable rules of each status, RCA identification, phases in one row with the shares of the calls with a value unless **Show Empty Status** is checked (also in the PowerPoint, Word and Reporting artifact), the rules of the pipeline cards on one line, time in status, moves and stalled calls) and **Root Cause Sources** (coverage and agreement of the three sources, the root domains, catalog categories and own hierarchy of each one, with the root domain of each category in a tag, and the RCA → Selected matrix) panels join the page, the PowerPoint, Word and Reporting artifact; the **Root Cause Analysis** panel counts, each in its tab, the root domains, categories (with their technology, such as Poor Coverage LTE or NR) and causes of the **NetCheck RCA**, of the **Ericsson RCA** or of the **Analysts** mapped onto the catalog, shows the Root Domains by Call Type (Classic, WhatsApp and Data) and NSA vs SA as donuts under one legend with the calls on their slices and the Root Domains by Technology as bars, in the colours of the domains, without the Not classified calls unless **Show Not classified** is checked (also in the PowerPoint, Word and Reporting artifact), and filters the calls of that source; the PowerPoint, Word and Reporting artifact show the Root Cause Sources and then the Root Cause Analysis of each source with its charts drawn like the page, the source in bold in their titles and what each one shows in its subtitle (the call type and NSA vs SA donuts, the Root Domains by Technology, and the eNB / gNB with Most Non-Qualified Calls that fit), followed by the Lifecycle & Phases as the page shows them (the pipeline with the Open frame and the rules of each status, the phases in one row, the time in each status and moves, and the stalled calls) and the Progress View (its period in bold in the titles, such as Progress View · Monthly, and what each slide shows in its subtitle). The Excel export starts with `JOIN_ID`, the Call Key and the CDR (its Speech Samples, Comments and History sheets also start with the `JOIN_ID` of each call and keep the rows of each call together, by date), then has every field under its section, with two header rows coloured like the meeting workbook, the status of each block in a darker shade, the root cause decision at the end of the RCA block, the comments of each call in one cell and the time of its last update. The catalog, Status Rules and RCA results are stored in the **NQ Analysis Fields**, **NQ Root Catalog** and **NQ RCA Results** tables and the workspace settings, and travel with the NQ Call Tracking; the new **NQ Calls Configuration** component of Import/Export, transfers and backups carries only the configuration (sections, fields, statuses, teams, Status Rules, root cause catalog and table columns), without RCA results or follow-up.
- **NQ Rate by Campaign and Operator:** a management panel of Non-Qualified Calls shows, per service, how many of every call of each campaign and operator are Non-Qualified, as cards per operator (named in its Operator Maps colour) and a pastel heat map from mint to rose whose cells drill down into the calls, counting each call once in its most recent CDR and recalculated only when its own filters or the indexed CDRs change; the PowerPoint and Word exports and the Reporting artifact show it as one table with every service and All services under each operator and the overall NQ rate above it, on one slide (two, each with half of the operators or campaigns, when they are many), and its calls are stored in the **NQ Call Population** table.
- **Vendor polygons:** the new **Vendors — Geospatial** datasets give the Vendor of each area for any Operator, by an `Operator` attribute or by the Operator chosen on upload (proposed from the file name and changeable in the Geographic Datasets card). Mapping, on upload or in the Map dialog, uses the Network Inventory or the Vendor polygons (the dialog asks when the workspace has both, and its rule follows the choice): each sample takes the polygon of its Operator at its start and end positions with the Network Inventory rule, `<Operator>_Unknown` (Vendor `Unknown`) outside every polygon, and `<Operator> - All` for Operators without polygons. Region Mapping datasets are now **Regions — Geospatial**.
- **Network Inventories of any Operator:** the new **Network Inventory** type stores the cell inventory of any Operator, whatever its layout, with its Operator (proposed from the file name and changeable in the Network Inventory & Vendor Mappings card); with a Vendor column and a cell identifier (a global cell ID, or eNodeB/gNodeB ID with local cell ID) it also maps the Vendor of its Operator's CDR samples with the first and last cell rule, on upload and in the Map dialog. The Vendor Mapping & Cell Inventory card is now **Network Inventory & Vendor Mappings**.
- **Operators and Vendors found in the CDRs:** the Operator Maps and Vendor Maps of Workspace Config have two numbered, collapsible subpanels: **names found in the CDRs** lists every name of the ready CDRs with its CDRs, label and status (Unassigned, Assigned or Merges N), and each name gets its label from a list of the labels or a **+ New label…**, so names are added up as one only when they are given the same label; **labels** sets the order and colour of each label and shows its possible names in the CDRs of the different campaigns as chips (highlighted with their CDRs when the current CDRs have them and ordered from the name in most CDRs), which are added, removed or moved from another label in place; their CDRs show on hover and stay in a floating panel on click. Saving asks for confirmation when a label would add up names of the CDRs, when a name leaves a label used by saved filters or Reporting Jobs (with how many use it) and when a new label contains `_`. The Campaign Maps preview tells the same status of each campaign and adds an exception for an unassigned one in one click. While some name or campaign is unassigned, a red card on every page lists them, with buttons to the matching map for the roles that edit Workspace Config and a request to inform an administrator for the others.
- **Reset Application:** super-admins have a **Reset Application** button in the Administrator Config header that, after a confirmation listing what is deleted and typing `RESET APPLICATION`, stops background work, deletes every workspace, database and the whole config and data folders (backups inside them included) and starts again as a new deployment with the default accounts, ending every session.
- **Starter content for every workspace:** new workspaces, and existing ones once, start with the content shipped in `assets` that they do not have yet: the **NetCheck 2026** Scoring methodology (`assets/scoring-methodologies`), the *NetCheck CDR Report* (NSA and SA) and *RF Quality (RSRP & SINR)* (NSA) Report Templates (`assets/ppt-templates`) and Dashboards (`assets/ppt-dashboards`), and the Auto-calculated Fields they use (`Call_Family`, `Test_Family`, `Test_Result_Group`, `RAT_Group`, which replace the former default fields; `assets/autocalculated-fields/default-autocalculated-fields.json`, formerly `assets/default-calculated-dimensions.json`), the Scoring report configurations *National*, *National & Main Cities* and *National per Vendor* (`assets/scoring-report-configurations`), six Query Builder queries (`assets/query-builder-queries`) and the disabled Reporting Job *Weekly NetCheck Report* (`assets/reporting-jobs`), all without Operator or Vendor filters so they fit any market; a new workspace takes its Vendor Maps from `assets/labels-vendors/default-vendor-maps.json`, and the default Campaign Maps (label format, order of the radio modes and exceptions) come from `assets/labels-campaigns/default-campaign-map.json`.
- **Country of a workspace:** creating a workspace asks for its country among the 50 main countries, and the workspace starts with the Operator Maps of that country's mobile operators in their corporate colours and with the ways the CDRs write them (`assets/labels-operators/operators-by-country.json`); without a country it starts without Operator Maps (formerly every new workspace started with the operators of the United Kingdom). The workspaces table changes the country of a workspace, which adds the operators its Operator Maps do not have yet.
- **NetCheck 2026-Q3 methodology:** a new Scoring methodology for the Q3 2026 campaigns, where NetCheck adds TikTok to Video Streaming: the 20 % of Video Streaming is divided between YouTube (13 %) and TikTok (7 %), with YouTube and TikTok success ratio, TTFP >= 10 s and irritating experience KPIs filtered by `Test_Name`, in both the Best Network and Most Reliable scorings. It is shipped beside NetCheck 2026, and a methodology shipped later reaches every existing workspace once.
- **National & Areas scoring slide:** **Include National & area summary**, in a new panel of each Scoring report scenario (included by default for the whole country; including it clears and disables the Region, Cluster, City and Main Cities filters), adds to each scoring a slide with the National scoring next to chosen cities (London by default, others or the Main Cities) and each Region, Cluster or City of its breakdown (Region by default): each operator's score, its GAP to the reference and its change from the previous campaign or period, with a trend chart per area. Its **Time split** aggregates all the selected CDRs into one value or splits by campaign or by the Daily, Weekly, Monthly, Quarterly or Yearly period of the start time of each test, whatever the CDRs are. The Calculation panel of Scoring & GAP Analysis has the same panel, saved with the calculation selection, the results show, below the Scoring Trend, the **National & Areas** table and charts (as tall as the table beside them, with their campaign or period labels tilted when they do not fit, and titled in one colour for National, another for the separate cities and another for the breakdown; the table and each chart enlarged alone with a double click; a single separate city charted next to National, several after the breakdown; an area not measured in the latest campaign listed without a score, with its earlier campaigns in its chart) and the Saved Filters cards show its time split, breakdown and separate cities. Each area is scored with its own measurements in the same calculation, which reads the CDRs once; the summary is saved with the report configurations.
- **About:** the last entry of the Help menu shows the credits of the application: its logo, name, version, release date and author.
- **Scoring History:** the workspace keeps the components of every scoring KPI of every CDR per day, campaign, operator, vendor, environment, Region, Cluster and City, filled in the background after the other work as soon as a CDR is processed, when the workspace is opened and when the server is idle, and identified by the SHA-256 of each CDR file and its processing (renames keep it, a deleted CDR keeps its history, Daily, Weekly and Final stages are recorded). Calculations whose split and filters it covers are done from it in seconds instead of minutes (the others say in their progress why they read the CDR rows, such as the CDRs still being indexed), with the same results (medians and 90th percentiles within a quarter of a percent); their Points Lost Map follows from the CDR rows (reading only what the map needs) in a job of its own, shown among the background tasks, after the calculations waiting; the Saved Filter being looked at goes first in the queue. Its tables are listed in Database Management.
- **Saved Filters:** every selection calculated in Scoring & GAP Analysis (CDRs, NR Mode, filters, Split by, GAP reference, methodology and National & area summary) becomes a Saved Filter, kept when it is marked with the star (an unstarred one goes when the user calculates or opens another), reused when the same selection is calculated again, and its result is saved until its CDRs, methodology or Operator Maps change, when opening it calculates it again (from the Scoring History, usually in a moment); without its CDRs or methodology the saved result is shown as it was. Each card can be named and starred (at the top), tells on one line how many times it was used (calculated or opened) and calculated, offers **Use newer CDRs** when a Final CDR (or a newer cumulative one) replaces one of its CDRs, and the Saved Filters travel without their results with the Scoring Configuration in Import / Export, transfers and backups, finding their CDRs by the SHA-256 of their files.
- **Methodologies by campaign:** each Scoring methodology can apply to some campaigns (**Applies to campaigns**, from one quarter of the workspace's campaigns to another, either end open, without overlapping those of another methodology, saved by Save Methodology and kept in exports, transfers and backups), and Scoring & GAP Analysis calculates **By campaign** by default, scoring each campaign with the methodology that applies to it (the default one otherwise, so with a single methodology every campaign uses it), so campaigns before and after NetCheck added TikTok are compared with their own rules.
- **Access requests:** **Request Access** on the login page creates the user of an Ericsson signum (7 letters starting with E, stored in capitals; sign-in ignores the case) inactive, as a `user-editor` in the user groups of the chosen workspaces (such as DE or UK) and in the chosen module access group, then opens an email that asks the administrator to activate it in Admin › Users (whose Groups column now follows the role); for a signum that already has a user the dialog warns of it and, with its current password, the request replaces its workspace and module groups and deactivates it until the administrator activates it again. Module access is managed by group: every deployment gets once the **NQ Calls** (Workspace and Non-Qualified Calls), **Benchmarking** (every main module except Non-Qualified Calls) and **All Modules** groups with their Features Activation rules.
- **Import Ericsson RCA Results and Import Previous NQ Analyses:** the **Analysis Center** menu of Non-Qualified Calls (also a submenu of the sections menu of its tab, with every option of the Analysis Center) ends with the imports of root causes: **Import Ericsson RCA Results** and, for admins and super-admins, **Import Previous NQ Analyses**, which imports the analyses made with the previous tool into the calls of the workspace without losing anything: every visible sheet of a CSV or Excel workbook with a `JOIN_ID` column (several `JOIN_IDs` in a cell share the analysis, and a row without one is its call by the Operator, Campaign and session of its CDR), its columns named as in the Analysis Center or as in the analysis workbooks of the previous tool (also the Excel export of Non-Qualified Calls), each comment column as a comment of its own and everything without a field (other columns, values of other rows, values that are not values of their field or that the call keeps) in an import note of the call, but for the copies of what the CDR or the Ericsson RCA of the call already says; the changes, comments and note of each call take the date of its analysis (Date last modification, else the date of the call a week later). The import translates the values (MOC and MTC by the direction of the call, years and quarters as fiscal years in Planned FY (2030 is FY30), two vendors in Vendor 1 and Vendor 2, Planned and Not Planned of the implementation columns in the Planned Status, Closed and Invalidated as Implemented and Not Applicable, No_Vendor_B and No_JoinID Missing of the Analysis Status as Invalidated, the new list values, teams, root categories and causes, and the domain each root category or cause takes in the catalog) with the equivalences of the workbooks of the previous tool built in, so no workspace needs to upload them; an **Equivalences** workbook, whose cells list the values they take, shows them (or every value of a file) and changes them for a workspace, which keeps its changes for the next imports and in its configuration. The analyses of calls not loaded yet wait for their CDR; a preview shows everything before importing, with a template and the choice of filling only the empty values or replacing them (confirmed in the application's dialog), a progress panel shows each step, **Import completed** sums up where the calls went and what they got, and importing a file again adds nothing.

#### 🚀 Enhancements:
- **Scoring report editor:** with several scenarios, the settings whose value differs between them (filters, aggregation levels, scorings, environments and chart, table and GAP options) are highlighted in amber in every scenario, and the highlights follow each change.
- **Renamed Operators and Vendors in saved filters:** renaming a canonical Operator or Vendor in the Operator Maps or Vendor Maps also renames it, besides Report Templates and Dashboards, in the Operator, Operator_Vendor, Vendor_Operator and Vendor filters of Reporting Jobs (GAP reference operator and Scoring report scenarios included), saved Scoring report configurations, the last Scoring calculation, the CDR Analysis filters and the Non-Qualified Calls filters.
- **Job Editor Close button:** the Job Editor closes with a red **Close** button with an exit icon instead of a round ×.
- **Module tab labels on phones:** phones and other small screens (up to 760 px wide) never show the module labels (ALPHA, BETA, NEW…) on the tabs.
- **Points Lost Map:** each map is headed by its operator in large type (web, PowerPoint and Word); maps and rankings are also shown for each environment, and the All Environments ranking names the environments of each area; the map areas are coloured, as chosen in **Colour areas by**, by the points of the city or route that loses most among those measured in them (default) or by the points lost in them by the city or route that loses most there; choosing rows of the ranking (click, Ctrl/⌘+click, Shift+click) dims the other areas and centres the map on theirs, and choosing areas on the map (a click, or Shift and a dragged rectangle) dims the rows measured elsewhere, while every area keeps its colour and changing **Colour areas by** keeps the choice; chosen rows share one colour. Side by side, the maps and rankings of every operator start at the same height even when one note is longer. The reference operator has its map and ranking too, and in the KPI GAP Profile its Gap to Maximum table (without the GAP to itself); a choice of operators saved before keeps the reference enabled. The PowerPoint and Word exports include them when the reference operator is one of the **Operators to compare**. The note above each map writes its points lost and the share of the listed rows in bold. Its location selector, shared with the KPI GAP Profile, lists the campaigns chronologically, in the order of the Campaign Maps, with the latest chosen by default.
- **Workspace Datasets in cards:** the Datasets panel shows the Final, Weekly and Daily CDRs at full width (the Weekly and Daily cards fold and are shown only when there are CDRs of their type) with their CDR Type and In Combined? columns (one font size for every value, small NR Mode, CDR Type and Combined pills, and Rows over Columns and Uploaded over Updated, each with its own sort button, so the tables fit the window), the Network Inventory & Vendor Mappings, Geographic (with the Type of each shape and the editable Operator of Vendor polygons) and Other datasets in compact cards side by side, each with its count and its own sorting and only the Preview, Reprocess and Delete actions, and below them the combined CDR tables in their own card (Rows over Columns, without NR Mode, Size or Uploaded); every card has its icon and the times of the dates their own colour; the CDR rows have a light green background when the combined tables include them and a light grey one otherwise; the Dataset Type filter hides the cards without datasets of that type.
- **Non-Qualified Calls across CDRs:** a call in several Daily, cumulative and Final CDRs is listed once with the data of its most recent CDR (the Final CDR first) and the same analysis in every CDR; choosing CDRs in the CDRs filter shows the calls of those CDRs with their versions there. A badge, the **CDR Version** filter and the **NQ Call Versions** table tell the calls changed between CDRs, with a newer version in another CDR, not in the Final CDR of their campaign or Completed in a newer CDR (listed only on request), and Call Details lists every CDR with the call. Each Speech call shows its number of Non-Qualified samples and the **Speech Samples** of its Call Details tab open each sample to follow it up on its own (also a sheet of the Excel export). **Calls table always shows Region over Cluster, between Operator / Vendor and City / Campaign; **Columns**, a button of the table, adds `JOIN_ID`, NR Mode, Cell ID, Direction, Session Type (CDR), End Time, the CDR, the fields of the Analysis Center and up to 12 CDR columns to it, each sorted and filtered from its header like the other columns (the filters of the new columns under **Additional Columns Filters**, after the **Default Filters** and the **Analysis Center Filters**), and up to 100 more CDR columns to the Excel export only; the Excel export also has Cluster, `Operator_Vendor`, `Vendor_Operator`, Session Type and the CDR columns. The Calls table shows the RCA columns before Root Category / Root Cause, Global Status and Team / Assignee before Comments and Session Type (CDR) before Test, fitting screens of 1440 px without scrolling sideways, its columns are reordered by dragging their headers (remembered by each browser per workspace, with **Default column order** to undo it), and a reload keeps the scroll position.
- **Defaults of a new deployment:** Features Activation starts with every module active for all users, Non-Qualified Calls included, except PPT Reporting (old), off for every role and user (formerly allowed to super-admins and EJAITUR); Interface Settings start with the tab labels off and the stable modules (Workspace, CDR Analysis and PPT Dashboards) without label text instead of STABLE; the Operator Maps start with EE, 3, VF, VF SA, VF VoNR, O2 and Lebara and the Vendor Maps with Ericsson, Ericsson (Mixed), Huawei, Samsung, NSN, SCW, Mixed (non-Ericsson), Other Vendor and (blank), in that order and with new colours and CDR spellings (Mixed Vendor is no longer created; `Mixed` is a spelling of Mixed (non-Ericsson)). Settings already saved keep their values.
- **Polygons map preview:** Preview of a Regions, Clusters or Vendors dataset draws its polygons on a map, each in its own colour and named on the map and in a legend, over the outline of their countries, with wheel zoom, drag to pan and double click to show them all again.
- **CDR Tables Updates:** the Workspace card formerly called Materialization status lists every background job that rewrites the CDR tables by what it does (Auto-calculated Fields, Combined CDR-DATA/VOICE/SPEECH recreations, Combined CDR tables after a CDR Type, NR Mode, In Combined? or deletion change, and CDR tables reconciliation) and reads *All CDR tables are up to date* when idle, and refreshes the Datasets cards in place when a job starts or ends (the combined tables read *Updating* while they follow a change instead of *Missing Rows*); the Auto-calculated Fields panel shows the progress of its materialization with a link to the card and holds the **Re-materialize All Fields** button, next to Edit and Export.
- **Default timezone:** without an Application Runtime timezone or `TZ`, timestamps use the server's own timezone instead of UTC (UTC when the server has none).
- **Interface Settings in Admin:** the Interface Settings panel follows Features Activation instead of Import / Export / Transfer.
- **Scoring & GAP Analysis results in cards:** each block of the Scoring Tables, Scoring Charts and GAP Analysis tabs (the Summary and Breakdown tables, each chart, the GAP tables, the KPI GAP Profile and the Points Lost Map) is a card whose top bar is a gradient of the colour of its tab; changing an operator or another result option keeps the card being looked at in place instead of jumping, and reloading the page returns to the same place once the maps are drawn. The Points Lost Map reads **Operators:** (each operator says that a click enables or disables it) and **Maps per** City, Region or Cluster, and its ranking names the environments in short in a **Type** column (City or Roads) per City and leaves them out per Region or Cluster, as its slides do.
- **Mixed Vendor labels:** the vendor mapping rule names its mixed groups `Ericsson (Mixed)` (Ericsson at one end) and `Mixed (non-Ericsson)` (formerly `Ericsson_Mixed` and `Non-Ericsson_Mixed`), and the CDRs, Vendor Maps, templates, saved filters and queries of existing workspaces are renamed when they are opened. `Mixed (non-Ericsson)` comes last of all in Vendor filters and aggregations (after the `<Operator> - All` values) and last within each Operator in Operator_Vendor.
- **Changelog release navigation:** the Releases list of the Changelog highlights the release being read, as Help Navigation highlights the open document, and the Help Navigation and Releases column grows down to the end of the document, scrolling only beyond it (formerly at the bottom of the window).
- **Server transfers in the background:** the progress dialogs of a server transfer are wider, keep a phase such as *importing workspace 1 of 1 — 90%* on one line and have a **Hide** button on both servers and the transfer continues as a System task (on the destination also while it waits for the package). The source dialog comes back once when the destination starts importing, with **Hide** again, and can cancel the transfer at any stage; the destination follows the source while it creates and sends the package, shows the import's progress and, from the arrival of the package until the end of its import, a dialog without **Hide** on every open page that keeps the application out of use. Each server shows the other's reason when the other stops the transfer because of an error.
- **Failed Scoring calculations in App Logs:** a Scoring & GAP Analysis calculation that fails writes a `scoring_job_failed` event with its error and where it happened.
- **File classification before uploading:** each selected file is a card tinted by its detected type (Data, Voice, Speech, mappings, geographic datasets, Network Inventories), with an icon badge, its extension and size, rounded selectors in one row and its mappings grouped below, and a dashed outline and a **Daily CDR** flag in its corner when it is a Daily CDR; the panel shows how many files are being classified. **Vendor source** is offered only when the CDRs have both sources (Multivendor Mappings or Network Inventories, and Vendor polygons, in the workspace or in the same upload), proposing the Vendor polygons when they cover every Operator of the Network Inventory source (VFUK covers Vodafone, 3UK covers 3, each Network Inventory its Operator) and the Network Inventory otherwise; with one source that source is used, and mapping selectors without choices are no longer shown.
- **Background dataset uploads:** the files of an upload are sent in chunks to the workspace where the upload started, and the upload continues after changing page, module or workspace (also after closing the browser, the next time the application is opened), from the bytes already received; it starts at once, and when the browser cannot keep the files (a batch over its storage quota) its card asks to keep the page open and leaving the page asks first. The mappings, polygons and inventories go first, and each file is processed in that workspace as soon as it is uploaded, while the next ones upload.
- **Parallel dataset processing:** the datasets of a workspace are processed in parallel, up to **Maximum simultaneous tasks** (the CDRs of an upload still wait for its mappings), while its combined tables, Auto-calculated Fields and Scoring calculations run one at a time; the effective limit only reserves one logical CPU for interactive requests (formerly at most 4 tasks, and one dataset of a workspace at a time).
- **Split by:** the Aggregation levels of Scoring & GAP Analysis (Calculation panel, job cards, report scenarios, Reporting Jobs and the covers of its PowerPoint and Word documents) are now called **Split by**, which tells what they do: split the results into separate scorings.
- **Rows Split and Columns Split:** the Rows Aggregation and Column Aggregation columns of the PPT Templates are now called **Rows Split** and **Columns Split** in the PPT Templates Editor, the shipped templates and the template files; the templates of every workspace are converted when it is opened and template files with the former names are still read.
- **Query Builder CDR panels:** the Source Datasets of Query Builder are chosen in a Data, a Voice and a Speech panel, as in the other modules, instead of one long list: each CDR shows its campaigns and NR Mode, Weekly and Daily CDRs are highlighted, and each panel has its count and **Select All / None**.
- **Campaign modes in the label:** **Show in label**, next to NSA and SA in the Campaign Maps, chooses the modes that `{mode}` writes, so NSA campaigns can read `2026-Q3` while SA ones read `2026-Q3-SA` without an exception per campaign.
- **Tab menus:** hovering a tab opens the list of its sections, each with an icon and in the colour of the module (the panels of each module, Admin and App Logs, the Readme chapters, the latest Changelog releases, and the Help chapters grouped in General, Main Modules, Administrative Modules and Reference with the Readme and the Changelog), and **Builders**, **Config** and the **User** badge (with **Change password**) open their menus on hover; in Config and Builders each page lists its sections beside it, and Scoring Results lists its Scoring Charts, Scoring Tables and GAP Analysis tabs; on small screens the menus fit their content and list those sections below their page, indented. Choosing a section opens the page with that section at its top, unfolding it when it was folded, also when the page fills its panels afterwards or opens again with its saved selection (and the Scoring Results entries bring the results tabs to the top), and a reload then returns to where the page was being read, while clicking the tab or page itself opens it at its top.
- **Back to top:** a round button at the bottom left, in the colour of the current module, appears once a page is scrolled down and takes it back to the top.
- **Campaign Comparison:** three selectors choose the operator, the baseline campaign and the campaign compared with it (by default the previous and the latest), and the table spans the whole card.
- **Faster Scoring results:** the GAP to the reference and the views of the results are built far faster (the Most Reliable scoring of a large job in a fraction of a second instead of most of a minute), and the results of completed jobs are kept compressed on the server, so opening a job again is immediate until it is recalculated or the Operator or Vendor Maps change.
- **Server transfers:** a transfer rejected by the destination server says so before its reason (for example *The destination server rejected the transfer request: …*), so it is not taken for an error of this server, importing a selection of several packages names the one being imported (for example *importing package 6 of 9 (Auto-calculated Fields)*), and imported Auto-calculated Fields that differ from those of the workspace update its CDR tables as a background task once every package is imported, instead of holding the import until they are materialized.
- **Sign in on the documents:** the Help, Readme and Changelog read without an account show **Sign in** as a large button in the brand colours, with an icon and a soft pulse, so it is not missed.
- **Non-Qualified Calls Reindex and Join ID:** the calls are indexed as a background task (in the floating background tasks card): the first time the module opens and, for new, changed or removed CDRs, once the server has been idle for two minutes, so its pages never wait for the indexing; **Refresh** is now **Reindex**, which indexes the calls of every CDR again in the background (keeping their follow-up) even when no CDR has changed, the Calls panel is now called **Non-Qualified Calls**, and its new **Join ID** search, centred in the title bar of the panel, finds the calls with one or more typed or pasted `JOIN_ID` values.
- **Idle server:** the requests that pages send on their own to refresh themselves (job progress, background tasks, transfers and the like) no longer count as use of the application, so the work kept for an idle server, such as the Dashboard chart warm-up and the Non-Qualified Calls indexing, is no longer held back by an open page.
- **Faster Non-Qualified Calls:** a filter change of a large workspace answers in about a second instead of up to a minute: the latest version of every call is kept in the **NQ Call Latest** table (rebuilt in the background after each indexing or a change of the Operator and Vendor Maps) and read with the current follow-up, the calls of a choice of CDRs are read once per request, the root cause suggestions are matched once per combination of CDR values and found once for all the panels of a filter change, only the panels on screen are calculated, and the pages no longer wait for the indexing or for a CDR being processed.
- **Lighter page polling:** every open page asks for the background tasks every 2 s only while work runs (every 8 s otherwise, every 30 s in a hidden tab) and for incoming transfers every 5 s (30 s hidden), so many users can keep pages open on one server.
- **Phones and small screens:** no panel widens the page any more (wide content scrolls inside its own container), filters go two per row and action buttons are as wide as their text; the Reporting Jobs, their Run History and the Admin tables of user groups, workspace access, features activation and interface settings become cards like the CDRs, the workspace cards keep the Access picker on its own row, and every pager of the application (cards on phones, dataset previews, Query Builder results, Database Management, Non-Qualified Calls) shares one design of round arrow buttons in a white pill instead of emoji buttons. Non-Qualified Calls fits the phone: its calls are cards with the name of each value, shown one at a time with the same pager (which moves through the pages of calls by itself), the table header is a row of chips that sort and filter, the panel bar stays on one row, indicators and pipeline go two per row, the call details and the Analysis Center dialogs fill the screen, and the Scoring setup, PPT Dashboards cards and Workspace Config Main Cities fit too.
- **Split by Campaign by default:** Scoring & GAP Analysis starts with Operator and Campaign checked in the Split by.
- **Progress periods:** the Progress View of Non-Qualified Calls shows the calls per month by default, and the Progress Status of a new Non-Qualified Calls artifact of the Reporting Jobs per week.
- **Merge teams:** in **Statuses & Teams** of Non-Qualified Calls, a team can be merged into another one (⤳ Merge into…): on Save its calls and members move to that team and it is removed.
- **Core and RF domains:** the root cause domain Core 2G/4G/5G of Non-Qualified Calls is now **Core**, also in existing workspaces with their calls and filters, and RF, the most frequent root domain, is blue instead of red (unless a workspace chose another colour).

#### 🐛 Bug fixes:
- **Job Editor drafts:** reloading the Reporting page no longer reopens the unsaved values of a job that changed on the server since then (imported, transferred, saved from another browser or renamed in the Maps), which could show old filters such as an Operator with its former name; its saved version opens instead.
- **Scoring calculation notice:** while the scoring of the selected CDRs and filters is calculating or recalculating, the yellow notice says so steadily instead of alternating with the number of selected CDRs each time the Saved Filters refresh, the Calculate Scoring and Recalculate buttons no longer flicker, and the results show an animated striped progress bar, **Calculating Scoring…**, in the colour of the result tab, with the progress and step of the calculation instead of alternating placeholder messages. The same bar, **Loading Scoring Results…**, shows while a saved result loads. The calculation also appears in the floating background tasks card with its elapsed time, and each card of the Saved Filters panel shows its calculation time (or the time elapsed while it runs).
- **Scoring calculation filters after an NR Mode change:** changing the NR Mode or the CDRs no longer unchecks values of the context filters: a filter with every value checked keeps every value of the new CDRs checked, and a partial choice keeps the values that the new CDRs do not have, for when they come back.
- **Scoring report editor:** the configuration of the last report is remembered as soon as Generate PowerPoint or Generate Word is pressed (formerly when the document was ready), and the selector shows the saved configuration whose scenarios are the ones shown, or **<Not saved configuration>** when none has them; formerly it kept the name of the configuration the last report was chosen from, also when its scenarios were different (for example Operator saved as All but shown as 4/5 selected).
- **Points Lost Map with a single campaign:** the web page and the PowerPoint and Word documents show the Points Lost Map of jobs aggregated by a level with a single value (for example Campaign with one campaign); formerly it was missing, without recalculating anything.
- **Mixed Vendor groups:** with `Mixed` as a Vendor Maps spelling of the mixed group of no Vendor, the Vendor filters and values no longer show it behind another Vendor (such as `Ericsson_Non-Ericsson_Mixed`): a Vendor is mapped as a whole label.
- **Server transfer reception:** opening or reloading an Admin page on the destination while it receives a package no longer deletes that package, which made the transfer fail with `[Errno 2] No such file or directory` once the package had arrived, and a retried transmission no longer shares its file with the interrupted one. An import that stops without recording its result is reported as failed instead of staying at *Validating 0%* until the source gives up after 24 hours.
