- **Full Environment and workspace exports of old workspaces:** exporting or transferring a workspace not opened since an update no longer fails with errors such as `no such column: p.cdr_stage`. Before the export or transfer starts, a dialog lists the selected workspaces whose database comes from an earlier version and offers to **Convert and include** them (their database is updated to the current version first, as opening them would) or **Exclude them**.
- **Operator colours of operators in some campaigns only:** an operator measured only in some campaigns or scopes of a Scoring result (such as VF SA) takes its Operator Maps colour in the Points Lost Map (heading, note and operator button) and in the KPI GAP Profile slides of the PowerPoint and Word exports, as it already did in the other charts and tables; formerly they used the colours of the first score table only.
- **CDR Analysis filters:** a filter applied with **All values** stays open, so values that appear later are selected too; **Vendor_Operator** shows the values chosen in it instead of none; **Vendor** stays in sync with **Operator_Vendor** and **Vendor_Operator**; and each CDR keeps its own filters when another CDR is opened and closed, as the browser no longer applies the filters of the last CDR by name to the next one (which could select nothing and lose the CDR's saved filters).
- **Workspace transfers without Input files:** transferring a complete workspace or a Full Environment with the Input folder unchecked no longer fails with *The transfer offer has incompatible content components*; the destination accepts it, as it already did without the Output folder. A package without the Input or Output folder lists its files: when it replaces (transferred or imported) a workspace with the same name that already holds the same files, such as those sent by an earlier transfer, that workspace keeps its own folder, and with Output its history of generated files, which now travels in the workspace database; otherwise the folder is empty and a warning names the Input files, the Output files or both, in the destination's approval dialog, the source's progress dialog and the import's completion message.
- **Root cause in the call panel:** the cause list of a Non-Qualified Call opens above its detail panel, so the cause can be changed there too (formerly it opened hidden behind the panel).
- **CDRs of imported Dashboards:** Dashboards imported or transferred into another workspace choose the CDRs of that workspace with the same name and type instead of keeping the ids of the source workspace's CDRs, and a Dashboard leaves out chosen CDRs deleted from its workspace (with none left it uses the newest CDRs), so it no longer fails to open with "Selected data CDR was not found".
- **CDR Tables Updates of a new workspace:** a workspace whose CDR tables were marked for an update that no job runs (such as a new workspace without CDRs) runs it instead of showing "Updating CDR tables · 0%" forever.
- **Deleting and creating workspaces:** a new workspace no longer takes the id of a deleted one, which made the Workspace page show "Workspace deleted" and hide the new workspace when it was created shortly after deleting the last one; and the folder of a deleted workspace moves to a trash folder at once, so a new workspace with the same name created while the old files are removed keeps its own files and database.
- **Scoring of areas without an environment:** in the National & Areas and the Scoring per location charts, an area or city with no measurements in an environment (London without Connecting Roads, for example) is scaled to the full maximum of the methodology, as the scoring calculation scales it, instead of showing only the points of its measured environments.
- **Clear history of a call:** the Clear history button of the call panel is shown only for calls with a history (formerly it was shown for every call to admins and super-admins).
- **Zoom buttons on touch screens:** a tap on a Scoring chart or map shows its floating zoom buttons, which stay a few seconds or until a tap elsewhere; formerly they only showed while the finger touched it.
- **Panels chosen in a module menu:** a panel chosen in the sections menu of a module stays at the top of the page while the page loads its data, also when that takes longer than 15 seconds or the panels above it change size (until you scroll, for up to a minute), and Non-Qualified Calls always opens it in its view instead of returning to where the page was read last.
- **Numbers in Non-Qualified Calls:** every number of the page reads in English, the language of the app, whatever the language of the browser: a comma between thousands, also in four-digit numbers, and a point before decimals (a browser in Spanish, for one, wrote 9999 and 11.516).
- **Confirmations over dialogs:** a confirmation asked from an open dialog (such as deleting the Ericsson RCA results, Apply to Calls of the Root Cause Catalog or replacing the values on importing analyses) shows the application's confirmation above it instead of hiding behind it, and Escape closes only the confirmation.
- **Progress View with the Status Rules:** the closures, reopenings, status changes and days to close of the Progress View of Non-Qualified Calls count the statuses the Status Rules set (as when importing analyses), not only those chosen by hand; the moves to a closed status are only the Closed column, and the days to close count from the call, as imported analyses follow a call up and close it at once.

#### 📚 Documentation:
- The **Vendor mapping rule** card of Workspace describes the current rule: every Vodafone and Three Operator is mapped (other spellings, such as Vodafone SA or VF_SA, keep their own name as prefix), Operators without a Vendor get `<Operator> - All`, and the result fills `Operator_Vendor`, `Vendor` and `Vendor_Operator`.

---

## Release: v0.6.0
### Release Date: 2026-10-06
#### ⚠️ Breaking Changes:
- CDR vendor columns are renamed everywhere: the former `Vendor` (`<Operator>_<Vendor>`) is now **`Operator_Vendor`** and the former `Vendor_Only` (the vendor alone) is now **`Vendor`**. Operators without a vendor show `<Operator> - All` in both columns (for example `EE - All`). Workspaces are migrated once at startup (processed CDRs, combined tables, catalogues and stored filter values), every filter and aggregation of CDR Analysis, Network Insights, E2E Dashboards, Scoring, Query Builder, Non-Qualified Calls and Reporting uses the new names, and files exported with the former names are still read with their former meaning. Report Templates are converted once so their charts stay identical: groupings and legends by `Vendor` become `Operator_Vendor`, and `Vendor_Only` filters become `Vendor`; templates written for earlier versions are converted the same way when imported, but exported templates cannot be imported by earlier versions. Review saved SQL in Query Builder that names `Vendor_Only`.
- Every PowerPoint and Word export (CDR Analysis, Network Insights, E2E Dashboards, Scoring & GAP Analysis, Non-Qualified Calls) and every Reporting Job artifact is named `yyyymmdd_hhmmss - <Module> - <report name>` (the timestamp appears once) and the download of a run is `yyyymmdd_hhmmss - Reporting - <job name>.zip`.
- **Dashboard Analytic is now DriveTest Analyzer:** the application, its header, browser title, login, emails and exports, the macOS launcher (`DriveTest Analyzer.app` and `DriveTest Analyzer Installer.pkg`), the GitHub repository (`DriveTestAnalyzer`), the FastAPI module (`src.DriveTestAnalyzer:app`), the Docker image (`jaimetur/drivetest-analyzer`), the environment variables (`DRIVETEST_ANALYZER_*`), the export, backup and temporary file names (`drivetest-analyzer-*`) and the launcher folders (`.drivetest-analyzer-runtime`, `.drivetest-analyzer-venv`) use the new name. Docker deployments must point `IMAGE_REPOSITORY` to the new image. Former `DASHBOARD_ANALYTIC_*` variables are still read when the new one is not set, export packages, JSON exports and backups created with the former name are still imported and restored, browser preferences are kept, and the launcher remembers the project folder chosen before the rename.
- **Mappings & Reference Data** replaces the Operator & Vendor Maps component of Import/Export, transfers and Backup (component `mappings-reference-data`, file `mappings-reference-data.json` with the Operator and Vendor Maps, Spectrum Holdings and Campaign Maps); packages and backups with the former Operator & Vendor Maps component are no longer accepted.
- **Scoring aggregation hierarchy:** the hierarchy is an application setting shared by every workspace and methodology, ordered from the Aggregation levels of Scoring & GAP Analysis and kept in the Application database backup. Methodologies no longer store it (a hierarchy in an imported methodology is ignored) and Workspace Config no longer has the Methodology Aggregation Hierarchy panel; hierarchies saved in methodologies are not migrated.

#### 🌟 New Features:
- **Non-Qualified Calls** lists every Voice and Speech call and every Data test whose result is not Completed in the ready CDRs of the workspace, indexed automatically when CDRs are added, reprocessed or deleted. An executive summary (totals, open, closed, attended, with team, commented, and breakdowns by Service, Result, Status, Team, Root Domain, Failure Classification, Campaign, Operator, Vendor, Region, Cluster and City) drills down into a filterable, sortable and paginated table — every indicator card and value of the Summary, Progress and Root Cause Analysis panels filters it — where editors set each call's status, responsible team and assignee in place or in bulk. The Filters panel (CDRs, Campaign, Operator, Operator_Vendor, Vendor, Region, Cluster, City with Main Cities, Service, Technology, Test Name, Result, failures, Status, Team and Assignee) is shared by every user of the workspace and restored in every session. A **Progress View** adds donut charts by Status, Team, Assignee, Service and Result, and per week, month, quarter or year the calls detected, attended, commented, closed and reopened, status changes, the open backlog and the average days to close, the age of open calls and the workload of teams and assignees. A side panel shows every CDR field of the call and an activity timeline of shared comments and changes with their author and time; comments can be edited or deleted by their author or an admin while the history keeps every previous value, and concurrent changes are detected instead of overwritten. Statuses and teams are configurable with colours in **Statuses & Teams**, where the **Members** of each team open a dialog to choose its users (a user can belong to several teams); a call of a team with members can only be assigned to one of them. The follow-up stays with each call when its CDR is reprocessed or uploaded again, admins and super-admins can delete single history entries, the history of a call or that of the selected calls, Export Excel downloads the filtered calls with their comments and history, Reporting Jobs include a **Non-Qualified Calls** artifact (Executive Summary and Progress Status in PowerPoint, Word or Excel, with its own filters, period and open-calls option), and the follow-up with teams and members travels as **NQ Call Tracking** with Import/Export, transfers, Full Workspace/Environment packages and backups.
- **Workspace access by role and group:** besides individual users, each workspace can be granted to roles and user groups when it is created or saved in **Workspace → Access** (super-admins) and in the new **Admin → Workspace Access** panel. The Users table of Admin chooses the user groups of each account in a new **Groups** column, and its **Workspaces** column also lists the workspaces an account opens through its groups and its role.
- **CDR type of a workspace:** Workspace Config and the creation of a workspace choose the CDR files the workspace handles. NetCheck CDR is the default and the only type supported today; Umlaut CDR is listed as coming soon.
- **Most Reliable Network scoring:** besides the Best Network scoring, Scoring & GAP Analysis calculates the NetCheck **Most Reliable** scoring, which rates the KPIs whose **KPI Type** is **Reliable** (call success, POLQA < 1.6, transfer success, throughput above 2/1 Mbit/s, browsing and video success) with its own maximum points and the same thresholds. Each methodology sets the **Most Reliable points** of its Reliable KPIs per environment in its KPI table (1,000 points in NetCheck 2026: 650 Drive - City and 350 Drive - Connecting Roads; Voice 350 and Data 650); points copied with two decimals that miss an environment's total by up to 0.1 points (1,000.04) are scaled to it exactly. Every job whose methodology has Most Reliable points shows both scorings with the same CDRs, filters, aggregation and GAP reference: a **Scoring** selector beside Environment switches the tables, charts, GAP analysis and notes, and the Scoring and GAP CSV follow it. The PowerPoint and Word exports and the Scoring artifact of Reporting Jobs contain both scorings in one document, a Best Network block followed by a Most Reliable block. Most Reliable points travel with the methodology in its JSON export and import, transfers and backups.
- **NetCheck-style Scoring insights:** for both scorings, in the web results and in the PowerPoint and Word exports (All Environments block or the exported environment), Scoring & GAP Analysis adds **Scoring per City / Cluster / Region** cards when that level has 2 to 12 values (each operator's Voice and Data points and total, labelled with the operator names of the mapping table and with tooltips on the web, scaled to the full maximum when a location is measured in fewer environments, with a dark blue summary panel), a **Scoring Trend** line chart when Campaign has more than four campaigns, a **KPI GAP Profile** per compared operator and per selected City, Cluster or Region (Gap to Maximum and Gap to the reference per KPI and environment, sorted, with gradient data bars in every column; the Most Reliable KPIs underlined in Best Network; on the web it follows the operator chosen in GAP comparison, and the reverse), a **Points Lost Map** per compared operator (on the web, chosen by location and by one or more operators shown side by side (all by default), both shared with the KPI GAP Profile, and by the analysis per City, Region or Cluster (disabled without data); each map zooms into a dragged rectangle or with floating zoom buttons shown while the pointer is over it; the UK ITL3/NUTS3 areas coloured by the points lost, with the bundled ONS boundaries, and the cities and Connecting Roads routes that lose most; per Cluster and per Region on the workspace's Clusters and Region Mapping polygons; each KPI's lost points are shared among the tests that cause them) and a **Campaign Comparison** of each KPI's value and points in the two latest campaigns with the total Scoring Points Gap. Scoring slide titles are blue with their focus in bold, the third chart is called **Scoring per Category (Breakdown)**, and cover titles wider than the slide are reduced to one line.
- **Scoring report scenarios:** **Generate Scoring Report (PPT/Word)** of Scoring & GAP Analysis (from the CDRs, NR Mode, methodology and GAP reference selected in the Calculation panel), and the Scoring artifact of Reporting Jobs (**Configure report…**, required: its filters and aggregation levels are those of the scenarios), open a report editor with one or more **scenarios** (for example National, London, Main Cities and per Vendor), each with its own filters (with a search box, **Select All / None** for the listed values, **Main Cities**, which checks the workspace main cities and follows that list at each run, and an **All** option, which follows the values of the CDRs at each run; the values stay editable, in every list; a list shows one chosen value by name and several by their number, such as `2/4 selected` or `Main Cities (5/12)`) and aggregation levels (a level with a single value, such as one Campaign, is left out of the scenario and of every Scoring chart, table and document) on the job's CDRs, NR Mode, methodology and GAP reference, calculated or reused when the document is generated. Each scenario chooses, for the Best Network and the Most Reliable Network scoring: All Environments only (default, and its subtitles omit it) or each environment too, the scoring charts (per Service, per Category, per Category (Breakdown), per City/Cluster/Region cards and trend), the tables (Category and Breakdown, KPI values in the PowerPoint Breakdown tables, GAP values and Campaign comparison) and the GAP analysis (operators to compare, Vodafone and Three by default; all-operators table, individual tables, KPI GAP Profile and Points lost per City map). Configurations are saved and deleted by name and loaded by choosing them in the selector, which opens on the configuration in use (Default included; unsaved changes ask to cancel, discard them or save them first), export and import as JSON, the last one used reopens in later sessions, and they travel with the Scoring & GAP Analysis Configuration in Import / Export, transfers and backups.
- **Non-Qualified Calls root causes:** each call can be labelled with a root cause from a configurable taxonomy of domains (RF, RAN, Core 2G/4G/5G, Core EPSFB, IMS/E2E, AAA, Protocol, Device) and causes, in the row, the call details or for several selected calls at once. Keywords of each domain and cause, matched as whole words in their configured order, suggest a root cause from the CDR Failure Classification, Category, Subcategory and Comment and, when the CDR leaves the cause unresolved, from the comments of the call, following a suggestion rule that every user can see and admins and super-admins can change (fields, steps and keyword matching); a click (or **Apply suggestions** on the selected calls) applies it, **Add missing defaults** restores missing default values, and a workspace setting can require a root cause to close a call. The **Root Cause Analysis** panel, the PowerPoint and Word exports and the Reporting Jobs artifact show the calls per root domain and cause, Classic vs WhatsApp, NSA vs SA, technology and the eNB/gNB of their last cell with its site, host and vendor from the cell mappings; the Root Domain and Root Cause filters, the Excel export, Database Management (**NQ Root Causes**) and the NQ Call Tracking import, export, transfers and backups include them.
- **Vendor_Operator:** every CDR offers `Vendor_Operator`, the `Operator_Vendor` identity the other way round (`<Vendor>_<Operator>`, `<Operator> - All` without a vendor), as a filter in CDR Analysis, E2E Dashboards, Network Insights, Scoring & GAP Analysis, Non-Qualified Calls and Reporting Jobs and as a chart dimension; it is derived when the CDRs are read, so existing CDRs have it too. The Operator_Vendor and Vendor_Operator filters stay in sync: selecting values in one selects the same identities in the other. Vendor_Operator is ordered by Vendor and then Operator, with Ericsson_Mixed beside Ericsson for each Operator and Non-Ericsson_Mixed last of all, after the operators without a vendor.

#### 🚀 Enhancements:
- **Select All per section:** the content selectors of Import / Export / Transfer, of the backup and of the backup restore show a Select All button beside every section with several options (for example Workspace Content), which turns into Select None once the whole section is selected.
- **Backup content:** the backup selector lists Configuration Content, then the **Workspaces** to back up by name (replacing the separate Workspaces to include field), then the Workspace Content kept **of the selected workspaces**; a green card under Backup Now summarises what the backup keeps (for example `UK: full workspace without Input` or `UK: 5 of 12 parts`) and an amber one explains selections that leave content out. The Input and Output folders can only be selected together with the Workspace Database they belong to, are listed last (Output, then Input) in the backup and restore selections, and schedules saved without the database leave them out. Saved Queries are called Query Builder Queries in backups, as in Import / Export.
- **Export / Transfer:** the workspace content section is called Current Workspace Content, the Full Workspace section notes that it includes the Input and Output folders, and exporting or transferring a Full Workspace asks whether to include its Output folder and, new, its Input folder (original CDR files, needed to reprocess datasets); both are included by default.
- **Module tabs:** Admin → Interface Settings sets, for every main module, the position of its tab (dragging the row or with arrows), its title and short title for narrow windows, its icon and its colour, besides its label, with a preview of each tab; the Modules menu, the Help navigation and Features Activation follow the same order, and the Help names renamed modules with their new title. Every Help navigation chapter shows an icon: its module tab icon and colour, or a document icon for the General and Reference chapters. These settings travel with App Config in Import / Export, transfers and backups. The default order is Workspace, CDR Analysis, E2E Dashboards, Reporting (old), Scoring & GAP Analysis, Network Insights, Non-Qualified Calls, Reporting and Builders. The tabs use a slightly smaller title and padding when that keeps them on one row, and return to their default size on two rows when even the compact size does not fit; the administrative tabs sit just above them, leaving room for the module labels.
- **Colour pickers** (Operator and Vendor Maps, module labels) show their value in hexadecimal beside them, to copy it or paste a new colour.
- **Features Activation** highlights the modules active for Nobody by default.
- **Operator and Vendor order everywhere:** whenever Vendor or Operator_Vendor is a row or column aggregation (CDR Analysis, E2E Dashboards, Reporting, Chart Builder, Scoring & GAP Analysis tables and exports, Network Insights groups and CDF curves, Non-Qualified Calls breakdowns), Vendors follow the Vendor Maps table and Operator_Vendor values follow the Operator Maps table and, within one Operator, the Vendor Maps table; Vendors missing from the map follow the mapped ones and Operators without a Vendor come last. Previously the Scoring Vendor level and the Vendor field were ordered alphabetically.
- **Network Insights:** opening or reloading the page with the selection of the last analysis shows that analysis again at once, from the results kept by the workspace, without pressing Analyse Network.
- **Scoring & GAP Analysis:** Scoring Charts is the first tab of the results panel and the one opened by default.
- **Scoring & GAP Analysis:** when the filters leave an environment without results in every series (for example City = London, only in Drive - City), the Combined scores scale the environments with results to the full maximum scoring, the same for every operator, and an orange card explains it; the maximum-score donuts show the configured points struck through beside the allocated ones, and the PowerPoint and Word exports show the notice in orange. Saved jobs are scaled when opened, without recalculating. Calculation notes group the series with the same missing contributions into short notes.
- **Scoring & GAP Analysis:** reloading the page keeps the selected results tab and the location, operators and analysis of the KPI GAP Profile and Points Lost Map, while opening the page again from another page or calculating a new scoring always opens Scoring Charts.
- **E2E Dashboards:** PPT Generation Jobs are listed newest first, so a relaunched job moves to the top; selecting a job in the table shows its charts in the Charts Panel, and the Charts Panel offers **View as Dashboard** to open the Dashboard of the selected job.
- **Operator and Vendor Maps:** groups with unsaved changes are highlighted with their save button, a counter above each table shows how many there are, **Save all changes** saves them together (groups that cannot be saved keep their edits and are listed), and leaving the page with unsaved changes asks for confirmation (with the application's dialog when following one of its links; reloading, closing the tab or going back can only use the browser's own).
- **Administration:** every panel describes its purpose under its title, Workspace Access is listed before Features Activation without the empty space above their tables, and the bulk dataset actions share the row of Apply Changes in Datasets Management.
- **Main tabs:** Non-Qualified Calls uses a raspberry palette, Network Insights an electric indigo palette, and Scoring & GAP Analysis shows its selected tab with the gradient of its header. Module tabs show their stage in a small label in the corner: a red ALPHA while in development (Network Insights and Non-Qualified Calls), a yellow BETA while being validated (Reporting and Builders), a blue NEW with a star once consolidated (Scoring & GAP Analysis) and a green STABLE for established modules (Workspace, CDR Analysis and E2E Dashboards). In Admin → Interface Settings, super-admins choose the label of each main module (any short text, or none), its colour and an optional predefined icon in front of it (star, sparkle, lightning bolt, rocket, flame, trending up, heart, crown, check mark, flask, warning, information, cross, bug, wrench, clock, lock, shield or dot) with its own colour, preview them live, reset them to the defaults or hide every label. Unselected module tabs are light and neutral, with their module colour on the icon and a thin top line, and take a tint of that colour on hover. The administrative tabs have icons and the same visible height as the main tabs. Every module header has a subtle decoration that represents the module: data layers (Workspace), CDF curves beside a bar benchmark (CDR Analysis), an antenna with radio waves, a drive-test coverage map and a site inventory (Network Insights), a dashboard slide with KPI tiles, bars, CDF curves and a donut (E2E Dashboards), a trophy beside ranked bars with a GAP bracket (Scoring), a failed call beside an executive follow-up dashboard (Non-Qualified Calls), report pages and a paper plane (Reporting), slides (Reporting (old)), a chart canvas with a pencil (Chart Builder), a database with SQL and a magnifier (Query Builder), a shield (Admin), meshing gears (Config) and a log sheet with a magnifier (App Logs). Module headers fit their content with the same spacing and text sizes, their decoration fills the header height and their descriptions wrap before it. Workspace and CDR Analysis show their selectors in a separate panel below the header (Select Workspace and Select CDR to Analyze, with the colour of the header), and the badges and actions of Workspace, Scoring & GAP Analysis and Non-Qualified Calls move to the first panel below the header with stronger contrast; Admin and App Logs headers no longer show a badge.
- **Cluster** is available after Region in the filters of CDR Analysis, Network Insights, E2E Dashboards, Scoring, Non-Qualified Calls and the Reporting Job artifacts, as a Network Insights grouping and column of its inventory tables, and as a Scoring aggregation level shown in Scoring job names and in the name of the generated PowerPoint. Saved Scoring methodologies keep their calculated jobs and place Cluster after Region.
- **Filter order:** Operator_Vendor and Vendor follow Operator in every filter panel, E2E Dashboards list Operator, Operator_Vendor and Vendor before the location filters and Campaign, and Main Cities is the first option of every City selector. The Network Insights artifact shows its filters in one row.
- **CDR Analysis** offers only measurements in its metric selector: the service KPIs and the radio and service measurements of each CDR (RSRP, RSRQ, SINR, CQI, MCS, BLER, transmit power, throughput, delays and RTT), without identifiers, timestamps, durations, bands or minimum and maximum columns, and every metric is selected by default.
- **Reporting** is available to every user, Reporting Job filter and metric dropdowns open one at a time, close when clicking elsewhere and show their name above them, list only the values of the CDRs each entry uses (its selected CDRs or those of its NR Mode), read All when every value or none is selected and offer Select All / None, all job actions share one row and the last run status has its own column. The Job Editor unfolds inside the jobs table, right below the edited job (or at the top for a new one), and folds away on Cancel, its × button or Save, asking before discarding unsaved changes and reopening with them after a page reload; it starts with the job name and a Job enabled switch, then the schedule and the delivery, and then the artifacts; disabled jobs keep their actions at full colour; each artifact checkbox reads as the title of its panel, every row of choices (formats, aggregation, CDF comparison, grouping, levels and weekdays) uses green chips, and at least one output format always stays selected.
- **Startup:** while the active workspace is opened and its database upgraded after an update, the application answers with a page that reports the progress and opens the login as soon as it is ready, instead of a blank screen.
- **Workspace dialogs:** Reprocess Datasets and Clear Mappings are wider and list the CDRs in Data, Voice and Speech panels with Select All/None, like Map CDR fields.
- **Datasets Analysis is now CDR Analysis** in the tabs, menus, Reporting Job artifacts, summary exports and documentation; its address (`/datasets-analysis`) and its Features Activation key are unchanged. **Open Dataset** shows the analysis at once, and the last metrics and Adaptive Filters applied to each CDR are shared by every user of the workspace and restored in every session (**Reset** clears them). Filters follow Source Sheet, Operator, Operator_Vendor, Vendor, Market, Region, Cluster, City and Test Name. CDF comparisons group by Operator by default with Operator listed first, charts have floating zoom controls, rectangle zoom as in E2E Dashboards and hover tooltips and rotate bar labels that do not fit, the CDF Visible X Range has a start and an end handle and starts by default where the first curve reaches 5% of its samples, mirroring the 95% end, Export PowerPoint and Export Word export the open CDR as on screen (with each chart's comparisons and visible range) or a summary of the selected CDRs from one dialog with CDR Data, Voice and Speech panels, PowerPoint exports use the PowerPoint template in assets with a title slide listing the filters, tables, percentiles, bars and CDF curves by Operator, Vendor or Operator_Vendor follow the order of the Operator and Vendor Maps of Workspace Config, grouped percentiles and Processed Metrics (with Cluster after Region) show two decimals, metric cards keep their sample count inside the card, and analysing many metrics is about twice as fast.
- **Vendor filters** list their values in the same order everywhere: pure vendors, then mixed, other and all-vendor groups, then operators without a vendor, each block alphabetically.
- **Reporting Jobs:** the CDR Analysis artifact chooses the metrics of each CDR type (Data, Voice and Speech; all by default), the Network Insights filters and single-choice Global Comparison and Global CDF Comparison rows, and Network Insights artifacts keep Operator in their grouping unless Vendor is checked, as in the module. CDR Analysis, Network Insights, Dashboard and Scoring artifacts choose their CDRs by hand or, at each run, with **Newest complete set of Data, Voice and Speech CDRs** or **All complete sets of Data, Voice and Speech CDRs** (every CDR whose campaigns have Data, Voice and Speech CDRs of the NR Mode; CDR Analysis applies them in each NR Mode), which replace every ready CDR (jobs saved with it use All complete sets); the locked CDR list shows the CDRs the chosen option selects now.
- **Faster pages:** Workspace and CDR Analysis open without rescanning the CDRs: which metrics have values in each CDR and the row count of each combined table are kept in the workspace until that CDR or table changes. CDR Analysis shows its first four metrics at once and loads the rest in the background in small batches, computes what every metric shares once, copies only the columns each metric uses and keeps the recent analyses of several CDRs in memory.
- **Network Insights exports** follow the order and layout of the page, each section on its own slide or page: the Overview cards of every group, RF Quality with the RSRP and SINR CDFs side by side above the table with the class bars, the Coverage and Interference maps of every group side by side with their areas tables under them (one slide per technology and group), Network Deployment with Vodafone beside Three, Cluster Sites Density and Spectrum; a progress dialog stays open while the document is prepared. Its Observed Sites/Cells tables list Cluster after Region, and the Export CSV, Export All to CSV and Previous/Next buttons stand out.
- **Non-Qualified Calls** exports the Executive Summary and Progress Status of the filtered calls to PowerPoint and Word from its page, with a progress dialog while the document is prepared (the PowerPoint and Word buttons of Non-Qualified Calls and Network Insights have their own colours and icons), and its Progress View timeline and donut charts show interactive tooltips (a click on a donut slice filters the calls).
- **Reporting Job Editor:** the Reporting Jobs and Run History tables list artifacts by module (the module in bold and, when it has several entries, its entries indented below it, with PPT, Word and Excel each in its own colour and, in Run History, each format linking to its file); Run History also shows how long each finished run took next to its status; artifacts are organised in tabs on top of the artifact's panel (the selected tab joins it), one per artifact type in the colour and with the icon of its module and a check after its name when it is included, and each artifact and entry is a white card with that colour; the Scoring artifact is generated in PowerPoint and/or Word, and Non-Qualified Calls always generates PowerPoint or Word, with Excel (the calls detail) as an optional extra explained by its tooltip.
- **Scoring & GAP Analysis** exports the selected job to Word as well as PowerPoint, from new buttons beside NR Mode, with a progress dialog while the document is generated; each Word page shows a slide exactly as in PowerPoint when LibreOffice is available (it is included in the Docker image), otherwise the slide tables and charts are rebuilt; PowerPoint and Word export buttons of CDR Analysis, Network Insights, Non-Qualified Calls and Scoring share their colours and icons.
- **Non-Qualified Calls PowerPoint and Word** show the Executive Summary (on one slide) and Progress View with the look of the page: indicator cards, breakdown bars, donuts with legends, the timeline with the open backlog, age and workload cards and styled detail tables, with the report selection on the cover.
- **Word exports** of CDR Analysis (current analysis and summary) and Scoring & GAP Analysis show every slide of their PowerPoint report, one landscape page per slide, when LibreOffice is available (the Docker image includes it); **Network Insights** tables in PowerPoint and Word use the style of the page (light teal header, lavender alternate rows, thin lines, right-aligned numbers, the colour swatch of each group in RF Quality and the cells bar of each Network Deployment group).
- **Generated documents** are grouped by module below `output/reports/` of each workspace: `dashboards` (E2E Dashboards), `cdr-analysis` (CDR Analysis), `network-insights` (Network Insights), `reports-old` and `reports-charts-old` (Reporting (old) reports and Chart Sets) and `reporting-jobs` (Reporting Job runs). Documents of existing workspaces, backups and transfer packages are moved there the first time the workspace is opened, and their jobs keep finding them.
- **Dataset actions** of Workspace and Admin show an eye on Preview Dataset, centred in its button, and the CDR Analysis tab icon on Show Analysis.
- **Workspace** no longer offers **Recalculate Scoring** among the actions of each CDR: a Scoring needs one CDR of each type, so Scoring jobs are launched from Scoring & GAP Analysis (CDRs still queue the default Operator scoring automatically when compatible companions are available).
- **Scoring charts** leave out of the bottom axis the hierarchy levels that have the same value under every bar (such as a single campaign), and long axis labels wrap at `_`, `-` and `/` or are shortened instead of overlapping their neighbours; bar tooltips keep the complete path.
- **Campaign Maps:** a new **Workspace Config → Mappings & Reference Data** subpanel (the former Operator & Vendor Maps panel, with Campaign Maps below Vendor Maps) sets how every chart, table, legend, filter and PowerPoint or Word report names and orders campaigns: a label format with markers (`{year}`, `{yy}`, `{quarter}`, `{mode}`, `{market}`), the order of the modes inside a quarter and exceptions for campaigns outside the pattern, with a live preview. New campaigns follow the format without editing the map; the default shows **2026-Q2**, **2026-Q2-NSA** and **2026-Q2-SA** in chronological order with the plain, NSA and SA campaigns of a quarter in that order. Filters, saved filters and exports keep the original campaign values, and the map travels with the Mappings & Reference Data in Import/Export, transfers and backups.
- **Mapped values everywhere:** Operators, Vendors and Operator_Vendor values are listed, filtered and shown with their Operator and Vendor Maps labels in every module (CDR Analysis, E2E Dashboards, Network Insights, Scoring & GAP Analysis and its report editor, Non-Qualified Calls and Reporting Jobs), in the order of the maps (Operator_Vendor by Operator and then Vendor, Vendor_Operator by Vendor and then Operator, and Operators without a Vendor last in both); choosing a mapped label selects every source spelling, and selections saved with a source spelling keep working. Only Preview Dataset shows the source values.
- **Vendor mapping for every spelling of an Operator:** the VFUK and 3UK mapping files also assign the Vendor of tests named in another mode (for example Vodafone SA, Vodafone VoNR or VF_SA), which share the same cells; their Operator_Vendor keeps their own name (for example `Vodafone VoNR_Ericsson`). CDRs mapped before need to be mapped again.
- **Scoring results toolbar:** the Calculation and Results toolbars offer **Scoring Methodologies** and, respectively, **Generate Scoring Report (PPT/Word)** (the report editor) and **Export Selected Scoring Report (PPT/Word)** (the selected job as calculated; it only asks for PowerPoint, in orange, or Word, in blue), which replace the former PowerPoint and Word buttons beside NR Mode, **Aggregation hierarchy**, in the Aggregation levels panel, opens a dialog to reorder the hierarchy, each **Export to CSV** sits above its Scoring or GAP table and exports the table shown, and the job selector takes the free width of the header; the **Scoring Charts**, **Scoring Tables** and **GAP Analysis** tabs, each in its own colour and with an icon, sit on a panel that holds the results.
- **Scoring per Category (Breakdown)** slides keep every category on one slide up to 50 bars and only split above that, into balanced pages, in PowerPoint and Word.
- **Scoring & GAP Analysis charts, maps and tables:** double-clicking a chart, a map or a table of Scoring Tables and GAP Analysis opens it larger, taking the whole screen but 5% on each side, and every Scoring chart and map zooms into a dragged rectangle or with floating zoom buttons, also in the larger view.

#### 🐛 Bug fixes:
- Administration panels that show saved settings (Users, User Groups, Workspace Access, Features Activation and Interface Settings) always show what the server saved. The browser restored its own last copy of their fields after every reload, and every checkbox of one Allowed or Forbidden picker shared that copy, so reloading could tick or untick a whole picker and the next save stored it (for example removing the super-admin role from Reporting (old)).
- Vendor labels containing an underscore (such as `Ericsson_Mixed`) are no longer matched by their last part (`Mixed`), which gave them the order and colour of another Vendor Map group in CDR Analysis; an Operator_Vendor value is split after its Operator (`3_Ericsson_Mixed` is Operator `3`, Vendor `Ericsson_Mixed`).
- Saving, moving, deleting or adding an Operator or Vendor Map group in Workspace Config updates its table in place again, without reloading the page or scrolling to the top, and keeps the unsaved edits of the other groups and of the new-group form (a rejected save also keeps what was typed in its own row). Since the tables moved to Workspace Config every save reloaded the page and discarded those edits.
- Backups are written under a hidden temporary name and get their backup name only once complete, so a backup interrupted mid-write (application stopped or restarted, computer shut down) is no longer listed, counted in the retention or offered for restore; finished backups also delete those leftovers after six hours. The Restore list marks ZIP files that were cut short before this version as incomplete and does not let them be selected.
- **Application Config** can be imported, transferred or restored from a backup of another server without breaking access or backups there. Packages list the name of each workspace id, so the user grants and the role and group access rules of each workspace follow the workspace with the same name on the destination; grants of workspaces the destination does not have are dropped, and those of workspaces only the destination has are kept. The backup schedule, its folder, workspaces and retention, and the runtime settings (background task limit, event time filtering, timezone) of the destination are kept as well, so a smaller retention can no longer delete its older backups.
- Selecting a Full Workspace in Export / Transfer no longer clears only some Workspace Content options: the Full Workspace of the active workspace replaces every kind of workspace content (all of it is inside that package), and the Full Workspace of another workspace keeps the content of the active one, which it does not contain.
- The incoming server transfer dialog is wider and lists each transferred content once, in two columns, with the proper names of Query Builder Queries, Reporting Jobs and NQ Call Tracking.
- Reporting Jobs brought by a backup restore, an import or a server transfer keep the owner they had on the source server when that user exists and is active here, and otherwise belong to the user who restores, imports or accepts them. They were previously owned by a placeholder `import` user with the default role, so their artifacts of modules limited to some roles (for example Network Insights and Non-Qualified Calls) failed with "import no longer has access". Jobs already imported that way get their owner on the next import of the same jobs.
- Backups delete, once they finish, the database snapshot folders (`drivetest-analyzer-export-*` and the former `dashboard-analytic-export-*`) that backups killed mid-copy (container stopped, computer shut down) left in the backup folder; folders changed in the last six hours are kept in case another backup is still using them.
- Column filter menus of Network Insights inventories and other Excel-style tables stay attached to their column while the page or the table scrolls.
- Non-Qualified Calls filter dropdowns are no longer clipped, and the Progress View legends show complete names.
- Tables in the Help show their first column without splitting words.
- Partial CDR catalogues (rows holding only some filter universes after the vendor migration) are detected and rebuilt from the CDR rows; Reporting Jobs rebuild them in the background and opens its editor at once.
- CDR Analysis CDF comparisons draw one curve for every value of the chosen dimension when its filter lists every value, instead of only the values whose mapped name equals the source name.
- Exports that draw charts (Network Insights, E2E Dashboards and Reporting) retry starting the chart renderer once and, if it still cannot start, show the reason instead of a generic message.
- Network Deployment counts the cells of inventories identified by `GCID`, `Cid__ECI`, `ECI`, `Cell_ID` or `Global CI`, as the inventory exports already did, and `shapely` (used to place sites in Region and Cluster polygons) is a declared dependency.
- CDR Analysis PowerPoint exports no longer fail when a Group Benchmark has negative means (RSRP, SINR…): bars are drawn above or below a zero line. Failed PowerPoint and Word exports show their reason in the application's error dialog instead of a bare status code.
- Help pages show `&` in link texts (for example Scoring & GAP Analysis) instead of `&amp;`. CDR Analysis and Non-Qualified Calls covers keep the report title, the analysed CDR and the filters readable, with long filter lists summarised; CDR Analysis content slides use the white layout of the template, CDR Summary names the analysed CDR as subtitle, and its bars and CDF curves keep the Operator and Vendor colours of the page.
- Opening CDR Analysis with a long saved selection (many metrics and filters) behind a reverse proxy such as Synology's showed the proxy's error page instead of the analysis: the selection was restored through a redirect whose address exceeded the proxy's header limit. The page now shows the saved selection directly.
- Importing, transferring or restoring Auto-calculated Fields identical to those of the workspace no longer materializes the whole workspace again, which kept it busy for minutes after a transfer.
- While the Auto-calculated Fields of the active workspace are being materialized, every page says so, since analyses and exports may be slower or fail until it finishes.
- Failed requests answered by a reverse proxy or by a server error (for example a timeout) show their cause, such as `The server did not answer in time (HTTP 504)`, instead of a generic message like `Unable to analyse the selected CDRs.`
- An incoming transfer left receiving or importing when the server stopped is marked as interrupted when it starts again, instead of being shown as a running task that could not be stopped; nothing is imported again without approval.
- Dashboard charts are prepared in the background again once nobody uses the application for five minutes: the periodic checks of incoming transfers and backups that pages make on their own counted as use, so with any page open the charts were only prepared when a Dashboard was opened.
- **Scoring & GAP Analysis:** jobs aggregated by Cluster showed every cluster as *Not specified* and mixed their scores in the result tables, charts and exports; each cluster now has its own columns, and the Scoring and GAP CSV add a `cluster` column.
- **E2E Dashboards:** Hide, Hide All and Keep only on an axis value (for example the Operator O2) now hide it everywhere even after a legend entry that contains it, such as `O2 · 2026-Q1` of a CDF chart, was shown or hidden on its own; before, those series and columns stayed visible.
- **Reporting Jobs with CDR Analysis in PowerPoint and Word:** building the Word document no longer removes the PowerPoint of the same run, and Run History links only the files of each run that still exist.
- **Scoring PowerPoint and Word titles** keep the template's size while they fit the 27.5 cm title placeholder (such as *Best Network Scoring per Category (Breakdown)*); only longer ones are made smaller to stay on one line, so they no longer cover the subtitle or the *Page x of y* note; the part that tells consecutive slides apart (after the dash, such as *GAP Analysis — **3 vs EE***, or the *per …* focus) is in bold, in the colour of the rest of the title.
- After Region or Cluster polygons were used (Network Insights, CDR mapping), the server could no longer start processes (dataset workers, Word and PDF conversions), which ended with a crash on macOS; the polygon libraries are now loaded at startup.

#### 📚 Documentation:
- Added the [Non-Qualified Calls](help/non-qualified-calls.md) guide and updated the overview, web interface, administrator, workspace, CDR Analysis, Network Insights, E2E Dashboards, Scoring, Reporting, Query Builder, project structure and roadmap documents.

---

## Release: v0.5.0
### Release Date: 2026-10-05
#### ⚠️ Breaking Changes:
- Scoring configuration exchange uses version 3 with explicit environment `source_filters`; Tableau fields, duplicate environment mappings and reference metadata are removed. Export methodologies again to create files in the new format.
- Report Templates use `Source Dataset` instead of `CDR source`, add `Dynamic Rows Field` and `Dynamic Columns Field` after `Layout`, and store canonical layout names such as `Title + 1 rows + 2 columns + comments down`. Stored templates are converted automatically at startup; previous formats remain importable, but exported templates cannot be imported by earlier versions.
- Vendor filters (`Vendor`, `Vendor V3`, `Operator_Vendor`, `OP_Vendor`) in templates, Dashboards, previews and Query Builder now apply to `Vendor_Only`. Operator-prefixed values are reduced to their vendor, so `Vodafone_Huawei` matches Huawei for every operator; review filters that include or exclude a vendor of one specific operator.
- `Vendor_Only` stores operators without a mapped vendor as `Operator - All` (for example `EE - All`) in processed CDRs, combined tables and exports.
- Auto-calculated Fields without explicit sources apply to the virtual `cdr-all` source too; exports that list it cannot be imported by earlier versions.
- E2E Reporting is now **Reporting (old)** at `/reporting-old`, and its API moved from `/api/e2e-reporting` to `/api/reporting-old`; page bookmarks to `/e2e-reporting` redirect. `/reporting` now opens the new Reporting module. Access to every main module is controlled in Admin → Features Activation.
- Vendor mapping applies the same rule to Vodafone UK and Three UK: the same Vendor at both endpoints returns `<Operator>_<Vendor>`, Ericsson at either endpoint with a different or missing Vendor returns `<Operator>_Ericsson_Mixed`, and every other different or missing combination returns `<Operator>_Non-Ericsson_Mixed` (`Vendor_Only` `Ericsson_Mixed` and `Non-Ericsson_Mixed`). They replace `Vodafone_Mixed Vendor`, `Vodafone_Other Vendor` and `3_Mixed Vendor`; map CDRs again to apply it, and review filters or templates that name the former values.

#### 🌟 New Features:
- **Scoring & GAP Analysis** calculates configurable NetCheck City/Road scores from Data, Voice and Speech CDRs. Persistent jobs reuse unchanged inputs, retain methodology snapshots and support deletion and CSV/PowerPoint exports.
- **Scoring Methodologies** provide workspace-stored KPI definitions, thresholds, interpolation anchors and Linear, Quadratic or Smooth curve mapping. Methodologies have a configurable default and do not depend on local reference files.
- Configurable **Environments** define CDR source matching, points, weights and KPI allocations. Environments can be created, renamed or deleted; reference-based point distribution preserves relative KPI weights, including zero-point allocations.
- Scoring calculations support Operator, Vendor, Region, City and Campaign filters, Main Cities and per-job methodology selection. A configurable aggregation hierarchy controls grouping; campaigns are separated when selected and pooled otherwise.
- **Scoring Tables**, **GAP Analysis** and **Scoring Charts** provide Summary/Breakdown tables, operator-versus-reference comparisons and Best Network charts by service/category, with maximum-allocation donuts.
- **Scoring configuration transfers** support JSON/ZIP, Admin imports, workspace transfers and backup/restore. Export Methodology (JSON) downloads the selected methodology; Admin exports/backups include all methodologies and the default. Import Methodology (JSON) adds or replaces matching methodology IDs while preserving other methodologies and the workspace default.
- **Scoring PowerPoint export** includes covers, environment transitions, charts, Scoring tables and combined/individual GAP comparisons, with editable matrices and charts.
- **Network Insights** analyses the radio fields of the selected Data, Voice and Speech CDRs: RSRP/SINR CDFs (Operator/Vendor colours, with the latest campaign drawn thickest and older campaigns thinner) and quality classes, low-coverage and high-interference shares against separate LTE and NR thresholds (defaults -110 dBm/0 dB for LTE and -115 dBm/-3 dB for NR) with changes between the two latest campaigns, coverage and interference grid maps with ranked weak areas, observed eNodeBs and bands, licensed spectrum by Low/Mid/High (TDD) class, and NNS/eMOCN/host-network/RAN-vendor site counts from the Vodafone and Three inventories. Every deployment table exports to CSV with a `_VF`, `_3` or `_VF_3` suffix for Vodafone, Three or combined tables; Observed Sites/Cells (from CDRs) lists the sites and cells served in the selected CDRs and Full Sites/Cells Inventory shows separate Vodafone and Three tables of every mapped cell, both with Excel-style column filters; the inventory applies the general filters, highlights Operator, Vendor, Vendor_Only, Region, City, Technology and Cluster first, resolves geographical fields from both operators’ source mapping headers, preserves original fields and mapping-file provenance, and exports every matching row per operator or together. Every deployment view offers Export All to CSV alongside its individual table exports. Analysis Selection keeps every control on one row, each CDR type card offers Select All/None, a dialog shows the analysis in progress with its elapsed time, and CDFs and maps have in-chart zoom and pan controls like E2E Dashboards. Network Deployment precedes Cluster Sites Density, which explains the required cluster polygons; Workspace accepts and validates Clusters datasets as GeoJSON/JSON or zipped shapefiles and preserves them through workspace portability and backups with input files. Results group by any combination of Operator, Vendor, Region, City, CDR type and Campaign (Operator and Campaign by default; Operator is checked and disabled unless Vendor is selected), and LTE+NR shows separate LTE and NR overviews, CDFs and maps without pooling them. Each CDR's samples are read in parallel the first time and cached per CDR, so changing the CDR selection or the filters takes seconds, and repeated analyses and deployment tables are reused from a workspace cache, also after a restart, until their CDRs, inventories or mappings change. Filters load from the CDR catalogues, including Main Cities, and selections are remembered per user and workspace. Licensed spectrum is configured in Workspace Config → Spectrum Holdings and travels with Operator & Vendor Maps exports, transfers and backups (package version 3; older packages remain importable).
- **RF Quality template and Dashboard** (workspace content, not installed automatically): CDFs with contour and per-Campaign histograms, averages, quality classes, thresholds and maps for all CDR types pooled and for Data, Voice and Speech, keeping LTE and NR separate.
- **Interactive chart legends:** clicking a legend entry in the Dashboard viewer, the expanded chart viewer or Network Insights hides or shows that series, bar or stacked segment for every chart type and closes the gap; hidden entries stay faded and struck through. In Dashboards, right-clicking a legend entry or an axis label at any aggregation level offers Keep only, Hide/Show, Hide All and Show All, and axis menus list their hidden values with a Show submenu. Hiding applies to the whole Dashboard and to every series, row, column or category where the value appears at any level, such as every Campaign of a hidden Operator. Dynamic grid cards of a hidden value leave the slide, and the remaining cards fill a smaller grid. PPT exports, their viewer and relaunched jobs reproduce the same state.
- **Reporting:** scheduled Reporting Jobs collect a Dataset Analysis and Network Insights reports (PowerPoint and/or Word), Dashboard PPTs and Scoring PPTs, each configured with every option of its own module, and add Network Insights, the same Dashboard or Scoring several times for several configurations (for example NSA and SA, LTE and NR, or different filters). Each artifact type (Datasets Analysis, Network Insights, E2E Dashboards, Scoring & GAP Analysis, Non-Qualified Calls) is checked independently, artifact cards and entries collapse and remember it after reloading, entry fields share one row and CDRs appear in per-type cards with Select All/None. Dashboard entries choose the NR Mode first and then offer its Dashboards and CDRs, or every ready CDR of that NR Mode at each run; jobs saved with one Network Insights selection keep it as one entry. Jobs run manually, once, daily, weekly or monthly in the application timezone whenever the application is running, for every workspace even when it is closed or another one is open, appear in the background tasks window, keep a run history with downloadable artifacts, and optionally email them with a body describing every artifact and its filters. The jobs table shows artifacts, next run, recurrence, email and last run, with run, download, edit, duplicate, enable/disable and delete actions. Users only include artifacts of modules activated for them, and other modules can register their own artifacts. Reporting Jobs travel with exports, transfers and backups.
- **Features Activation and User Groups:** Admin groups users into custom User Groups, and admins and super-admins configure each main module, in tab order, as available to all users or to nobody by default, with Allowed and Forbidden roles, groups and users in which Forbidden always wins (a lower role can grant a feature to a higher role but never remove it, so admins cannot take features away from super-admins); modules that are not active for an account leave its navigation and answer 403. Reporting starts allowed for super-admins, Reporting (old) for super-admins and EJAITUR, and the new Non-Qualified Calls module, under construction, is hidden from everyone.
- **Email Delivery:** Application Config configures the SMTP server, encryption, account, sender and attachment size limit for Reporting emails, with a test email; `SMTP_*` deployment variables provide defaults.
- **Summary exports:** Datasets Analysis exports a Summary PowerPoint or Word with every KPI of the selected datasets (all by default), and Network Insights exports its current analysis — RF overview, CDFs, maps, weakest areas, spectrum, both observed and inventory Cluster Sites Density views and every grouped deployment view (including Cluster when polygons or inventory fields are available, with Region and Cluster names taken from imported polygons and inventory fields as the fallback when polygons are absent), excluding the Sites/Cells tables, with LTE and NR in separate sections for LTE+NR — to PowerPoint or Word. The inventory pending-input notice lists only operators in the filtered analysis without ready inventories. Full inventory tables and CSVs include all mapping rows filtered by their attributes, independently of observed CDR cells; CSV downloads are prepared in full before delivery to avoid truncated exports. Its PowerPoint uses the E2E Dashboard template, and both formats fit table widths to their content with single-line cells and readable wide-table layouts.

#### 🚀 Enhancements:
- **Methodology editor:** methodologies and titles sit directly in Scoring Setup, with matching dark green Environment, Hierarchy and GAP panels and nested pastel red KPI settings with a clear editing prompt when collapsed. Shortcuts open Methodology Environments, Aggregation hierarchy and KPI Priorities directly. All Save Methodology buttons commit the entire open methodology. Set Default selects future jobs’ methodology and protects it from deletion.
- **Scoring setup:** NR Mode precedes CDR selection. Green SVG shortcuts select all or the latest one/two per type, using name dates before upload time and ID. Pastel green subpanels place GAP Reference below aggregation; Calculation buttons and configuration shortcuts share the methodology selector row at matching heights; bold yellow status notices span the panel underneath and expand for long messages. Cached CDR filters, centered point distribution and category-aware KPI priority moves simplify editing.
- **Scoring job navigation:** a dropdown and history cards show filters (All Vendors for full vendor selections), CDR sources, methodology, GAP Reference and ordered aggregation levels; methodology and reference values use bold, contrasting colors. Selecting a job restores its calculation inputs for relaunching. Saved selections persist across users/browsers; result tabs, scroll position and display choices restore within the browser session.
- **Scoring responsiveness:** cached catalogues, indexed KPI lookups and on-demand result tabs avoid unnecessary source scans and repeated rendering. Export controls remain visible while results load.
- **Environment results:** All Environments sums configured maxima and weighted contributions. Global raw KPIs are recalculated from pooled source rows; older jobs require recalculation to populate them. Incomplete coverage keeps original weights, and jobs initially select a complete environment when available.
- **Coverage explanations:** one pale yellow Calculation notes card lists each affected combination, excluded KPI names and exact achievable maximum in larger bold text. Incomplete values and N/A* cells use bold red text; PowerPoint slide notes include the same coverage details.
- **Vendor filtering:** selectors show vendor names only and match their rows across operators, while retaining operators without vendors unless excluded by the Operator filter. Vendor aggregation displays All for those operators, consistently in web tables/charts and PowerPoint.
- **Scoring table readability:** Summary removes the duplicate Category column; Breakdown groups category cells through subtotals and offers optional raw KPI values. Web tables use softer metadata colors, distinct KPI types and consistent total-row fills; best/worst scores are ranked across each entire Summary row, preserving ties, and have a separate legend from KPI score bands.
- **GAP presentation:** optional GAP columns appear at the end or beside scores. Individual comparisons sort by signed GAP, with unavailable values last; combined comparisons retain KPI order. Partial GAPs use common valid environment contributions and carry an asterisk. Individual and All Operators GAP tables include column totals in web and PowerPoint.
- **Web chart readability:** enlarged previews, context tooltips, adaptive bars, contrasting labels and horizontal scrolling support dense series. Mapped operator colors/order and year-quarter campaign labels remain consistent; chart numbers use decimal points regardless of browser language.
- **Allocation donuts:** environment/service/category rings show configured maximum points, percentages and compact icon legends. Service charts stack Data below Voice in web and PowerPoint. Grayscale service/category keys distinguish contributions without duplicating operator legends.
- **PowerPoint layout:** environment blocks use consistent display names, mapped operator colors, readable covers/transitions with white titles/field labels, bold yellow filter/aggregation/campaign values and compact lists. Filenames include timestamp and filter selections while respecting filesystem limits; exporting All Environments includes the combined block followed by each environment.
- **PowerPoint chart pagination:** exports ask whether to split above 20 Best Network bars or 40 Scoring bars. Splitting keeps categories complete and prioritizes 3, EE and VF_UK together when possible; declining keeps all bars on one slide. Scoring labels become smaller integers above 40 bars and are hidden above 70.
- **PowerPoint chart axes:** single aggregation levels use normal labels; multiple levels use editable grids aligned to bar centers, with Category as level 0 where applicable. Each level uses uniform text sizing, and grayscale keys keep a fixed width within the chart area.
- **PowerPoint table layout:** Category values are bold and left-aligned; legends align above Scoring tables with their right edges flush with the table. GAP color scales have reserved space below tables, and individual comparisons have a Priority arrow spanning the table and signed KPI averages and column-averaged GAP totals in side notes.
- **Workspace configuration:** Main Cities precedes the renamed Operator & Vendor Maps panels. Mapping panels collapse independently; export/backup labels match, and the Backup content menu wraps descriptions on smaller screens.
- **Radio-quality maps:** Map KPIs accept an optional third measure after latitude/longitude; Value Bucket grouping and four configured thresholds colour measured quality from red to green in the Dashboard and PPT.
- **Multivendor comparisons:** a Vendor comparison selector beside Report type and Scope chooses Vendor Only (All Operators Combined, the default), which pools operators sharing a vendor, or Operator - Vendor. Vendor filters across the application use `Vendor_Only` with shared vendor ordering; dynamic vendor slides paginate at six values keeping each vendor's operators together, and saved universes and job retries keep the choice.
- **Report Template layouts:** fixed grids of rows and columns with comments below, beside or none, dynamic grids whose rows or columns follow the values of a field, and title, transition and logo closing layouts. The `CDR-All` source combines the selected Data, Voice and Speech CDRs, and CSV imports accept columns in any order. Charts in grids with three or more rows or four or more columns show their title as readable text above the chart in the Dashboard and PPT, with the dynamic value (for example the Operator) in bold.
- **Dashboard and CDR performance:** preparing a Dashboard no longer rescans every row of the selected CDRs to validate cached columns. Charts read a per-CDR column cache: each column is read from SQLite once per CDR together with the Dashboard filter columns. Changing CDRs, dates, Operator, Vendor, Region, City, Campaign or other filters, or switching between Operator Comparison and either Vendor Comparison mode, then assembles the rows in memory without reading the CDRs again, with the values, types and row order SQLite returns; preparing that universe counts its rows from the cached filter columns and reuses each CDR's distinct filter values. Charts sharing fields reuse one read and one filtering pass, and dynamic grid cells reuse a common filtered frame. Background Dashboard work runs on one worker, one Dashboard at a time in creation order. While the application is idle, it also builds each Dashboard's default-universe charts in all three comparison modes and caches their columns for the other CDRs, pausing as soon as the application is used again. Operator and Vendor labels are normalised once per distinct value, and CDR processing derives Market, Period and Vendor_Only once per distinct value.
- **Dashboard status:** the library shows Pre-Caching Universe while universes are prepared, Data Cached · Charts n/m while the default universe still has charts to render, Rendering Charts n/m while the background worker renders them, and Ready only when every chart of the default universe is rendered.
- **Dashboard viewer responsiveness:** the charts of the open slide are calculated before any prefetch: nearby slides prefetch one chart at a time and wait while visible charts are calculated, and a visible chart already being prefetched is requested again with priority. Background pre-caching stops at its next step when a user opens or prepares a Dashboard, instead of holding the preparation slot until it finishes.
- **Chart axis labels:** aggregation headers and category labels at every axis level wrap onto two lines, shrink or turn vertical with the room they need, instead of being truncated or rotated diagonally, in the Dashboard viewer and in the PPT.
- **Main tabs:** the main tabs follow the order Workspace, Datasets Analysis, Network Insights, E2E Dashboards, Scoring & GAP Analysis, Non-Qualified Calls, Reporting, Reporting (old) and Builders, and each shows a representative icon. Tabs never overlap: below 1800 px wide the main tabs use their short names, below 1400 px they drop the icons, and tabs that do not fit wrap onto further lines without mixing main and administrative tabs on the same line.
- **Public documentation:** Help, the Readme and the Changelog open without signing in, with a Sign in link. Every Help chapter is available to every reader except Reporting (old), which requires that feature.
- **Workspace mappings:** Map applies Vendor, Region and Cluster mappings to CDRs that are already mapped, replacing the previous result without a prior Clear; a mapping left as "No … mapping" keeps its previous result, and re-mapping only Vendor recalculates it on the stored rows instead of rebuilding the CDR. Clusters — Geospatial polygons fill the CDR `Cluster` column like Region polygons fill `Region`, from the Map dialog and from the mapping selectors when importing CDRs.
- **Read-only viewers:** the `user-viewer` role can explore the workspace and generate jobs (Dashboard PPTs with any filters, Scoring, Network Insights, queries and reports) but can no longer upload, delete, reprocess, stop, map or clear datasets, change NR Modes, recreate combined tables or rematerialize fields, or save, rename or delete Dashboards, saved queries and Scoring jobs; the controls are hidden and the server rejects these requests.

#### 🐛 Bug fixes:
- Interactivity packet-error ratio honors IFNULL loss components while requiring a positive observed Packets_Sent denominator.
- Road scoring uses the corrected Drive Connecting Roads environment name and Connecting Roads source selector.
- GAP comparisons fall back to the reference operator's All vendor group when an exact vendor match is unavailable, preserving other aggregation boundaries and removing obsolete missing-baseline warnings.
- Saving methodologies verifies persisted environment names/source selectors and preserves pending environment edits when saving hierarchy or priorities. Switching after save no longer creates a false unsaved-change warning; historical result labels reflect environment renames without changing calculations.
- Scoring PowerPoint exports avoid malformed table/chart XML and allocation labels that caused repair prompts or blank slides.
- Matching scoring selections enable Recalculate instead of Calculate, including jobs saved before engine updates; recalculation updates the existing job, its displayed date/time and results without adding a duplicate. Job lists show the latest calculations first.
- Threshold Stacked Vertical Bars legends show the configured threshold instead of a fixed `1.6`, and explicit `Buckets` ranges with negative limits use readable, ordered labels such as `< -110`, `-110 to -100` and `-80+`.
- Help for users without E2E Reporting access no longer lists E2E Reporting in the Product Overview index or the Datasets Analysis guide.
- Average and Median bar charts no longer fail when a group has no valid sample; such groups, like any combination without samples, show no bar instead of a 0.00 bar.
- Retrying a dataset through the legacy `/dashboard/retry/{id}` route works again.

#### 📚 Documentation:
- Scoring & GAP Analysis help consolidates workflows, methodology configuration and source references into one structured guide, with practical GAP examples, comparison tables and colored information/tip/warning cards. Internal help links navigate in the same tab.
- E2E Dashboards help uses task-based navigation, concise workflows, action tables and colored notices covering selections, Apply/Save, live views, exports, cache and portability.
- Remaining Help chapters, README and contributor guidance use task indexes, shorter explanations, reference tables and visual notices; workspace/scoring configuration, source-value semantics and Dashboard cache guidance reflect current behaviour.
- New Network Insights help chapter covers the analysis selection, panels, data sources and pending inputs; Workspace Config help documents Spectrum Holdings.

---

## Release: v0.4.2
### Release Date: 2026-09-28
#### ⚠️ Breaking Changes:
- Python 3.12 or newer is now required. `pyproject.toml`, the README, the Docker image (`python:3.12-slim`), the macOS launcher and the unit-test workflow no longer support Python 3.11.

#### 🌟 New Features:

#### 🚀 Enhancements:
- The Charts Panel filters PowerPoint jobs by their complete Region and City selections as well as NR Mode, Dashboard, Template and Scope; each filter offers values from jobs matching the other active filters. Its job selector fills the space up to View Filters, shows the export's NR Mode and geography, and lets long city lists use the remaining width.
- On phones and other small screens, **Open Filters** in Manage Dashboards shows Dashboard Datasets & Filters in the same floating panel as the viewer's **Dashboard Filters** instead of an extra table row.
- The E2E Dashboards jobs panel is renamed **PPT Generation Jobs**. There and in E2E Reporting's Reports and Charts Jobs, the PPT download is a labelled **Download PPT** button with the PPT icon in the orange PowerPoint accent instead of a small circular icon, since it is the table's primary action. Both job tables use narrower data columns so the wider Actions column fits the panel without horizontal scrolling, and PPT Generation Jobs centres its Filters button and Status badge places View Dashboard right after Download PPT and shows its Filters tooltip in the same dark palette as the Reports and Charts Jobs datasets tooltip. The Generate PPT action in Manage Dashboards uses the same orange accent and is now a labelled **Generate PPT** button right after Open Filters, and its Dashboard and Template columns share the same width.
- CDR workbooks import every operator sheet after `KPI Definition`, including technology-specific sheets such as `Vodafone VoNR`, which were previously skipped when a sheet with the plain operator name (`Vodafone`) existed. Reprocess affected CDRs to include those rows.
- NR Mode is colour-coded by value (pastel yellow for NSA, pastel blue for SA) in the Workspace Datasets and Manage Dashboards selectors and as a badge in PPT Generation Jobs and E2E Reporting's Reports and Charts Jobs.
- The Dashboard viewer header and Title Slide cover, and the exported PPT cover, show the Dashboard's Campaigns (for example `Campaigns: 2026-Q1, 2026-Q2_SA`, limited by any Campaign filter) in orange before the Scope; on both covers the Campaigns line sits right above the decorative line, in a larger font than the Scope, Region and City lines below it. Campaigns are cached with each CDR's catalogue (CDRs processed earlier are read once in the background), and PPT file names comparing several Campaigns end with them joined by `_vs_`, for example ` - 2026-Q1_vs_2026-Q2`. PPT Generation Jobs list the Campaigns right above the Operators in their Filters. The viewer always shows the slide title and subtitle in capitals.
- Refresh in the individual chart viewer spins and stays disabled while the chart is rendered again, like Relaunch, and now only re-reads the Dashboard's CDR rows when Operator or Vendor mappings changed since they were loaded, so refreshing a chart takes seconds instead of minutes.
- Chart slide subtitles in exported PPTs are larger (20 pt), and chart, cover and transition subtitles use normal letter spacing instead of inheriting the template's condensed title style.
- Table charts share the available height among all their rows (tables are no longer cut after 36 rows, or 18 for simple tables, nor clipped at the bottom; very large tables are capped at 96 and 60 rows) and size their text to both the row height and the column width, so they read well in Dashboard cards, exported PPTs and the expanded chart view: column headings always fit whole, and only exceptionally long values are shortened with an ellipsis.
- The Generate PPT dialog from Manage Dashboards (now **Select Dashboard Universe and Filters**, with the Dashboard's NR Mode and name in its heading) groups Scope, CDR datasets and Dates in a lavender **Dashboard Universe** sub-panel above a blue **Main Dashboard Filters** sub-panel, whose Operators, Vendors, Regions and Cities selectors start with the values saved in the Dashboard. **Show All Filters** adds the Dashboard's other main filters and its Additional Filters (pink sub-panel) to the export, and **Save Universe** / **Save Filters** store the dialog's choices in the Dashboard when they differ from the saved ones; their menus open right below the selector and may overflow the dialog instead of being clipped by it.
- The Changelog release navigators (side panel and floating drawer) show each release with its date, for example `v0.4.2 ● 2026-09-28`.

#### 🐛 Bug fixes:
- On small screens, the **Builders** and **Config** module tabs have the same height as the other tabs and their menus only take the height of their options (iOS Safari stretched them to the full screen), the Dashboard viewer's **Scope** selector uses the same font size as its neighbouring buttons, and the Workspace Datasets cards no longer show the Combined CDR tables banner and a blank column-header row as cards of their own: the banner is shown above each combined CDR card.
- The Filters of PPT Generation Jobs show **All Operators** and **All Vendors** when no specific Operator or Vendor was selected, as they already did for Regions and Cities; existing jobs show them too.
- Dashboard filter options, the viewer cover and PPT exports only consider the Operators, Vendors, Regions, Cities and Campaigns of the selected CDRs: selecting all of them reads as **All …** even when other CDRs (for example NSA CDRs in an SA Dashboard) or older saved filters hold more values, and saved values missing from those CDRs are no longer offered or listed.

#### 📚 Documentation:

---

## Release: v0.4.1
### Release Date: 2026-09-26
#### ⚠️ Breaking Changes:
- E2E Dashboards only use CDRs whose NR Mode matches the Dashboard's NR Mode. Existing CDRs receive an NR Mode proposed from their file name (SA only when the name says SA/Standalone, otherwise NSA); review SA CDRs in the Workspace Datasets table, or SA Dashboards may show `Data needed`.

#### 🌟 New Features:
- CDR datasets have an NR Mode (NSA or SA). Uploads propose it from each file name and offer a per-file selector before processing starts, the Workspace Datasets table shows an editable **NR Mode** column after Input Type, and changes are logged. The value lives in `dataset_profiles`, so it is included in workspace exports, transfers, backups and restores.

#### 🚀 Enhancements:
- Distribution charts with explicit range buckets (for example `Buckets = 1,5,20`) now stack and list `<1`, `1-5`, `5-20` and `20+` in ascending numeric order instead of the order in which values first appear in the CDR rows.
